281 — Symbolic Model Verifier

Goal: Build, from scratch in Rust, a symbolic model checker (a NuSMV-class tool): a Reduced Ordered Binary Decision Diagram (ROBDD) package, an SMV-style finite-state modeling language, a boolean encoding of finite-domain variables into symbolic init / transition relations, symbolic reachability and CTL model checking by BDD fixpoint iteration, LTL model checking via an LTL-to-Buchi translation with Emerson-Lei fair-cycle detection, SAT-based Bounded Model Checking (BMC) by k-step unrolling, and counterexample / witness extraction. Differential-test against NuSMV. It is a symbolic verifier — not a SAT core (#277), an SMT solver (#278), an explicit-state model checker (#279), or a theorem prover (#280).

Project Number: 281

Project Name: Symbolic Model Verifier

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

1. Goal

Goal: Build, from scratch in Rust, a symbolic finite-state model checker — an ROBDD package + SMV front end + boolean encoder + symbolic CTL/LTL engines + SAT-based BMC + counterexample extraction — shipped as seven embeddable smv-* crates and the smv CLI.

1.1 Precise engineering definition (what is built)

A symbolic model checker that decides whether a finite-state Kripke structure satisfies a temporal-logic specification, representing state sets (never individual states) as ROBDDs. Seven concerns, each a crate:

  • ROBDD package — BddManager owning a node arena, a unique table (hashcons on (level,low,high)), a computed/op cache; ite as the single primitive; not/and/or/xor/imp; restrict (cofactor); exists/forall over a cube; and_exists (relational product); rename (present↔next); satcount; node_count; mark-sweep GC; dot export.
  • SMV-style front end — Lexer + recursive-descent Parser → SmvModule AST (VAR boolean/enum/range; ASSIGN init/next/plain; DEFINE; INVAR; TRANS; CTLSPEC/LTLSPEC); name + type checking; CircularDefine detection.
  • Boolean encoder — Encoder maps finite-domain vars (boolean, enum/scalar via ⌈log₂⌉ bits + out-of-range constraint, bounded Range) to boolean BDD levels (VarEncoding); builds EncodedFsm { present, next, init, trans, invar, fairness, ap }.
  • Symbolic reachability — forward image, backward preimage (both via and_exists + rename), the reachable least-fixpoint lfp Z. init OR Img(Z).
  • CTL engine — CtlChecker evaluates EX/EU/EG + derived AX/AU/AG/AF/EF to a state-set Bdd; spec holds iff init ⊆ sat(f) (i.e. init AND not sat(f) == bdd_false).
  • LTL engine — LtlChecker: LTL→Büchi translation, symbolic product with the model, Emerson-Lei fair-cycle detection; counterexample = fair lasso.
  • SAT-based BMC — Unroller builds I(s0) AND ⋀ T(si,si+1) AND ¬prop_k as a CnfFormula via a Tseitin transform; a SatBackend trait (DIMACS in / assignment out); SAT model → Trace.
  • Counterexample / witness extraction — Trace { states, loopback } (lasso); BDD cubes mapped back to readable StateAssignment over original SMV vars.

1.2 System boundaries

Inside the boundary Outside the boundary
ROBDD construction, canonicity, GC Generic SAT-solver core internals (delegated via SatBackend)
SMV-style finite-state modeling language Full NuSMV input compatibility (subset; same verdicts on the corpus)
Boolean encoding of finite domains Infinite-state / unbounded-integer / real arithmetic
Symbolic CTL + LTL + safety BMC Probabilistic / timed / hybrid temporal logics
Counterexamples + witnesses (lasso) Proof certificates / interactive proof
smv CLI driving all of the above GUI / IDE / waveform viewer

State representation is symbolic: a Bdd denotes a set of states; fixpoints iterate over sets. The engine never materializes the state graph as nodes/edges.

1.3 Explicit NON-goals

  • Not a standalone SAT solver — that is #277. smv-bmc consumes a CNF/DIMACS SatBackend; it ships only a bundled simple DPLL/CDCL as a default and a hook for #277. It does not aim to be a competitive solver.
  • Not an SMT solver — that is #278. All domains are finite and boolean-encoded; no theory reasoning (arithmetic, arrays, bitvectors-as-theories).
  • Not an explicit-state enumerative model checker — that is #279. THIS engine is symbolic: it stores state sets as BDDs and never enumerates individual states.
  • Not a theorem prover — that is #280. Verification is decision-procedure / fixpoint based, not deductive.
  • Not infinite-state / software model checking, probabilistic / timed / hybrid automata, or a GUI.

1.4 Deployment model — who uses & operates it

  • Form: an embeddable Rust crate set (smv-bdd … smv-bmc, no_std + alloc where marked) + a single std binary smv.
  • smv subcommands: check (CTL/LTL), bmc, reachable, simulate, bdd-stats, encode, dot.
  • Operators / users:
    • Hardware verification engineers — check protocol/controller/arbiter RTL models, get counterexample traces.
    • Protocol designers — encode mutual-exclusion / cache-coherence / handshake FSMs, verify safety + liveness.
    • Researchers — embed smv-bdd / smv-check as libraries; prototype new fixpoint algorithms against a clean ROBDD API.
    • Students — learn ROBDDs, symbolic CTL/LTL, BMC from a readable reference with NuSMV-comparable verdicts.
  • Integration: library callers link smv-bdd/smv-model/smv-check; CI/scripts shell out to smv check model.smv. Owner: Mujir Qureshi.

1.5 Phase-0 deliverable — the seven smv-* crates

The Phase-0 (## 12) skeleton stands up the workspace and seven crate shells, in dependency order:

  1. smv-bdd — ROBDD package; BddManager, Bdd(u32), unique table, computed cache, ite/apply, restrict, exists/forall, and_exists, rename, satcount, node_count, GC, dot. (no_std + alloc)
  2. smv-syntax — Lexer, Token, Parser, the model AST (SmvModule, VarDecl, VarType, AssignDecl/AssignKind, DefineDecl, InvarDecl, TransDecl, SpecDecl), ParseError/ParseErrorKind. (no_std + alloc)
  3. smv-model — Encoder, VarEncoding, EncodedFsm; finite-domain → boolean BDD levels. (deps: smv-bdd + smv-syntax)
  4. smv-logic — CtlFormula, LtlFormula, property parsers, NNF/normalization, AP→Bdd binding. (deps: smv-bdd + smv-model; no_std + alloc)
  5. smv-check — CtlChecker, LtlChecker, reachable, image/preimage, Trace, counterexample/witness extraction. (deps: the above)
  6. smv-bmc — Unroller, CnfFormula, SatBackend, SatResult; Tseitin unrolling; SAT model → Trace. (deps: smv-model + smv-logic)
  7. smv-cli — the smv binary; subcommands check/bmc/reachable/simulate/bdd-stats/encode/dot. (deps: all; std)

Phase-0 also lands error types, CI/Makefile/Dockerfile, and a smoke test (ithvar(0); parse a 1-var model; assert bdd_true != bdd_false).

1.6 What later phases unlock

Phases Capability unlocked
1–2 (## 13–## 14) Correct, canonical ROBDD with all model-checking ops (and_exists, rename, quantification, GC)
3–4 (## 15–## 16) Parse + type-check SMV; boolean-encode finite vars; compile Expr→Bdd
5–6 (## 17–## 18) Symbolic FSM (init/trans/invar/fairness); reachable-set fixpoint, image/preimage
7–8 (## 19–## 20) Symbolic CTL verdicts; shortest counterexamples/witnesses; readable StateAssignment
9–10 (## 21–## 22) LTL→Büchi; symbolic product; Emerson-Lei fair-cycle LTL verdicts + fair lasso
11 (## 23) SAT-based BMC: Tseitin unrolling, SatBackend, model→Trace
12 (## 24) Optimizations: sifting reorder, partitioned trans, early quantification, fairness, BMC completeness threshold
13 (## 25) Validation gate: differential vs NuSMV, BDD property/fuzz, benchmarks vs targets — the "done" gate

1.7 Deferred / stretch features

Gated behind a PhaseDeferred marker (one per item below) — not in the v1 path; each anchor is a real phase heading, closed out in the Deferred Features Register:

  • Zero-suppressed BDDs (ZDDs) and ADD/MTBDD numeric terminals → PhaseDeferred("§24").
  • IC3/PDR and k-induction proof engines → PhaseDeferred("§23").
  • Partitioned/disjunctive transition relations beyond the conjunctive default and image-computation MBM clustering → PhaseDeferred("§24").
  • Full LTL[*] / PSL / SVA assertion syntax → PhaseDeferred("§21").
  • Streett/Rabin (beyond Büchi/Streett fairness) and parity acceptance → PhaseDeferred("§24").
  • Interpolation-based unbounded BMC and abstraction-refinement (CEGAR) → PhaseDeferred("§23").
  • Parallel/multi-threaded BDD ops and concurrent GC → PhaseDeferred("§14").

1.8 Estimated timeline

Milestone Estimate
Phase-0 skeleton (7 crates compile, smoke test green) 6–14 weeks
ROBDD core + ops (Phases 1–2) +4–8 weeks
Full symbolic CTL/LTL/BMC + validation (Phases 3–13) several months total

Single-maintainer estimates; canonicity bring-up (Phase 1–2) and LTL→Büchi (Phase 9) dominate risk.

1.9 Recommended language — Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78)

Rationale (a BDD package is performance-critical, pointer/hash-table-heavy):

  • Index-based node arena — u32 handles (Bdd(u32)), no Rc cycles → maps cleanly onto Rust ownership; eliminates the use-after-free / double-free bugs endemic to C BDD libraries during GC.
  • Control over allocation + cache layout — decisive for the hot unique table + computed cache; no_std + alloc keeps the core lean and embeddable.
  • Enums + exhaustive match — model the SMV AST (Expr, VarType, SpecDecl) and CtlFormula/LtlFormula precisely; the compiler enforces total case coverage.
  • Canonical references translate directly — CUDD and NuSMV (both C) port cleanly while gaining memory safety on the most bug-prone subsystem (unique-table memory management).

Rejected: C/C++ (manual unique-table memory mgmt is the classic BDD bug source); Python/Java (ite/apply is too hot for GC + boxing); Haskell (laziness fights the strict mutable unique-table/cache discipline a BDD package requires).

1.10 Required prerequisites

  • Boolean algebra & BDDs — Shannon expansion, cofactors, ROBDD reduction rules, canonicity (Bryant 1986).
  • Finite automata — Büchi / generalized Büchi automata, ω-acceptance, product construction.
  • Temporal logic — CTL (EX/EU/EG/AX/AU/AG/EF/AF) and LTL (X/U/R/F/G) semantics over Kripke structures.
  • Fixpoint theory — Knaster-Tarski; lfp/gfp on the lattice of state sets; monotone iteration to convergence.
  • SAT / CNF — Tseitin transform, DIMACS format, DPLL/CDCL (for the bundled SatBackend).
  • Rust — ownership/borrowing, arena allocation with u32 indices, no_std + alloc, trait objects (SatBackend).

1.11 Foundational references (cited throughout)

NuSMV / nuXmv (SMV language + symbolic CTL/LTL); CUDD (canonical BDD package); Bryant 1986 (Graph-Based Algorithms for Boolean Function Manipulation — ROBDD); Burch/Clarke/McMillan/Dill 1992 (Symbolic Model Checking: 10²⁰ States and Beyond); Biere/Cimatti/Clarke/Zhu 1999 (Bounded Model Checking); Emerson-Lei 1986 (fair-cycle fixpoint); McMillan's SMV.

Load-bearing invariant (carried into every phase): any bug that breaks ROBDD canonicity silently corrupts O(1) equality and fixpoint convergence — wrong verdicts, no crash. Canonicity is the single most important property the entire stack rests on.

2. Why Build This

A symbolic model checker is the single project that fuses the three load-bearing skills of formal-methods systems engineering: canonical data structures with hashconsing, fixpoint computation over symbolic sets, and the automata-theoretic decision procedure. It is the historical artifact that turned model checking from a 10^4-state toy into industrial hardware verification. Building it from scratch — ROBDD package, SMV front end, symbolic FSM, CTL/LTL/BMC engines — forces mastery of ideas that recur in compilers, databases, theorem provers, and SAT/SMT.

2.1 Unique systems knowledge this teaches

Concept Where it lives here Why it is rare / transferable
Canonical DAG + hashcons (unique table on (level,low,high)) smv-bdd: BddManager unique table Structural identity ⇒ semantic identity; equality becomes O(1) handle compare. Same trick: LLVM FoldingSet, Egg/e-graphs, Rc-interned ASTs, Git object store, GHC Core.
Memoized fixed-point primitive (ite + computed cache) BddManager::ite, computed cache keyed (f,g,h) ite(f,g,h)=(f∧g)∨(¬f∧h) is THE single primitive; cache turns exponential recursion into O(|f|·|g|·|h|). Same shape as DP, parser packrat memo, query-plan memo.
Fixpoint computation over symbolic sets smv-check: reachable()=lfp Z. init∨Img(Z), EU=lfp, EG=gfp Reasoning about sets of states as one BDD instead of enumerating elements — the conceptual leap of abstract interpretation, dataflow analysis, Datalog seminaïve eval.
State-explosion defeated by symbolic representation image/pre-image via and_exists+rename; conjunctive partitioning; early quantification A BDD of 10^5 nodes can encode 10^20 reachable states. Teaches why representation beats enumeration — the difference between #279 (explicit) and this engine (symbolic).
Automata-theoretic verification smv-logic LTL→Büchi (GPVW/VWAA); LtlChecker product + Emerson-Lei fair-cycle gfp/lfp "A⊨φ iff product of model and Büchi(¬φ) has no reachable fair cycle." The bridge between logic and ω-automata; reused in runtime verification, synthesis, Spin/NuSMV.
Boolean encoding of finite domains smv-model::Encoder, VarEncoding (⌈log2⌉ bits + out-of-range constraint) Bit-blasting finite vars to BDD levels = same encoding compilers/SMT bit-vector theories/circuit synthesis use.
SAT reduction via unrolling + Tseitin smv-bmc::Unroller → CnfFormula; SatBackend I(s0)∧⋀T(si,si+1)∧⋁¬p(si) SAT iff k-step counterexample exists. Tseitin CNF transform is the universal "compile a circuit/formula to clauses" move.
Greatest- vs least-fixpoint duality EG=gfp (safety/recurrence), EU=lfp (eventuality) Knaster–Tarski in practice; convergence is guaranteed because the lattice of BDD state-sets is finite and operators monotone.

2.2 Why symbolic model checking matters (historical calibration)

  • Bryant 1986 (Graph-Based Algorithms) — ROBDDs gave Boolean functions a canonical form: fixed total variable order + two reduction rules (merge isomorphic nodes; delete any node with identical low/high children) ⇒ a unique DAG. Equivalence checking dropped from intractable to a pointer compare. This is the data-structure result the whole field rests on.
  • Burch/Clarke/McMillan/Dill 1992 — "Symbolic Model Checking: 10^20 States and Beyond." Representing the transition relation and reachable set as BDDs broke the state-explosion wall; verification of designs far past explicit enumeration's reach. The result that made model checking industrial (Intel/IBM hardware verification, the Pentium-era push after FDIV).
  • Emerson-Lei 1986 — fair-cycle detection as a nested gfp/lfp; the algorithmic core of symbolic LTL/fairness.
  • Biere/Cimatti/Clarke/Zhu 1999 — Bounded Model Checking: ride the SAT revolution by unrolling k steps; complete once k hits the completeness threshold (reachability/recurrence diameter). Often finds shallow bugs faster than BDDs.
  • NuSMV/nuXmv (SMV language + symbolic CTL/LTL) and CUDD (the canonical BDD package) are the production reference points; both are C — this project earns the same capability with Rust's memory safety over an index-based node arena (Bdd(u32), no Rc cycles, no use-after-free).

2.3 Engineering muscles built

  • Invariant discipline under silent corruption. The gravest failure class: any bug breaking ROBDD canonicity silently corrupts O(1) equality and fixpoint convergence — wrong verdicts, no crash. Canonicity is the load-bearing invariant; you learn to defend an invariant that, when violated, produces no stack trace, only lies.
  • Cache-and-arena performance engineering. Unique table + computed cache are the hottest paths; ite/and_exists dominate runtime. Forces real work on hash-table layout, memoization keys, allocation, and mark-sweep GC of dead nodes.
  • Property-based & differential testing as a way of life. Phase 13 gates on differential testing vs NuSMV (same verdict + comparable counterexamples) and BDD property/fuzz tests (apply vs truth table; canonicity). You learn to validate an engine whose correctness is undecidable to eyeball.
  • Lattice/fixpoint reasoning. Picking lfp vs gfp correctly for each temporal operator; proving termination from monotonicity over a finite lattice.
  • Counterexample extraction. Onion-ring backward sets for shortest CTL witnesses; lasso witnesses for EG; mapping opaque BDD cubes back to readable StateAssignment over original SMV vars — turning a proof-failure into an actionable trace.
  • Encoding & compilation. Flattening a modeling language, bit-blasting finite domains, Tseitin-transforming to CNF — the compiler-backend skillset in miniature.

2.4 Cross-links to the portfolio

Project Relationship to #281
#277 SAT solver smv-bmc::SatBackend is the consumer interface; #277 plugs in as the DIMACS-in/assignment-out backend. #281 does NOT re-implement a competitive solver — it bundles only a simple DPLL/CDCL fallback.
#278 SMT solver Next step beyond Boolean: lift finite-domain BDD encoding to theories (bit-vectors, arrays). #281's bit-blasting is SMT's bit-vector decision procedure in embryo.
#279 Explicit-state Model Checker The deliberate contrast: #279 enumerates individual states; #281 is symbolic — it represents state sets as BDDs and never enumerates. Same logics (CTL/LTL), opposite state representation; pairing them teaches when symbolic wins.
#280 Theorem Prover Model checking = automatic, finite-state, push-button; theorem proving = interactive, infinite-state, manual. #281 anchors the automated end of the verification spectrum.
Parser / compiler projects smv-syntax (Lexer, recursive-descent Parser, SmvModule AST, ParseError) and the CTL/LTL property parsers reuse the same lexing/parsing/AST/error-reporting machinery.

2.5 Calibration value

  • A working ROBDD package + symbolic CTL/LTL/BMC engine is a recognized staff/principal-level formal-methods milestone; it is the canonical "can you build NuSMV's core" question.
  • Quantifiable competence target (set in Phase 13): on a model+spec corpus, match NuSMV verdicts, verify reachable sets of ≥10^15–10^20 states with BDD node counts in the 10^4–10^6 range, and produce counterexamples of comparable length — i.e. demonstrably defeat state explosion on real models, not toys.
  • Demonstrates fluency in the exact stack — canonical structures, fixpoints, automata, SAT reduction — that underpins hardware verification, software model checking, and the broader #277–#280 verification suite.

2.6 What Mastery Looks Like

A practitioner who has completed this blueprint can demonstrate the following concrete competencies, each grounded in a named phase:

  • ROBDD canonicity reasoning (Phase 0–2): Given any two Boolean expressions, explain in under two minutes why f == g iff bdd(f) == bdd(g) (handle equality), trace the unique-table hashcons path for a sample ite(f,g,h) call, and identify the variable-ordering choice that will blow up the BDD for a ripple-carry adder — without running any code.
  • BDD memory management and GC (Phase 3): Diagnose a simulated "node table full" scenario: explain which roots survive mark-sweep, why the computed cache must be invalidated post-GC, and what the dead-node ratio indicates about variable-order quality; correlate with the node-count output from smv-cli bdd-stats.
  • Symbolic image computation and reachability (Phase 4–5): Write the forward-image formula (∃V . T(V,V') ∧ S(V) renamed to V) from memory, explain why and_exists fuses conjunction and existential quantification in one BDD traversal rather than two (P7/P8), and give the fixpoint termination condition for an SCC-free model.
  • CTL fixpoint derivation (Phase 6–7): Derive the EG(f) greatest-fixpoint formula from the Tarski fixpoint theorem, explain why EG requires a gfp (not lfp) and where that choice appears in the implementation (## 19), and construct a two-state counterexample to AG(p) by tracing the pre-image ∩ ¬p path.
  • LTL-to-Büchi translation and fair-cycle detection (Phase 8–9): Sketch the GPVW tableau construction for the formula G(p → F q) on a whiteboard, name the acceptance condition it produces, explain how Emerson-Lei fair-SCC detection decides emptiness without explicit cycle enumeration, and contrast this with Nested-DFS (ADR-15).
  • SAT-based bounded model checking (Phase 10–11): Unroll a two-variable, one-step transition relation into a Tseitin CNF manually, identify the completeness threshold concept, and articulate exactly why a SAT-BMC "no bug at depth k" result is NOT a proof of correctness unless k ≥ diameter (P11).
  • Differential validation and the "done" gate (Phase 12–13): Explain the NuSMV oracle protocol: which test cases to compare, what a verdict mismatch at depth k=1 implies about the ite kernel vs. the Tseitin encoder, and how to triage a false-negative (SMV says HOLDS, NuSMV says VIOLATED) vs. false-positive in the counterexample trace.

3. Architectural Overview

3.1 Canonical Architecture Diagram

                          ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                          │  smv-cli   (binary "smv")                                      │
                          │  subcommands: check · bmc · reachable · simulate ·             │
                          │               bdd-stats · encode · dot                         │
                          └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                 │  IN: model.smv  +  CTLSPEC / LTLSPEC text (UNTRUSTED)
                                 │  OUT: verdict (HOLDS / VIOLATED) + Trace + stats
                                 v
        ┌────────────────────────────────────────┐     specs      ┌────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ smv-syntax                              │ ─────────────> │ smv-logic                            │
        │  Lexer/Token -> Parser                  │  CTLSPEC/      │  CTL/LTL property parsers            │
        │  -> SmvModule AST                       │  LTLSPEC str   │  -> CtlFormula / LtlFormula          │
        │  (VAR/ASSIGN/DEFINE/INVAR/TRANS/SPEC)   │                │  NNF/normalize; AP -> Bdd binding    │
        │  name+type check; ParseError            │                │  (AP indices resolved vs ap table)   │
        └────────────────────────────────────────┘                └────────────────────────────────────┘
                 │  SmvModule AST                                          │  CtlFormula / LtlFormula
                 v                                                         v
        ┌────────────────────────────────────────┐    EncodedFsm  ┌────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ smv-model                               │ ─────────────> │ smv-check                            │
        │  Encoder: finite vars -> boolean        │  present/next  │  reachable() = lfp Z. init OR Img(Z) │
        │  BDD levels (VarEncoding);              │  init:Bdd      │  CtlChecker: EX/EU/EG fixpoints      │
        │  build init / trans / invar /           │  trans:Bdd     │   -> SAT state-set Bdd; spec HOLDS   │
        │  fairness as BDDs -> EncodedFsm         │  invar/fair    │   iff init AND not sat(f)==false     │
        │  conjunctive partitioning of trans      │  ap:[(Atom,    │  LtlChecker: Buchi x model +         │
        │                                         │   Bdd)]        │   Emerson-Lei fair cycle; Trace      │
        └────────────────────────────────────────┘                └────────────────────────────────────┘
                 │  init:Bdd  trans:Bdd  cube                            │  uses (ite/and_exists/rename/
                 │  VarMap(present<->next)                               │        exists/restrict/satcount)
                 v                                                       v
        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ smv-bdd   BddManager                                                                            │
        │  node table + unique table (hashcons on (level,low,high)) + computed cache + fixed var order    │
        │  ite (THE primitive); not/and/or/xor/imp; restrict (cofactor); exists/forall over a cube;       │
        │  and_exists (relational product); rename (present<->next); satcount; node_count;                │
        │  mark-sweep GC of dead nodes; dot export                                                        │
        │  INVARIANT: canonicity => handle equality iff boolean-function equality (O(1))                  │
        └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                 ^  CNF unroll (BMC path; does NOT use BddManager)
                 │  EncodedFsm{init,trans} + LtlFormula/InvarSpec
        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ smv-bmc   Unroller                                                                              │
        │  I(s0) AND conj_{i<k} T(si,si+1) AND or_{i<=k} not-prop(si)  --Tseitin-->  CnfFormula(DIMACS)   │
        │  SatBackend trait (DIMACS in / SatResult out): bundled DPLL/CDCL OR #277 hook                   │
        │  SatResult::Sat(model) -> StateAssignment per step -> Trace                                     │
        └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

3.2 Data Flows

# Flow Producer -> Consumer Payload Direction
F1 model.smv text -> AST smv-cli -> smv-syntax UTF-8 source bytes parse
F2 AST -> FSM smv-syntax SmvModule -> smv-model SmvModule (VAR/ASSIGN/DEFINE/INVAR/TRANS) encode
F3 FSM -> engine smv-model EncodedFsm -> smv-check {present,next,init,trans,invar,fairness,ap} check
F4 spec text -> formula smv-syntax SpecDecl -> smv-logic CTLSPEC/LTLSPEC string + ap table parse+bind
F5 formula -> verdict smv-logic CtlFormula/LtlFormula -> smv-check typed formula w/ Ap(Bdd) leaves evaluate
F6 FSM + formula -> result smv-check -> smv-cli verdict (HOLDS/VIOLATED) + Trace report
F7 FSM + formula -> CNF (BMC) smv-model+smv-logic -> smv-bmc EncodedFsm{init,trans} + bound k unroll
F8 CNF -> model smv-bmc CnfFormula -> SatBackend -> Trace DIMACS literals; SatResult solve

Pipeline (verification path): model.smv -> Lexer/Parser -> SmvModule -> Encoder -> EncodedFsm ‖ SpecDecl -> CtlFormula/LtlFormula (Ap->Bdd); then EncodedFsm + formula -> {CtlChecker | LtlChecker | Unroller+SatBackend} -> verdict + Trace. All boolean-function manipulation funnels through one BddManager; the BMC path is the only one that bypasses it (CNF/SAT instead of BDD).

3.3 Boundaries

Boundary Location Rule
Trust boundary smv-cli ↔ smv-syntax/smv-logic Model .smv text + spec text are UNTRUSTED. Lexer/Parser are the only components touching raw bytes; every malformed input maps to a typed ParseError/ParseErrorKind (UnexpectedToken, UnknownVar, BadType, TypeMismatch, CircularDefine, OutOfRange) — never a panic, never UB. Past this boundary the AST is well-typed; downstream crates assume validated structure.
Concurrency boundary inside smv-bdd BddManager is a single-threaded mutable arena (&mut self on ithvar/ite/and/or/restrict/exists/forall/and_exists/rename). One model-check = one sequential thread driving one manager; the unique table + computed cache are not shared across threads. No Send/Sync on live Bdd handles across managers — a Bdd(u32) is an index valid only for its owning manager. Parallelism (if ever) is between independent managers, never within one.
Persistence boundary none at runtime The verifier is a pure batch transform: read model.smv + specs once, hold all BDD state in RAM, emit verdict/Trace/stats, exit. No DB, no on-disk index, no checkpointing of the node table. Only optional output artifacts: dot graph files (dot subcommand) and a textual Trace. Re-running re-encodes from source — deterministic given fixed var order.
Module boundary (encode) smv-syntax ↔ smv-model AST carries no BDD handles; VarEncoding/EncodedFsm are the first artifacts to reference Bdd. Variable→level assignment is fixed here and frozen for the manager's lifetime (canonicity prerequisite).

3.4 Per-Box Responsibilities

3.4.1 smv-bdd — the ROBDD engine

Responsibility: the shared boolean-function representation under every other crate; owns BddManager, the Bdd(u32) handle, the unique table (hashcons on (level,low,high)), the computed cache, and the fixed total variable order. In: levels (via ithvar), existing Bdd handles, cubes. Out: canonical Bdd handles; satcount(f,nvars)->u128; node_count(f)->usize; dot export. Primitive surface: ite (the one primitive); not/and/or/xor/imp as ite specializations; restrict (cofactor); exists/forall over a cube; and_exists (relational product, the image workhorse); rename (present↔next swap); mark-sweep GC of dead nodes. Key invariant: canonicity — fixed var order + two reduction rules (delete any node with low==high; merge isomorphic nodes via hashcons) ⇒ structural handle equality iff boolean-function equality, so equality + fixpoint-convergence tests are O(1). This is the load-bearing invariant: any breach silently corrupts equality and fixpoints, yielding wrong verdicts with no crash. no_std + alloc. Phases: 0 (handle/manager shells), 1 (node+unique table, reduction, ite, terminals, canonicity property test), 2 (restrict/exists/forall/and_exists/rename/satcount/node_count/cache/GC/dot), tuned in 12 (sifting, cache tuning).

3.4.2 smv-syntax — the SMV modeling language front end

Responsibility: turn untrusted .smv source into a validated SmvModule AST. In: UTF-8 source bytes. Out: SmvModule { vars, defines, assigns, trans, invar, specs }; on failure a typed ParseError. Components: Lexer/Token; a recursive-descent Parser; AST nodes VarDecl/VarType(Boolean/Enum(Vec<Atom>)/Range(i64,i64)), AssignDecl/AssignKind(Init/Next/Plain), DefineDecl, InvarDecl, TransDecl, SpecDecl(Ctl(String)/Ltl(String)/InvarSpec(Expr)), and the Expr tree (Var/Const/Not/Bin/Case/Next). Performs name resolution + type checking + CircularDefine detection on the DEFINE dependency graph. Key invariant: no panic on adversarial input — every lexical/syntactic/semantic error is reported as a precise ParseErrorKind; a returned SmvModule is guaranteed well-typed and name-resolved, so smv-model may encode it without re-validating. no_std + alloc. Phases: 0 (AST shells, error types), 3 (full Lexer+Parser, name/type checking, CircularDefine).

3.4.3 smv-model — flattening + boolean encoding (Encoder / EncodedFsm)

Responsibility: flatten the typed AST and boolean-encode finite-domain state variables onto BDD levels, then build the symbolic FSM. In: SmvModule + a BddManager. Out: VarEncoding { levels, ty } per variable and EncodedFsm { present, next, init, trans, invar, fairness, ap }. Encoding rules: Boolean→1 level; Enum/scalar→ceil(log2 |domain|) bits + an out-of-range constraint BDD; Range(lo,hi)→bounded binary. Builds two VarMaps (present, next), compiles each Expr to a Bdd over present levels (and over next when wrapped in Next), assembles init = init() ∧ INVAR, trans = next() ∧ TRANS ∧ next-INVAR (stored conjunctively partitioned), and fairness Bdds. Key invariant: level assignment is total, injective, and frozen for the manager's lifetime, and present/next are disjoint level ranges related only by rename — the prerequisite that makes downstream image/pre-image and canonicity sound. Depends: smv-bdd + smv-syntax. Phases: 0 (type shells), 4 (VarEncoding, present/next VarMap, Expr→Bdd), 5 (init/trans/invar/fairness, conjunctive partitioning).

3.4.4 smv-logic — temporal logic front end

Responsibility: parse property text into typed temporal formulas and bind atomic propositions to BDDs. In: SpecDecl strings (Ctl/Ltl) + the EncodedFsm.ap table [(Atom,Bdd)]. Out: CtlFormula (Ap/Not/And/Or/Ex/Eg/Eu; AX/AU/AG/AF/EF derived) and LtlFormula (Ap/Not/And/Or/X/U/R/F/G), each in NNF with Ap leaves carrying a resolved Bdd. Components: the CTL and LTL property parsers, NNF/normalization (push negations to leaves; reduce derived operators to the EX/EU/EG and X/U primitives), and AP→Bdd binding against the model's ap. Key invariant: AP closure + NNF normal form — every atomic proposition resolves to exactly one model Bdd, and every formula handed to smv-check is in NNF over the primitive operator set, so the checker dispatches on a small fixed set of cases. Depends: smv-bdd + smv-model. no_std + alloc. Phases: 0 (formula shells), 7 (CTL parse + derived expansion path used by CtlChecker), 9 (LTL parse + NNF feeding the Buchi build).

3.4.5 smv-check — the symbolic engine (CtlChecker / LtlChecker)

Responsibility: decide whether the FSM satisfies a temporal property, entirely by BDD set operations (state SETS as BDDs; never enumerates states). In: &mut BddManager + &EncodedFsm + a CtlFormula/LtlFormula. Out: a SAT state-set Bdd (CtlChecker::check), reachable()->Bdd, and counterexample(...)->Option<Trace>. CTL: EX p=preimage(p)=and_exists(trans,p,next-cube) then rename; E[p U q]=lfp Z. q OR (p AND EX Z); EG p=gfp Z. p AND EX Z; spec HOLDS iff init AND not sat(f) == bdd_false (i.e. init ⊆ sat(f)). reachable=lfp Z. init OR Img(Z) where Img(S)=(exists present.(trans AND S))[next->present]. LTL: build a (generalized) Buchi automaton for ¬φ, take the symbolic product with EncodedFsm, then Emerson-Lei nested gfp/lfp fair-cycle detection over EX and the acceptance/fairness sets; A φ holds iff no fair cycle is reachable from init. Witnesses: onion-ring backward sets for shortest CTL counterexamples, lasso witnesses for EG/LTL, cubes mapped back to readable StateAssignment; Trace { states, loopback }. Key invariant: fixpoint soundness rides on canonicity — convergence is detected by O(1) handle equality, so a correct BddManager makes lfp/gfp termination and the subset test exact. Depends: smv-bdd + smv-model + smv-logic. Phases: 6 (reachability/image/pre-image fixpoint framework), 7 (CtlChecker), 8 (counterexamples/witnesses/StateAssignment), 9–10 (LtlChecker: product + Emerson-Lei), 12 (fairness, early-quantification schedule).

3.4.6 smv-bmc — Bounded Model Checking (Unroller / SatBackend)

Responsibility: the SAT-based path — find a length-≤k counterexample by unrolling the transition relation into CNF (no BDDs). In: EncodedFsm { init, trans }, an LtlFormula/InvarSpec, and a bound k. Out: CnfFormula { clauses, nvars } (DIMACS literals), then a Trace. Components: Unroller builds I(s0) AND conj_{i<k} T(si,si+1) AND or_{i<=k} not-prop(si) via a Tseitin transform (fresh variables per gate, linear-size CNF); the SatBackend trait (solve(&CnfFormula)->SatResult, DIMACS in / SatResult::Sat(Vec<bool>)|Unsat out) so the bundled simple DPLL/CDCL or #277 plugs in interchangeably; on Sat, each step's bits decode to a StateAssignment, yielding the counterexample Trace. NON-GOAL: smv-bmc does not implement a competitive SAT core — that is #277; it only consumes a SatBackend. Key invariant: unrolling fidelity + completeness threshold — the CNF is SAT iff a k-step counterexample exists, and safety checking is complete once k reaches the reachability/recurrence diameter. Depends: smv-model + smv-logic. Phases: 11 (Unroller, Tseitin CNF, SatBackend, bundled DPLL/CDCL, incremental k, model→Trace), 12 (completeness threshold).

3.4.7 smv-cli — the "smv" binary

Responsibility: the user-facing orchestrator wiring source+spec through the pipeline and rendering results. In: CLI args, model.smv, CTLSPEC/LTLSPEC. Out: process exit code + textual verdict/Trace/stats/dot. Subcommands: check (CTL/LTL via smv-check), bmc (smv-bmc), reachable (lfp reachable set + satcount), simulate (step the FSM), bdd-stats (node_count/unique-table/cache metrics), encode (dump VarEncoding/EncodedFsm), dot (export BDDs). Key invariant: it is glue only — owns no verification logic; all decisions live in smv-check/smv-bmc, all encoding in smv-model, all parsing in smv-syntax/smv-logic. It is the sole std crate and the sole holder of the trust boundary at its input edge. Depends: all six library crates. Phases: 0 (binary skeleton, smoke test: ithvar(0); parse a 1-var model; assert bdd_true != bdd_false), incrementally extended each phase as subcommands light up, finalized in 13 (validation/differential-testing harness vs NuSMV).

4. System Constraints

Hard, quantified envelope for the symbolic engine. All numbers are targets/ceilings, enforced or asserted somewhere downstream (config defaults, debug_assert!, conformance Phase 13 ## 25). A symbolic checker's cost is dominated by live BDD node count, not by reachable-state count — 10^20 states can fit in 10^4 nodes, or blow past 10^7 nodes for a badly-ordered 10^6-state model. Constraints therefore bound nodes, ops, and cache behavior, with reachable-state scale as a derived capability.

4.1 Quantified constraints table

# Constraint Target / Ceiling Unit Enforced where Failure mode if violated
C1 BddManager live node ceiling 1e7 live nodes (configurable cap node_cap) nodes smv-bdd alloc path BddError::NodeCapExceeded (typed, no OOM-abort)
C2 Bytes per node 16–24 B (level u32 + low u32 + high u32 + next-link u32 + refcount u16/pad) B/node node-table layout exceeds C3 memory ceiling
C3 BddManager memory ceiling (nodes only) ≤ 256 MiB default (= ~1e7 nodes × 24 B + slack); hard cap configurable MiB derived from C1×C2 cap overflow → C1 error
C4 Unique-table (hashcons) load factor resize when entries/buckets > 0.75; buckets = pow2; rehash on cross ratio unique-table insert degraded insert → O(chain) collisions
C5 Computed/op cache size 2^20–2^24 entries (default 2^22 ≈ 4.19M), fixed-size, lossy (overwrite-on-collision) entries computed cache lower hit rate, more recompute (not wrong, just slow)
C6 Computed-cache hit rate ≥ 90% on reachability fixpoint of corpus models % Phase 13 conformance ite/apply complexity regresses toward worst case
C7 Reachable-state scale (symbolic) verify 1e9–1e20 reachable states within node/time budget states Phase 6 ## 18 / 13 ## 25 timeout or C1 cap
C8 ite/apply throughput ≥ 5e6 cache-mediated ops/sec (single core, warm cache) ops/s Phase 1 ## 13 / 13 bench misses bench gate
C9 Unique-table lookup (warm) ≤ ~150 ns amortized per hashcons probe ns smv-bdd feeds C8 regression
C10 Fixpoint-iteration latency (reachable lfp) ≤ 60 s wall for corpus models ≤ 1e7 peak nodes s smv-check timeout verdict, not crash
C11 Fixpoint iteration count converges in ≤ diameter+1 steps; abort if > iter_cap (default 10^6) steps reachable/CTL loop CheckError::FixpointDiverged (guards a canonicity bug)
C12 BMC bound k default k_max = 100; configurable; incremental k=0,1,…,k_max steps Unroller smv-bmc inconclusive (not wrong) below completeness threshold
C13 BMC SAT call budget one SatBackend::solve per k; per-call timeout sat_timeout default 300 s calls/s smv-bmc BmcResult::Inconclusive
C14 CNF size growth (Tseitin) clauses = O(k · |T|), linear in k; |T| = encoded transition gate count clauses Unroller memory pressure → cap k
C15 Boolean encoding width enum/scalar of domain d → ⌈log2 d⌉ bits; Range(lo,hi) → ⌈log2(hi−lo+1)⌉ bits + out-of-range constraint BDD bits/var Encoder Phase 4 ## 16 wider order → bigger BDDs (C1)
C16 Total BDD levels (vars) present + next interleaved; ≤ 2·(sum encoded bits); soft ceiling ~2000 levels before reorder advised levels VarMap / Encoder ordering sensitivity (C20)
C17 satcount precision exact up to u128 (≤ 2^128−1 states); saturates/typed-error beyond states satcount return type BddError::CountOverflow (no silent wrap)
C18 Startup / global init zero global mutable state; no lazy statics; manager built explicitly — all crates violates determinism (C19)
C19 Determinism same model + same variable order ⇒ bit-identical node counts, BDD handles, and verdict across runs/platforms — Phase 13 ## 25 non-reproducible bug reports; hides C-class faults
C20 Variable-ordering sensitivity node count can vary 2^n between best/worst order; default = declaration order; sifting (Phase 12 ## 24) opt-in factor documented exponential blowup, not incorrectness
C21 Concurrency model single process, single thread per BddManager; Bdd handles not Send/Sync across managers — smv-bdd API UB / handle aliasing if shared
C22 Recovery: parse/encode parse + encode errors are typed Result, never panic!/unwrap on user input — smv-syntax / smv-model a panic is a release-gate failure
C23 GC (mark-sweep) trigger when live/total > 0.90 or on node_cap near-miss; reclaims dead (refcount 0) nodes ratio smv-bdd Phase 2 ## 14 premature C1 cap hit
C24 GC pause ≤ 100 ms for a 1e7-node table (single mark+sweep pass, O(live)) ms smv-bdd latency spike in fixpoint loop
C25 no_std footprint smv-bdd/-syntax/-model/-logic/-bmc are no_std + alloc; only smv-cli is std — crate manifests breaks embeddability target
C26 Counterexample length shortest CTL counterexample = onion-ring BFS depth; lasso ≤ |reachable| states states Phase 8 ## 20 over-long/non-minimal trace

4.2 Memory model notes

  • Node table = index arena of u32 handles (Bdd(u32)); no Rc, no cycles, no pointers → kills use-after-free/double-free that plague C BDD libs. Max 2^32−2 addressable nodes; C1 cap (1e7) is the operational ceiling, far below the type limit.
  • Layout (C2): one node = { level: u32, low: u32, high: u32, next: u32 (unique-table chain), refcount: u16, flags: u16 } = 20 B; pad to 24 B for alignment. Terminals (bdd_true/bdd_false) are reserved indices 0/1, never GC'd.
  • Two arenas only: node table (the DAG) + unique table (hashcons buckets, Vec<u32> heads). Computed cache (C5) is a separate fixed Vec of (key, result) slots — lossy, never grows, so it cannot cause OOM.
  • Memory ceiling (C3) counts nodes only; cache + unique-table buckets add ≤ (2^22·12 B ≈ 48 MiB cache) + (buckets·4 B). Total resident default budget ≈ 320 MiB; configurable upward for large verifications.

4.3 Throughput & latency notes

  • ite complexity is O(|f|·|g|·|h|) only with the computed cache (C5/C6); a cold or thrashing cache degrades toward exponential. C6 (≥90% hit) is the load-bearing performance invariant for fixpoints.
  • Forward image (and_exists + rename) is the hot inner op of reachability/CTL; conjunctive partitioning + early quantification (Phase 6 ## 18, Phase 12 ## 24) keep intermediate BDD node counts bounded so C10/C24 hold.
  • Fixpoint latency (C10) scales with peak intermediate node count and iteration count (C11 = ~diameter), not with reachable-state count (C7) — this is why 10^20 states is feasible.

4.4 Correctness, recovery & isolation notes

  • Canonicity is the gravest invariant (see Threat Model): a reduction/hashcons bug breaks O(1) equality → fixpoints "converge" to wrong sets → wrong verdict, no crash. C19 (determinism) is the primary detector; C11 (FixpointDiverged) is the safety net.
  • Recovery contract: every fallible boundary returns a typed enum (ParseError/ParseErrorKind, BddError, CheckError, BmcResult::Inconclusive). A cap overflow (C1) returns Err(NodeCapExceeded), it does not abort/OOM-kill the process — the CLI prints the cap and exits non-zero cleanly.
  • No global init (C18) + single-thread (C21) ⇒ the engine is a deterministic pure function of (model, variable_order, caps); reproducibility (C19) is verifiable bit-for-bit, which is mandatory for differential testing vs NuSMV (Phase 13 ## 25).
  • Ordering sensitivity (C20) is a performance envelope, never a correctness one: any valid total order yields the same verdict, only different node counts and times. Default order = SMV declaration order; dynamic sifting is opt-in (Phase 12 ## 24).

5. Required Reading

Curated, load-bearing only. Each entry: exact locus + why + what to extract (a decision/algorithm/invariant we implement). Read in the order: BDD package → symbolic MC theory → temporal logic → BMC/SAT → reference code. The four pillars map to the spine: ROBDD (smv-bdd), symbolic CTL/LTL (smv-check), BMC/Tseitin (smv-bmc), SMV language (smv-syntax/smv-model).

# Book (author, ed.) Chapters Extract → applies to
B1 Clarke, Grumberg, Peled — Model Checking (MIT, 1999/2018 2e) 5 (Symbolic MC), 6 (Partitioned trans rel + early quant), 4 (CTL), 9 (Fairness), 7 (BDDs) The CTL fixpoint table (sat(EX)=preimage; sat(E[pUq])=lfp; sat(EG p)=gfp); image via and_exists; conjunctive partitioning + early-quantification schedule. → smv-check Ph6–7, smv-model Ph5, Ph12
B2 Baier, Katoen — Principles of Model Checking (MIT, 2008) 5 (LTL), 4.3 (Büchi/GNBA→NBA), 6.3 (CTL), 9 (fairness, persistence/recurrence), 4.4 (nested DFS — for contrast) Precise LTL/CTL semantics (the conformance oracle); generalized→nondeterministic Büchi degeneralization; fair-cycle = reachable accepting cycle. → smv-logic, LtlChecker Ph9–10
B3 Huth, Ryan — Logic in Computer Science (Cambridge, 2e 2004) 3.2 (LTL), 3.4 (CTL + CTL*), 3.6.1 (SMV models), 3.7 (BDD intro) Operator-precedence + NNF/derivation identities (AG p=¬EF¬p; AF p=¬EG¬p; AX p=¬EX¬p); SMV next()/init() modeling idioms. → smv-syntax parser, smv-logic NNF

5.2 Papers

# Paper (author, venue, year) Core contribution Implementation relevance
P1 Bryant — Graph-Based Algorithms for Boolean Function Manipulation (IEEE TC, 1986) ROBDD: fixed total var order + 2 reduction rules ⇒ canonical DAG; apply via recursion+memo THE smv-bdd blueprint: reduce-rules (drop node if low==high; merge isomorphic), ite/apply recursion, computed cache, O(|f|*|g|) bound. Ph1
P2 Brace, Rudell, Bryant — Efficient Implementation of a BDD Package (DAC, 1990) ite() as the single universal primitive; hashcons unique table; computed cache; complement edges Confirms ite-only design; unique table = hashcons on (level,low,high); cache key (f,g,h); O(1) equality via shared nodes. Ph1–2
P3 Burch, Clarke, McMillan, Dill — Symbolic Model Checking: 10²⁰ States and Beyond (LICS/I&C, 1990/92) Represent init/trans/state-sets as BDDs; CTL by BDD fixpoints; relational product Reachable=lfp Z.init∨Img(Z); EU=lfp, EG=gfp; image=∃present.(trans∧S) then rename. smv-check Ph6–7
P4 Emerson, Lei — Efficient Model Checking in Fragments of the Propositional μ-Calculus (LICS, 1986) Fair-cycle as nested gfp/lfp; fairness handled in fixpoint LtlChecker fair states = gfp over EX + acceptance/fairness sets; nested μ-calculus iteration. Ph10, Ph12
P5 Biere, Cimatti, Clarke, Zhu — Symbolic Model Checking without BDDs (TACAS, 1999) BMC: unroll T k steps + ¬prop → SAT; counterexample iff SAT Unroller builds I(s0)∧⋀ T(si,si+1)∧⋁¬p(si); SAT model→Trace; SatBackend. Ph11
P6 Biere, Cimatti, Clarke, Strichman, Zhu — Bounded Model Checking (Adv. in Computers, 2003) Completeness thresholds (diameter/recurrence diameter); LTL BMC encodings (with loop/no-loop) The "complete at k=diameter" gate; loop-selector vars for non-safety LTL BMC. Ph11–12
P7 Rudell — Dynamic Variable Ordering for OBDDs (ICCAD, 1993) Sifting: move each var to its best level by adjacent swaps; reorder triggers Dynamic reordering (sifting) in BddManager; growth-threshold trigger; swap preserves canonicity. Ph12
P8 Gerth, Peled, Vardi, Wolper (GPVW) — Simple On-the-fly Automatic Verification of LTL (PSTV, 1995) Tableau LTL→generalized Büchi; on-the-fly node expansion LTL→Büchi construction; node fields (Now/Next/Old); generalized acceptance per U-subformula. Ph9
P9 Coudert, Berthet, Madre — Verification of Sync Sequential Machines Based on Symbolic Execution (Automatic Verification Methods, 1989) Image computation via the constrain/restrict + ∃; early quantification and_exists (relational product) instead of build-then-quantify; restrict to shrink intermediate BDDs. Ph2, Ph6
P10 Burch, Clarke, Long — Symbolic Model Checking with Partitioned Transition Relations (VLSI, 1991) Conjunctive/disjunctive partitioned trans rel + quantification schedule Store trans as ⋀ Ti (sync) / ⋁ Ti (async); quantify out vars as early as the schedule allows. Ph5, Ph12
P11 Somenzi — Binary Decision Diagrams (NATO/CUDD notes, 1999) Practical BDD engineering: GC, cache sizing, reordering hooks Mark-sweep GC of dead nodes; cache ratio vs unique-table size; satcount via shared-node DP. Ph2, Ph12
P12 Clarke, Grumberg, Hamaguchi — Another Look at LTL Model Checking (CAV, 1994) Symbolic LTL via tableau product + fair cycle Alternative to P8 for symbolic product encoding (state bits + transition Bdd). Ph9–10 cross-check

Optional deep cuts: Tarski (1955) — lattice fixpoint existence (justifies lfp/gfp termination on the finite powerset lattice); Vardi-Wolper (1986) — automata-theoretic LTL (theoretical backing for the Büchi product).

DO NOT copy: it whole — #277 is the real solver; bundled DPLL/CDCL is a fallback behind the SatBackend trait, not a competitive core (non-goal)
R6 PRISM / model corpus (case studies) dining philosophers, mutex/Peterson, arbiter, traffic-light .smv models TAKE: canonical model+spec fixtures for the Ph13 corpus (known verdicts). DO NOT copy: PRISM's probabilistic/timed extensions (non-goal)

Boundary reminder (non-goals): R5 is a reference, not a target — smv-bmc consumes a DIMACS SatBackend (#277), never re-implements a competitive solver; no SMT (#278), no explicit-state enumeration (#279).

Spec Source / locus Authoritative for Used by
SMV language reference NuSMV 2.6 User Manual §2 (Input Language); McMillan SMV manual VAR (boolean/{enum}/a..b range), DEFINE, ASSIGN init(x):=/next(x):=/x:=, INVAR, TRANS, CTLSPEC/LTLSPEC; case…esac; operator set/precedence smv-syntax grammar (Ph3), smv-model encoding (Ph4)
CTL semantics Clarke-Emerson-Sistla 1986; B1 ch.4; B3 §3.4 Path quantifiers A/E × temporal X/U/G/F; EX/EU/EG as basis; derived AX/AU/AG/EF/AF smv-logic CtlFormula + NNF; CtlChecker fixpoints (Ph7)
LTL semantics Pnueli 1977; B2 ch.5; B3 §3.2 X/U/R/F/G over infinite paths; A φ ⇔ Büchi(¬φ)×model has no fair cycle smv-logic LtlFormula; LtlChecker (Ph9–10)
Büchi acceptance B2 §4.3 (GNBA/NBA); P8 (Generalized) acceptance condition; degeneralization; fair = reachable accepting cycle Ph9 construction, Ph10 Emerson-Lei
DIMACS CNF format SAT Competition DIMACS spec p cnf <nvars> <nclauses>; one clause/line, space-sep literals, 0-terminated; i/-i polarity CnfFormula { clauses: Vec<Vec<i32>>, nvars: u32 }; SatBackend wire format (Ph11)
DOT / Graphviz Graphviz DOT language Digraph syntax; node shapes; solid/dashed edges (high/low child) smv-bdd dot export (Ph2); smv-cli dot subcommand

Spec invariants to enforce while reading: (1) SMV next() is legal only on the RHS of next(x):=/TRANS, never inside init() — parser rejects otherwise (ParseErrorKind::UnexpectedToken). (2) enum/range values out of declared domain ⇒ OutOfRange; the encoder also emits an out-of-range constraint BDD so unused codes of a ceil(log2)-bit field are excluded. (3) DIMACS literals are 1-indexed and signed; variable 0 is illegal — the Tseitin transform allocates fresh vars from nvars+1.

6. Design Principles

Non-negotiable laws for the whole workspace. Each is stated once here and referenced by name in later phases (## 12..## 25). Violation column = the concrete failure the principle exists to prevent. Ordered roughly by blast radius (P1 = load-bearing).

6.1 Catalogue

# Principle One-line law
P1 Canonicity is sacred Equal boolean functions ⇒ identical Bdd handle, always.
P2 Hashcons everything No node (level,low,high) exists twice; unique table is the only node factory.
P3 ite is the only primitive All ops reduce to ite; one correct recursion, one place to audit.
P4 Cache or die Every memoizable op consults the computed cache first; misses cost exponential.
P5 Symbolic, never enumerate State sets are Bdd; we never materialize individual states.
P6 Determinism: fixed order ⇒ fixed result Same model + same var order ⇒ bit-identical BDDs, traces, verdicts.
P7 Errors, never panic on untrusted models Bad .smv input ⇒ typed ParseError, never a panic/abort.
P8 Counterexamples are first-class A NO/FAIL verdict MUST yield a concrete Trace; absence is a bug.
P9 Variable order is everything (and tunable) Order picks BDD size between linear and exponential; expose + auto-tune it.
P10 Bounded resources Node/cache caps ⇒ typed BddError::NodeLimit, never silent OOM.
P11 Differential truth: NuSMV is the oracle A verdict is "correct" iff it matches NuSMV/nuXmv on the corpus.
P12 Separate the SAT backend BMC depends on the SatBackend trait, never a concrete solver (#277 is external).
P13 Fixpoints converge or are caught Every lfp/gfp is monotone + bounded; non-convergence ⇒ assertion, not a hang.
P14 no_std + alloc for the engine core smv-bdd/syntax/model/logic stay portable; only smv-cli pulls std.
P15 One owner of mutability &mut BddManager is the single mutation root; no interior Rc/RefCell graph.

6.2 The principles in full

P1 — Canonicity is sacred (the load-bearing invariant)

  • Law: fixed total variable order + both reduction rules (delete node whose low == high; merge isomorphic subgraphs) ⇒ a unique canonical DAG. Two Bdd handles are equal iff they denote the same boolean function. Equality = u32 comparison, O(1).
  • Tradeoff: every constructor pays a unique-table lookup; we buy O(1) equality, free tautology/contradiction checks (f == bdd_true/bdd_false), and terminating fixpoints with it.
  • Operational: Phase 1 (## 13) ships a canonicity property test; Phase 13 (## 25) fuzzes apply-vs-truth-table. CI gate.
  • Violation: if any path emits a node with low == high, two handles for x ∧ ¬x and false differ ⇒ init AND not sat(f) never reaches bdd_false ⇒ the reachable lfp (P5/P13) never converges or converges wrong ⇒ a wrong verdict with no crash. This is the gravest failure class in the system; canonicity is checked, not assumed.

P2 — Hashcons everything

  • Law: BddManager creates a node only via the unique table, hashconsed on (level, low, high). A request for an existing triple returns the existing Bdd; it is impossible to allocate a duplicate.
  • Tradeoff: one hash probe per mk_node; in exchange P1 holds by construction, structural sharing minimizes memory, and node_count (## 14) is meaningful.
  • Operational: the unique table is the only &mut-node factory (see P15); GC (## 14) mark-sweeps unreferenced nodes but never deletes a shared live one.
  • Violation: bypass the table to "quickly" build a node and you mint a second handle for an existing function ⇒ P1 broken ⇒ same silent-wrong-verdict path.

P3 — ite is the only primitive

  • Law: ite(f,g,h) = (f ∧ g) ∨ (¬f ∧ h) is THE recursion. and/or/xor/imp and derived ops are thin wrappers: and(f,g)=ite(f,g,⊥), or(f,g)=ite(f,⊤,g), xor(f,g)=ite(f,not g,g), imp(f,g)=ite(f,g,⊤). not is O(1) (complement edge / cache).
  • Tradeoff: one slightly more general routine instead of N hand-tuned ones; one correct Shannon-cofactor recursion to verify, one cache key shape (f,g,h).
  • Operational: new boolean ops are added as ite wrappers, never as fresh recursions. Audit surface = one function.
  • Violation: a bespoke and recursion that forgets to cofactor on the top variable, or skips the reduction check, produces non-reduced nodes ⇒ P1 broken; and you now have two code paths to keep canonical instead of one.

P4 — Cache or die

  • Law: every memoizable op (ite, restrict, exists, forall, and_exists, rename, satcount substructure) checks the computed cache keyed on its operands before recursing, and inserts on the way out.
  • Tradeoff: memory for the cache vs. complexity. With the cache ite is O(|f|·|g|·|h|) (polynomial in node counts); without it the recursion is exponential in BDD depth.
  • Operational: cache size + collision policy tuned in Phase 12 (## 24); a cache miss must be safe (recompute), a stale/wrong hit must be impossible (keys are full canonical handles + op tag).
  • Violation: drop the cache and a 20-variable model's image computation that should take milliseconds blows up to minutes/hours; reachability (## 18) and CTL/LTL checking become unusable. A wrong cache key (e.g. ignoring the op tag) returns one op's result for another ⇒ silent wrong BDDs.

P5 — Symbolic, never enumerate

  • Law: this engine represents state SETS as Bdd and operates on them wholesale (image/pre-image via and_exists + rename, fixpoints over sets). It never iterates individual states. (Explicit-state enumeration is #279, a non-goal.)
  • Tradeoff: BDD size is unpredictable (P9) but can encode 10^20+ states in thousands of nodes (Burch/Clarke/McMillan/Dill 1992); enumeration is predictable but dies at ~10^7 states.
  • Operational: reachable() -> Bdd, CtlChecker::check() -> Bdd all return set-valued BDDs; satcount reports |set| as u128 without listing members.
  • Violation: "just loop over the reachable states to check a property" reintroduces the state explosion the whole project exists to avoid — a 100-bit state space has 2^100 states, unrepresentable explicitly, trivial as a BDD.

P6 — Determinism: fixed order ⇒ fixed result

  • Law: given a model and a fixed variable order, every output — BDD node ids, node_count, satcount, the chosen counterexample Trace, the verdict — is reproducible bit-for-bit. No reliance on hash-map iteration order or address-derived randomness for results.
  • Tradeoff: we forgo nondeterministic shortcuts; we gain a usable differential oracle (P11) and reproducible bug reports.
  • Operational: counterexample selection uses the deterministic onion-ring construction (## 20) so the same shortest trace is returned each run; any iteration over a HashMap that influences output is sorted or replaced by ordered structures.
  • Violation: if trace extraction depends on HashMap iteration order, two runs disagree on the witness, P11 differential tests flake, and users cannot reproduce a reported counterexample.

P7 — Errors, never panic on untrusted models

  • Law: every failure reachable from a user-supplied .smv file or property string is a typed Result::Err — ParseErrorKind (UnexpectedToken, UnknownVar, BadType, TypeMismatch, CircularDefine, OutOfRange) or an encoding/BddError — never panic!/unwrap on input.
  • Tradeoff: more Result plumbing; we gain a library safe to embed and fuzz.
  • Operational: unwrap/expect/array-index-without-bounds on input-derived data is a CI lint failure; panics are reserved for internal invariant violations (P1, P13) that signify a code bug.
  • Violation: a recursive DEFINE that panics on stack overflow instead of returning CircularDefine (## 15) turns a malformed model into a crash, and fuzzing (## 25) cannot distinguish "bad input handled" from "engine broken."

P8 — Counterexamples are first-class

  • Law: a verdict of "property does NOT hold" MUST be accompanied by a concrete witness: counterexample(f) -> Option<Trace>, where Trace { states: Vec<StateAssignment>, loopback: Option<usize> } is a finite prefix or a lasso (loopback = Some(i)). A FAIL with None is itself a defect.
  • Tradeoff: the checker stores onion-ring backward frontiers (## 20) instead of only the final fixpoint, costing memory; users get an actionable trace over original SMV variable names, not an opaque "false."
  • Operational: CTL safety/EG counterexamples come from onion-ring shortest-path + lasso; LTL counterexamples are fair lassos from the product (## 22); BMC counterexamples are decoded from the SAT model (## 23).
  • Violation: an engine that returns only true/false is undebuggable and fails P11 (NuSMV emits comparable counterexamples we diff against).

P9 — Variable order is everything (and is tunable)

  • Law: BDD size is exponentially sensitive to variable order; the same function can be O(n) nodes under one order and O(2^n) under another (canonical example: the order of bus/comparator bits). Order is an explicit, inspectable, tunable property of BddManager.
  • Tradeoff: good static order needs domain knowledge; dynamic sifting (## 24) costs runtime but rescues pathological blowups automatically.
  • Operational: interleave present/next variable bits (x0,x0',x1,x1',…) for a compact transition relation; expose dynamic reordering (sifting) and bdd-stats (## 25 / smv-cli) to observe node counts.
  • Violation: a poor or accidental order (e.g. all present bits, then all next bits) bloats trans from thousands to millions of nodes, and a model that should verify in seconds exhausts the node cap (P10).

P10 — Bounded resources

  • Law: BddManager enforces a configurable node-count ceiling and cache budget. Hitting the ceiling returns a typed BddError::NodeLimit (or aborts the current op with a clean error), never an uncontrolled malloc-until-OOM.
  • Tradeoff: a soluble-but-large problem may be refused; in exchange the tool degrades predictably and can suggest reordering (P9) or BMC (P12) instead of being killed by the OS.
  • Operational: GC (## 14) runs at high-water marks before declaring the limit; CLI surfaces the cap and current usage via bdd-stats.
  • Violation: unbounded growth lets a single bad order (P9) or a 200-bit model take down the host with the OOM killer mid-fixpoint, losing all progress with no diagnostic.

P11 — Differential truth: NuSMV is the oracle

  • Law: correctness is defined as agreement with NuSMV/nuXmv on a model+spec corpus: same verdict for every CTLSPEC/LTLSPEC/INVARSPEC, and comparable counterexamples. NuSMV is ground truth, not our own intuition.
  • Tradeoff: we accept an external dependency in the test harness; we gain a battle-tested reference covering decades of edge cases (fairness, nested temporal ops, vacuity).
  • Operational: Phase 13 (## 25) is the "done" gate — differential testing vs NuSMV is mandatory and blocking; divergence is a P0 bug.
  • Violation: trusting our own checker over the oracle hides a real defect (e.g. a wrong EG gfp, or an off-by-one in BMC unrolling) behind self-confirming tests.

P12 — Separate the SAT backend

  • Law: smv-bmc depends only on the SatBackend trait (solve(&CnfFormula) -> SatResult, DIMACS in / assignment out). It builds CNF via Tseitin and consumes a result; it does not implement a competitive solver (that is #277).
  • Tradeoff: a layer of indirection; in exchange the bundled DPLL/CDCL can be swapped for #277 or any DIMACS solver with zero changes to the unroller (## 23).
  • Operational: Unroller outputs CnfFormula { clauses: Vec<Vec<i32>>, nvars }; the backend is injected. Tseitin correctness is tested independently of any one solver.
  • Violation: inlining solver internals into the unroller couples BMC to one engine, blocks the #277 hook, and makes the CNF transform untestable in isolation.

P13 — Fixpoints converge or are caught

  • Law: every reachability/CTL/LTL computation is a monotone fixpoint over a finite lattice (subsets of a finite state set), so it provably terminates. lfp Z. F(Z) grows; gfp Z. F(Z) shrinks; iterate until the Bdd stops changing (Z_{i+1} == Z_i, O(1) by P1).
  • Tradeoff: none for correctness; the discipline forbids "iterate N times and hope."
  • Operational: the Phase 6 (## 18) fixpoint framework is the single shared loop reused by CTL (## 19) EU(lfp)/EG(gfp); a debug-mode iteration counter > |state-bits|+slack fires an assertion (signals a canonicity/monotonicity bug, P1).
  • Violation: a fixpoint whose step function isn't monotone (e.g. EU implemented without the q OR (p AND EX Z) envelope) oscillates and hangs; the assertion converts an infinite hang into a loud, diagnosable failure.

P14 — no_std + alloc for the engine core

  • Law: smv-bdd, smv-syntax, smv-model, smv-logic compile no_std (with alloc). Only smv-cli (the smv binary) and the test/bench harness use std.
  • Tradeoff: no std::collections::HashMap convenience in core (use alloc-based / hand-rolled tables), no file I/O below the CLI; we gain a portable, embeddable verifier core.
  • Operational: std-only deps live behind smv-cli's Cargo.toml; a CI job builds the core crates with --no-default-features to prove the boundary.
  • Violation: a stray std::println! debug line in smv-bdd breaks the no_std build and silently makes the core un-embeddable; caught only by the dedicated CI target.

P15 — One owner of mutability

  • Law: all node/table/cache mutation flows through &mut BddManager. Bdd is a copyable u32 handle, not an owning pointer. No Rc/RefCell/Arc<Mutex> node graph; no reference cycles to leak.
  • Tradeoff: ops take &mut self (single-threaded mutation; parallelism, if ever, is per-manager); in exchange we get Rust-checked aliasing, trivial GC (the manager owns every node), and zero use-after-free — the classic C-BDD bug class is eliminated by construction.
  • Operational: checkers hold &mut BddManager + &EncodedFsm (CtlChecker<'a>); handles outlive nothing the manager doesn't own; GC is safe because liveness is computed from manager-rooted references only.
  • Violation: an Rc<Node> design reintroduces cycle leaks and makes mark-sweep GC (## 14) unsound (a node freed while another Rc clone still points at it ⇒ use-after-free / double-free — exactly the failure Rust + arena indices are chosen to prevent).

6.3 Conflict resolution (when principles collide)

Tension Winner Rule
P10 bounded vs. solving a big instance P10 Refuse with NodeLimit; never OOM. Suggest sifting (P9) / BMC (P12).
P4 cache memory vs. P10 node cap P10 then P4 Live nodes are sacred; the cache is flushed first under pressure (cache misses are safe, P4).
P6 determinism vs. P9 dynamic sifting P6 within a run Sifting is deterministic given the model; "fixed order" means the order in effect, reproducibly chosen.
P8 counterexample vs. P10 limit report partial If trace reconstruction exceeds the cap, return a typed error stating the witness exists but was not materialized — never a bare FAIL (still honors P8's "no silent NO").
P1 canonicity vs. any speed hack P1 No optimization may emit a non-reduced or duplicate node, ever. Canonicity is never traded.

7. System Invariants

Properties that hold at every observable program point (between public API calls; not mid-ite recursion). Each is debug_assert!-checked in dev, audited by bdd-stats/property tests, and load-bearing for correctness. INV-1 (canonicity) is the gravest class: violations are silent — no panic, no crash — and corrupt equality, fixpoint convergence, and verdicts simultaneously.

Severity legend: GRAVE = silent wrong verdict; HIGH = wrong verdict or panic; MED = degraded perf / recoverable.

ID Invariant (one line) Sev Enforced in
INV-1 ROBDD canonical: fixed total order + reduced ⇒ handle eq iff function eq GRAVE smv-bdd unique table + ithvar order
INV-2 Unique table holds no two distinct nodes with same (level,low,high) GRAVE smv-bdd BddManager hashcons
INV-3 No live node has low==high (redundant-test elimination) GRAVE smv-bdd node-construction mk_node
INV-4 Computed cache is a pure memo: hit value == recompute value GRAVE smv-bdd ite/and_exists cache
INV-5 GC never reclaims a referenced/reachable node HIGH smv-bdd mark-sweep GC
INV-6 image/preimage quantify exactly the present/next cube; no leaked var GRAVE smv-check Img/preimage
INV-7 Every fixpoint iteration is monotone + terminates (finite lattice) HIGH smv-check lfp/gfp loops
INV-8 Spec verdict exact: holds ⇔ (init AND not sat(f)) == bdd_false GRAVE smv-check CtlChecker::check
INV-9 A NO verdict always yields a Trace that re-simulates to violate spec HIGH smv-check counterexample / smv-bmc
INV-10 BMC unrolling preserves trans relation exactly (CNF ⇔ BDD trans) HIGH smv-bmc Unroller + Tseitin
INV-11 not(f) is an involution and O(1); not(not f)==f structurally HIGH smv-bdd not (complement edge)
INV-12 satcount/node_count invariant under canonicity; eq BDDs ⇒ eq counts MED smv-bdd satcount/node_count
INV-13 Terminals fixed: bdd_false==index 0, bdd_true==index 1, level=⊤ HIGH smv-bdd init
INV-14 rename is a bijective level remap; double-rename (p→n→p) == identity HIGH smv-bdd rename + VarMap
INV-15 Encoding total + injective: distinct concrete states ⇒ distinct cubes HIGH smv-model Encoder / VarEncoding

7.1 INV-1 — ROBDD canonicity (GRAVE; the load-bearing invariant)

  • Statement. For one BddManager with a fixed total variable order, every boolean function has exactly one ROBDD; thus a == b (u32 handle compare) iff a,b denote the same function. Equality is O(1).
  • Why. All downstream correctness depends on it: fixpoint loops detect convergence by Z_{i+1} == Z_i (handle eq); spec verdict is a == bdd_false test; cache keys are handles. Break canonicity → two handles for one function → fixpoint never converges or converges to a wrong set → wrong YES/NO with no crash.
  • Where enforced. (a) Fixed order: ithvar(level) assigns levels once, monotone; reordering only via Phase 12 sifting which re-canonicalizes the whole table atomically. (b) Reduction: every node is born through one mk_node(level,low,high) chokepoint applying INV-2 + INV-3. No node is constructed by any other path.
  • Detection. bdd-stats --verify-canonical: rebuild a fresh manager from the live DAG via post-order mk_node; assert resulting handles equal originals. Property test prop_canonical: random formula built two structurally different ways (e.g. (a∧b)∨c vs De Morgan dual then re-negated) ⇒ identical handle. Truth-table oracle for ≤16 vars: enumerate satcount per assignment, two handles agree on table iff equal.
  • Telemetry. bdd_unique_nodes, bdd_collisions, bdd_canonical_violations (must stay 0). On detection: event=canonicity_violation node=N level=L low=Lo high=Hi.
  • Recovery. None at runtime — abort the verification with Err(InternalCanonicity); never emit a verdict from a corrupted manager (a wrong verdict is worse than no verdict).
  • Debugging. Bisect the op sequence (SMV_BDD_TRACE=1 logs every mk_node); export both divergent DAGs via dot; diff structurally; the first node whose (level,low,high) matches an existing entry but got a fresh index localizes the unique-table miss.

7.2 INV-2 — Unique table has no duplicate (level,low,high) (GRAVE)

  • Statement. Hashcons key (level, low, high) maps to ≤1 live node index. Construction is "lookup-or-insert".
  • Why. Duplicates are the direct mechanism by which INV-1 fails: same function, two indices.
  • Where. mk_node first probes the unique table; returns existing index on hit, allocates + inserts on miss. The only allocator of interior nodes.
  • Detection. bdd-stats scans table for collisions where bucket holds two entries with identical key (must be 0). Insert-time: assert post-insert lookup returns the just-inserted index. Property test inserts the same (l,lo,hi) twice ⇒ identical handle.
  • Telemetry. bdd_uniq_hits / bdd_uniq_misses (hit ratio drop signals a hashing/eq bug); bdd_duplicate_keys (==0).
  • Recovery. Abort; same rationale as INV-1.
  • Debugging. Suspect the hash fn or the key-eq (most bugs: hashing the complement bit inconsistently, or low/high swapped). Dump the colliding bucket; compare raw triples.

7.3 INV-3 — No live node has low==high (GRAVE)

  • Statement. Reduction rule #2: if low==high, the variable is irrelevant; mk_node returns the child, never allocates the node.
  • Why. Redundant nodes are the other canonicity-breaking mechanism: (x ? a : a) and a must be one handle.
  • Where. First line of mk_node: if low == high { return low; } — applied before the unique-table probe.
  • Detection. bdd-stats --verify-reduced: walk live DAG, assert ∀ interior node: low != high. Property test: build ite(ithvar(k), g, g) ⇒ result handle == g.
  • Telemetry. bdd_redundant_suppressed (count of suppressed redundant constructions); bdd_redundant_live (==0).
  • Recovery. Abort if a live redundant node is found (means a path bypassed mk_node).
  • Debugging. Grep for any node construction not routed through mk_node; the most common cause is a hand-built terminal/edge in a new op. Trace the offending op via SMV_BDD_TRACE.

7.4 INV-4 — Computed cache is a pure memo (GRAVE)

  • Statement. The computed cache keyed by (op, f, g, h) only accelerates; a hit returns exactly what recomputation would. Removing/clearing the cache changes performance, never results.
  • Why. A stale/aliased cache entry yields a wrong subresult that propagates into a correct-looking handle → silent wrong verdict. Cache correctness is a sub-pillar of INV-1.
  • Where. ite, and_exists, restrict, exists, rename consult + populate cache; entries are never mutated after insert; on GC, entries referencing dead nodes are purged atomically with sweep (see INV-5).
  • Detection. Test mode SMV_BDD_NOCACHE=1 disables the cache; differential test asserts identical handles cache-on vs cache-off across the whole corpus. prop_cache_purity: random op, compare cached vs forced-recompute. Audit: after GC, no cache entry references a swept index.
  • Telemetry. bdd_cache_hits/misses/evictions, bdd_cache_load_factor; spike in misses after GC is expected, a hit returning a dead index is event=cache_dangling.
  • Recovery. Flushing the cache is always safe and is the runtime mitigation (correctness preserved); a dangling-index hit is GRAVE → abort.
  • Debugging. Reproduce with NOCACHE; if the bug vanishes, the cache is the culprit — inspect key construction (forgetting op discriminant or the quantify-cube in and_exists key is the classic bug).

7.5 INV-5 — GC never collects a referenced node (HIGH)

  • Statement. Mark-sweep frees only nodes unreachable from any rooted Bdd handle. Every live Bdd and the children it transitively reaches survive.
  • Why. Freeing a referenced node → dangling u32 index → reuse → arbitrary function corruption.
  • Where. GC roots = the set of externally-held Bdd (tracked via a reference registry / explicit protect set + manager-internal temporaries on the work stack). Mark phase DFS from roots; sweep frees unmarked; cache purged of swept indices (INV-4 coupling).
  • Detection. prop_gc_safety: build N functions, hold their handles + truth tables, force GC, assert each handle still satisfies its recorded truth table and node_count unchanged. Stress: GC after every op. Assert post-sweep: live_nodes == reachable_from_roots.
  • Telemetry. bdd_gc_runs, bdd_nodes_freed, bdd_nodes_live, bdd_gc_pause_us; bdd_use_after_free (==0, instrumented by poisoning freed slots in debug).
  • Recovery. Debug build poisons freed node slots (sentinel level) so any subsequent access panics deterministically rather than corrupting silently; release aborts on sentinel read.
  • Debugging. Enable slot poisoning; the panic backtrace names the op that read a freed node, usually an unprotected temporary across a mk_node that triggered GC. Fix: protect the root before any allocating call.

7.6 INV-6 — image/preimage quantify exactly the present/next cube (GRAVE)

  • Statement. Img(S) = (exists present_cube . (trans AND S))[next→present] quantifies all and only present-variable levels; pre-image quantifies all and only next-variable levels. No model variable leaks unquantified; no auxiliary/Buchi bit is wrongly quantified.
  • Why. A leaked present variable keeps the image dependent on the source state (over-constrained → reachable set too small → spurious YES for safety). An over-quantified variable drops a constraint (under-constrained → set too large → spurious NO). Either gives a wrong verdict.
  • Where. smv-check builds the present/next cube once from EncodedFsm.present/.next VarMap; and_exists(trans, S, present_cube) then rename(.., present_map). The cube is asserted to equal the conjunction of exactly the VarMap's levels.
  • Detection. Assert cube support == VarMap level set (support(cube) == present_levels). Test: a model with a frozen variable (next(x):=x) must keep x in the image support post-rename. Differential vs NuSMV reachable-state count. prop_image_idempotent: Img over a closed set S where S is trans-invariant returns ⊆ S.
  • Telemetry. image_cube_vars, image_support_after, reachable_states (satcount), reachable_iters; mismatch logs event=cube_support_mismatch expected=… got=….
  • Recovery. Abort the reachability/CTL computation (verdict would be wrong).
  • Debugging. Print support(trans), the cube, and support(Img(S)); a present-level surviving in the image means it wasn't in the cube. Cross-check the present/next VarMap pairing emitted by Encoder.

7.7 INV-7 — fixpoint iterations are monotone and terminate (HIGH)

  • Statement. lfp loops (reachable, E[p U q]) produce a ⊆-monotone increasing chain; gfp loops (EG, Emerson-Lei) a ⊇-monotone decreasing chain. Over the finite BDD lattice (≤ 2^nvars functions) both reach a fixed point in finitely many steps; the loop halts on Z_{i+1} == Z_i (INV-1 handle eq).
  • Why. Non-monotonicity → oscillation → infinite loop or wrong fixpoint. Termination depends on monotonicity + finiteness.
  • Where. Each loop body is monotone by construction: lfp Z := Z OR step(Z) (never removes states), gfp Z := Z AND step(Z) (never adds). Convergence test is structural handle equality.
  • Detection. debug_assert! each lfp step Z_new ⊇ Z_old (i.e. Z_old AND not Z_new == bdd_false); dual for gfp. Iteration cap MAX_FIX_ITERS = 4 * nvars (generous; reaching it ⇒ bug, panic). Property test on small Kripke structures: fixpoint == brute-force set.
  • Telemetry. fix_iters, fix_kind (lfp/gfp), fix_peak_nodes, fix_converged (bool); event=monotonicity_violation iter=i.
  • Recovery. On monotonicity assert failure: abort. On iteration-cap hit: abort with diagnostic (do not return a non-fixpoint set as a verdict).
  • Debugging. Log node_count(Z_i) + satcount(Z_i) per iteration; a non-monotone step (count dips for lfp) localizes the bad step. Frequent cause: wrong polarity in EX/preimage or mixing AND/OR between lfp and gfp.

7.8 INV-8 — spec verdict is exact (GRAVE)

  • Statement. For CTL formula f, spec holds ⇔ init ⊆ sat(f), computed as the BDD emptiness check (init AND not sat(f)) == bdd_false. No sampling, no approximation; the answer is exact w.r.t. the Kripke structure.
  • Why. This is the single point where a state-set becomes a YES/NO. A subset test done by satcount comparison (instead of emptiness) would be unsound on overlapping sets; this exact formulation is mandatory.
  • Where. CtlChecker::check returns sat(f) (a Bdd); the verdict wrapper computes and(init, not(sat)) and tests == bdd_false. LTL: holds ⇔ no fair lasso reachable (INV via Emerson-Lei fair set == bdd_false intersect reachable).
  • Detection. Differential vs NuSMV verdict on the whole corpus (Phase 13 gate). Self-check: if verdict is NO, INV-9 must produce a witnessing Trace (cross-validates). Property: for f = true, holds; for f = false, fails with init as counterexample.
  • Telemetry. verdict (HOLDS/FAILS), sat_nodes, init_minus_sat_nodes, verdict_method=emptiness.
  • Recovery. None — a verdict is final; if upstream invariants fired, no verdict is emitted.
  • Debugging. When verdict disagrees with NuSMV: export sat(f), init, and init AND not sat(f) via dot; enumerate cubes of the non-empty difference — each is a concrete bad initial state, feed it to simulate to confirm against the SMV semantics.

7.9 INV-9 — a NO verdict yields a re-simulable Trace (HIGH)

  • Statement. Whenever the engine returns "spec fails", counterexample(f) returns Some(Trace) such that replaying Trace.states through the SMV semantics (a) starts in init, (b) each consecutive pair satisfies trans, (c) for a lasso (loopback = Some(i)) the loop edge satisfies trans, and (d) the trace violates f (for LTL/EG) or reaches a witness state (for EU/safety). Conversely a YES verdict returns None.
  • Why. A counterexample the user cannot reproduce is worthless and usually signals an extraction bug masking a deeper canonicity/quantification fault.
  • Where. smv-check onion-ring backward sets (shortest CTL CEX), lasso for EG/LTL fair cycle; smv-bmc SAT-model→Trace. Cube→StateAssignment via inverse VarEncoding.
  • Detection. Self-validating CEX: every emitted Trace is re-simulated in-process before return — assert (a)-(d); failure ⇒ panic (event=cex_invalid). Differential: NuSMV produces a comparable-length CEX. BMC↔BDD cross-check: if BDD says fails at depth d, BMC at k=d must be SAT.
  • Telemetry. cex_len, cex_loopback, cex_revalidated (must be true), cex_extract_method (onion/lasso/sat).
