  • Recovery. If re-simulation fails: abort the run (do not emit an invalid CEX); the YES/NO verdict itself is suspect → escalate to INV-1/INV-6 audit.
  • Debugging. The first failing step index i in re-simulation localizes the fault: bad init cube (encoding inverse), bad transition (preimage onion ring), or bad loopback (fair-set membership). Dump StateAssignment per step + the trans cofactor at the break.

7.10 INV-10 — BMC unrolling preserves the transition relation exactly (HIGH)

  • Statement. The CNF emitted by Unroller for depth k is equisatisfiable with I(s0) ∧ ⋀_{i<k} T(s_i,s_{i+1}) ∧ ⋁_{i≤k} ¬p(s_i): the Tseitin transform introduces fresh aux vars but preserves the models projected onto state variables. The CNF trans between step i,i+1 is the same relation as the BDD trans (modulo variable renaming per step).
  • Why. A unrolling that drops/duplicates a constraint reports wrong CEX existence (spurious SAT → false alarm; spurious UNSAT → missed bug).
  • Where. smv-bmc Unroller instantiates EncodedFsm.trans per step with fresh s_i variables; Tseitin CnfFormula clauses; SatBackend::solve.
  • Detection. Cross-engine equivalence (Phase 13): for bounded k, BMC SAT/UNSAT must agree with the BDD reachability "reachable within k". For ≤16 boolean state vars, enumerate all length-k paths and check the CNF's model set equals the path set (truth-table on state projection). prop_tseitin_equisat: random Expr→CNF, assert CNF SAT iff Expr BDD ≠ bdd_false.
  • Telemetry. bmc_k, cnf_vars, cnf_clauses, bmc_result (Sat/Unsat), bmc_aux_vars; event=bmc_bdd_disagree k=….
  • Recovery. Disagreement ⇒ abort + flag (both engines suspect; usually the Tseitin or per-step var renaming). No verdict.
  • Debugging. Minimize k to the smallest disagreeing depth; dump the SAT model's state projection and feed to simulate; if it violates trans, the per-step instantiation aliased two steps' variables (classic: reusing s_i indices for s_{i+1}).

7.11 Supporting invariants (INV-11 .. INV-15)

ID Detection Telemetry Recovery / Debug
INV-11 not involution, O(1) prop_not_involution: not(not f)==f (handle); not(bdd_true)==bdd_false bdd_complement_edges If not(not f)!=f: complement-edge normalization bug (canonical form requires regular-edge high child). Abort.
INV-12 counts canonicity-invariant equal BDDs ⇒ equal satcount/node_count; satcount(f)+satcount(not f)==2^nvars satcount_value, node_count_value Mismatch ⇒ canonicity (INV-1) is broken upstream; satcount is a detector for INV-1, not an independent failure.
INV-13 terminals fixed assert bdd_false==0, bdd_true==1 at manager init; bdd_true != bdd_false smoke test (Phase 0) bdd_terminal_false, bdd_terminal_true Any op returning terminal index ≥2 ⇒ allocation bug. Abort.
INV-14 rename bijective prop_rename_roundtrip: rename(rename(f,p2n),n2p)==f; support fully swapped rename_support_in/out Non-identity roundtrip ⇒ VarMap not a bijection (present/next level mispairing in Encoder).
INV-15 encoding total+injective prop_encode_injective: distinct concrete states → distinct cubes; out-of-range enum/Range value → constrained out by INV constraint encoded_bits_per_var, oob_constraint_nodes Two states sharing a cube ⇒ too few bits (ceil(log2) off-by-one) or missing out-of-range exclusion.

7.12 Invariant enforcement matrix

Invariant Compile-time debug_assert! (dev) Property/fuzz test Differential vs NuSMV Runtime telemetry (release)
INV-1 canonicity — ✓ (mk_node) ✓ prop_canonical ✓ verdict bdd_canonical_violations
INV-2 no duplicate — ✓ ✓ — bdd_duplicate_keys
INV-3 no low==high — ✓ ✓ — bdd_redundant_live
INV-4 cache pure — — ✓ NOCACHE diff ✓ cache_dangling
INV-5 GC safety partial (ownership) ✓ poison slots ✓ prop_gc_safety — bdd_use_after_free
INV-6 cube exact — ✓ support eq ✓ ✓ reachable count cube_support_mismatch
INV-7 fixpoint — ✓ monotone + cap ✓ small Kripke ✓ monotonicity_violation
INV-8 verdict exact — — ✓ true/false ✓ corpus verdict_method
INV-9 valid Trace — ✓ re-simulate ✓ ✓ CEX cex_revalidated
INV-10 BMC exact — — ✓ prop_tseitin_equisat ✓ BDD↔BMC bmc_bdd_disagree

Global rule: any GRAVE-class violation detected at runtime aborts with Err(Internal*) and emits no verdict — a missing answer is recoverable, a silent wrong answer (the canonicity failure mode) is not. All debug_assert!s compile out of release; the release safety net is the telemetry counters above, all of which must read 0 for the violation gauges, validated by the Phase 13 corpus before the "done" gate.

8. Domain Model — SMV Language, BDDs, and Temporal Logic

The domain has four interlocking models: (1) the ROBDD as a canonical representation of boolean functions over a fixed variable order; (2) the SMV modeling language that describes a finite-state machine; (3) the Kripke structure semantics that SMV denotes and that the symbolic engine manipulates as BDDs; (4) the CTL/LTL temporal logics that state properties over Kripke paths. This section pins down each model precisely — structure, invariants, grammar, semantics, encoding — and ties them together with a worked example. All names are LOCKED per the canonical spine.

8.1 The BDD model — node structure, reduction, canonicity

A BDD is a rooted DAG over a fixed total order of boolean variables, where each non-terminal node tests one variable and branches to its low child (variable = 0) and high child (variable = 1). An ROBDD is a BDD that is reduced and ordered.

Node structure (conceptual; physical layout in §10):

Field Type Meaning Invariant
level u32 index in the global variable order (0 = top, deepest = bottom) level(node) < level(low) and level(node) < level(high) — strictly increasing toward leaves
low Bdd (u32) cofactor with this var = 0 (the "else"/0-edge) points to a node of strictly greater level, or a terminal
high Bdd (u32) cofactor with this var = 1 (the "then"/1-edge) points to a node of strictly greater level, or a terminal

Terminals: two leaves, bdd_false (0) and bdd_true (1), with a sentinel level (u32::MAX) so the ordering invariant holds uniformly. Bdd(u32) is a handle = index into the manager node table; it is the only thing callers hold.

Ordering. A single global total order on variables is fixed for the manager. Every root-to-leaf path visits levels in strictly increasing order; no variable is tested twice on a path; a variable may be skipped on a path (its value is irrelevant there — that skip is the compression).

Reduction rules (the two Bryant 1986 rules; both enforced at construction by the unique table, never as a post-pass):

Rule Statement Effect
R1 — no redundant test If low == high for a node, the node is useless (the variable does not affect the function): return the child directly, never create the node. deletes redundant decision nodes
R2 — no duplicate node If a node with identical (level, low, high) already exists, return the existing handle instead of allocating a new one (hashcons via the unique table). merges isomorphic subgraphs

Canonicity invariant (load-bearing). Fixed total order + R1 + R2 ⇒ for a given boolean function there is exactly one ROBDD. Therefore:

two functions are equal  <=>  their BDD handles (u32) are equal      // O(1) equality
a function is the constant false  <=>  its handle == bdd_false       // O(1) validity/UNSAT test
a function is a tautology          <=>  its handle == bdd_true

This is THE invariant the whole engine rests on: fixpoints terminate because successive BDDs become handle-equal when the set stops changing; CTL init ⊆ sat(f) is decided by one handle comparison (init AND not sat(f)) == bdd_false. Any bug that breaks canonicity silently corrupts equality and fixpoint convergence, yielding wrong verdicts with no crash — the gravest failure class (Threat Model). Property test: build the same function by structurally different expressions; assert identical handle (Phase 1 acceptance).

ASCII picture — ROBDD of f = (a ∧ b) ∨ c with order a < b < c (levels a=0,b=1,c=2). Solid edge = high (var=1), dashed = low (var=0):

                 ┌─────┐
                 │  a  │ level 0
                 └┬───┬┘
            low(0) ┊   │ high(1)
                   ┊   │
              ┌────▼┐  └──┐
              │  c  │     │
              └┬──┬─┘     │
         low(0)┊  │high(1)│
               ┊  │       │
            ┌──▼┐ │   ┌───▼┐
            │ 0 │ │   │  b │ level 1
            └───┘ │   └┬──┬┘
                  │low ┊  │ high
                  │    ┊  │
                  └────┤  │
                       │  │
                  ┌────▼┐ │      (the c-node is SHARED: reached
                  │  c  │◄┘       from a=0 directly, and from a=1,b=0)
                  └┬──┬─┘
             low(0)┊  │high(1)
                   ▼  ▼
                 ┌──┐┌──┐
                 │0 ││1 │      level 2 terminals
                 └──┘└──┘

Reading: a=1,b=1 → 1 (independent of c); else the value is c. The single c node is shared by R2; no node has low==high by R1. (Schematic — exact child wiring depends on the chosen edge convention; with complement edges only the constant 1 terminal exists, 0 = not 1.)

ite — the Shannon expansion (the single primitive). Every BDD operation is built from ite. For the top variable x (smallest level among the operands):

ite(f, g, h) = (f ∧ g) ∨ (¬f ∧ h)
             = x · ite(f_x,  g_x,  h_x)  +  ¬x · ite(f_¬x, g_¬x, h_¬x)      // Shannon on x

where f_x is the high cofactor (restrict x=1), f_¬x the low cofactor. Recursion bottoms out at terminals; results are memoized in the computed cache keyed by the triple (f,g,h). With the cache, ite is O(|f|·|g|·|h|) nodes. Derived ops are special cases:

Op as ite
and(f,g) ite(f, g, bdd_false)
or(f,g) ite(f, bdd_true, g)
not(f) ite(f, bdd_false, bdd_true) (or O(1) complement edge)
xor(f,g) ite(f, not(g), g)
imp(f,g) ite(f, g, bdd_true)

8.2 The SMV modeling language — EBNF

A model is a single MODULE main (this engine: flat, no module instantiation — a NON-GOAL). Grammar (EBNF; terminals quoted; maps directly to SmvModule and Expr in smv-syntax):

module        ::= "MODULE" ident moduleBody ;
moduleBody    ::= { section } ;
section       ::= varSection | assignSection | defineSection
                | invarSection | transSection | spec ;

varSection    ::= "VAR" { varDecl } ;
varDecl       ::= ident ":" varType ";" ;
varType       ::= "boolean"                              (* VarType::Boolean *)
                | "{" atom { "," atom } "}"              (* VarType::Enum   *)
                | intLit ".." intLit ;                   (* VarType::Range(lo,hi) *)

assignSection ::= "ASSIGN" { assignDecl } ;
assignDecl    ::= "init" "(" ident ")" ":=" expr ";"     (* AssignKind::Init  *)
                | "next" "(" ident ")" ":=" expr ";"     (* AssignKind::Next  *)
                | ident ":=" expr ";" ;                  (* AssignKind::Plain *)

defineSection ::= "DEFINE" { defineDecl } ;
defineDecl    ::= ident ":=" expr ";" ;                  (* DefineDecl: macro, substituted *)

invarSection  ::= "INVAR"  expr ";" ;                    (* InvarDecl: state constraint *)
transSection  ::= "TRANS"  expr ";" ;                    (* TransDecl: relational constraint *)

spec          ::= "CTLSPEC"  ctlExpr ";"                 (* SpecDecl::Ctl(String)  *)
                | "LTLSPEC"  ltlExpr ";"                 (* SpecDecl::Ltl(String)  *)
                | "INVARSPEC" expr ";" ;                 (* SpecDecl::InvarSpec(Expr) -> AG expr *)

expr          ::= orExpr ;
orExpr        ::= andExpr   { ("|" | "xor") andExpr } ;
andExpr       ::= relExpr   { "&" relExpr } ;
relExpr       ::= addExpr   { ("=" | "!=" | "<" | "<=" | ">" | ">=") addExpr } ;
addExpr       ::= unaryExpr { ("+" | "-" | "mod") unaryExpr } ;
unaryExpr     ::= "!" unaryExpr | primary ;
primary       ::= ident                                  (* Expr::Var *)
                | const                                  (* Expr::Const *)
                | "next" "(" expr ")"                    (* Expr::Next — only legal in TRANS / next() RHS *)
                | caseExpr
                | "(" expr ")" ;
caseExpr      ::= "case" { expr ":" expr ";" } "esac" ;  (* Expr::Case(Vec<(guard,val)>); first-match *)

const         ::= "TRUE" | "FALSE" | intLit | atom ;     (* Value: Bool | Int | EnumAtom *)
ident, atom   ::= letter { letter | digit | "_" } ;
intLit        ::= [ "-" ] digit { digit } ;

Notes / pitfalls baked into the grammar:

  • Expr::Next (next(e)) is only meaningful inside TRANS or on the RHS of a next(x) := e assignment — it refers to the next-state value. next() nested inside next() is a parse/type error.
  • case … esac is first-match: guards evaluated top to bottom; the value of the first true guard wins. An un-exhaustive case (no guard provably true) is a model error for an assignment target; convention requires a final TRUE : …; arm.
  • ASSIGN is the functional style (init/next/plain); INVAR/TRANS are the relational style. Both compile to the same symbolic FSM (§8.6, Phase 5).
  • INVARSPEC e is sugar for the CTL spec AG e.

8.3 Kripke-structure semantics and the SMV→Kripke map

The denotation of an SMV model is a Kripke structure M = (S, S₀, R, L):

Component Definition SMV source
S — states all type-valid valuations of the declared VARs VAR section (the variable domains)
S₀ — initial states states satisfying the initial predicate init(x):=e conjuncts ∧ INVAR
R ⊆ S×S — transition relation (s,s') allowed pairs next(x):=e ∧ TRANS ∧ INVAR(s')
L : S → 2^AP labeling: which atomic props hold in s evaluation of DEFINEs / atomic relations over s

Key modeling facts:

  • A state is a complete assignment to all state variables; S is finite because every VarType is finite (boolean=2, Enum=|atoms|, Range(lo,hi)=hi−lo+1 values). |S| can be astronomically large (10^20+) — symbolic methods (BDDs) represent sets of states, never enumerate them (the core distinction from explicit-state #279).
  • R is total in NuSMV semantics (every state must have a successor) unless restricted; a model with a deadlock state (no successor) makes that branch's CTL semantics subtle — flagged as a failure mode, optionally completed by a self-loop.
  • The initial predicate init and transition relation trans are both built as BDDs (Phase 5): init: Bdd over present-state vars; trans: Bdd over present and next-state vars.
  • An atomic proposition is a boolean expression over present-state vars (e.g. pc = critical); each binds to a state-set Bdd in EncodedFsm.ap : Vec<(Atom, Bdd)>.

8.4 CTL — grammar and semantics (state formulas, branching time)

CTL formulas are state formulas (true/false at a state), interpreted over the branching tree of all paths. Each temporal operator is a path quantifier (A=all paths, E=some path) immediately followed by a path operator (X,F,G,U). Internal representation: CtlFormula (LOCKED) with primitives Ap, Not, And, Or, Ex, Eg, Eu; the rest are derived.

ctlExpr ::= "!" ctlExpr | ctlExpr ("&"|"|"|"->"|"<->") ctlExpr | "(" ctlExpr ")"
          | "EX" ctlExpr | "AX" ctlExpr | "EF" ctlExpr | "AF" ctlExpr
          | "EG" ctlExpr | "AG" ctlExpr
          | "E" "[" ctlExpr "U" ctlExpr "]" | "A" "[" ctlExpr "U" ctlExpr "]"
          | ap ;                                  (* ap = boolean state expr -> Expr -> Bdd *)

Semantics — s ⊨ φ ("state s satisfies φ"); sat(φ) = { s | s ⊨ φ } is the state-set Bdd the CtlChecker returns:

Formula Meaning Fixpoint (computed in smv-check)
EX φ some successor satisfies φ sat(EX φ) = preimage(sat φ)
EG φ some path where φ holds forever gfp Z. sat(φ) ∧ EX Z (greatest fixpoint)
E[φ U ψ] some path: φ until ψ (ψ eventually) lfp Z. sat(ψ) ∨ (sat(φ) ∧ EX Z) (least fixpoint)
EF φ E[TRUE U φ] lfp Z. sat(φ) ∨ EX Z
AX φ ¬EX ¬φ dual of EX
AG φ ¬EF ¬φ — invariant ¬(lfp Z. ¬sat(φ) ∨ EX Z)
AF φ ¬EG ¬φ dual of EG
A[φ U ψ] derived from EU + EG (NuSMV identity) ¬(E[¬ψ U (¬φ∧¬ψ)] ∨ EG¬ψ)

LFP vs GFP — why. EU/EF (reachability-style, "eventually") are least fixpoints: start from false, add states, converge upward. EG/AG-invariants ("forever") are greatest fixpoints: start from true (or sat φ), remove states, converge downward. Both iterate the preimage operator until the BDD handle stops changing (canonicity ⇒ exact termination test). Monotone operator over the finite lattice 2^S ⇒ Knaster–Tarski guarantees a unique fixpoint reached in ≤ |S| (in practice ≤ BDD-diameter) iterations.

Verdict. Spec φ HOLDS on M iff every initial state satisfies it: S₀ ⊆ sat(φ), decided by (init AND not sat(φ)) == bdd_false. Many tools intersect sat(φ) with the reachable set first (avoids spurious counterexamples in unreachable states); the engine restricts to reachable states for counterexample generation (Phase 8).

8.5 LTL — grammar and semantics (path formulas, linear time)

LTL formulas are path formulas (true/false of a single infinite path π = s₀ s₁ s₂ …). M ⊨ φ means every path from every initial state satisfies φ (implicit universal/A). Internal type: LtlFormula (LOCKED): Ap, Not, And, Or, X, U, R, F, G.

ltlExpr ::= "!" ltlExpr | ltlExpr ("&"|"|"|"->"|"<->") ltlExpr | "(" ltlExpr ")"
          | "X" ltlExpr | "F" ltlExpr | "G" ltlExpr
          | ltlExpr "U" ltlExpr | ltlExpr "R" ltlExpr
          | ap ;

Semantics — π,i ⊨ φ (path π at position i); write π ⊨ φ for π,0 ⊨ φ:

Op Reading Definition
X φ next π,i ⊨ Xφ iff π,i+1 ⊨ φ
φ U ψ until (strong: ψ must occur) ∃ j≥i. π,j ⊨ ψ ∧ ∀ i≤k<j. π,k ⊨ φ
φ R ψ release (dual of U) ∀ j≥i. π,j ⊨ ψ ∨ ∃ i≤k<j. π,k ⊨ φ
F φ eventually = TRUE U φ ∃ j≥i. π,j ⊨ φ
G φ globally = ¬F¬φ = FALSE R φ ∀ j≥i. π,j ⊨ φ

Checking strategy (Phase 9–10). M ⊨ A φ iff M has no path satisfying ¬φ. Procedure: translate ¬φ to a (generalized) Büchi automaton A_¬φ (GPVW/tableau); form the synchronous product M × A_¬φ (BDD of product states); then M ⊨ Aφ iff the product has no fair (accepting) cycle reachable from an initial state. The fair states are computed by Emerson-Lei, a nested gfp/lfp over EX and the acceptance/fairness sets; a counterexample is a lasso (finite stem + accepting cycle).

CTL vs LTL — incomparable, both kept: CTL can express branching properties (AG EF reset — "reset is always reachable") that LTL cannot; LTL can express fairness/path properties (GF req → GF ack) awkwardly or not at all in CTL. The engine implements both front ends sharing the BDD/fixpoint machinery.

8.6 Boolean encoding of finite-domain variables

smv-model's Encoder maps each finite VarType to a contiguous run of boolean BDD levels (VarEncoding { levels: Vec<u32>, ty: VarType }), and allocates a paired present and next copy (present: VarMap, next: VarMap) so the transition relation can relate s to s'. rename swaps present↔next.

VarType bits encoding extra constraint
Boolean 1 level ↔ value none
Enum(atoms) `⌈log2( atoms )⌉`
Range(lo,hi) ⌈log2(hi−lo+1)⌉ offset binary: encode (value − lo) out-of-range constraint excludes codes > hi−lo

Worked sizing: a var with 5 enum atoms → 3 bits (8 codes), 3 illegal codes excluded; a Range(0,9) (mod-10 counter) → 4 bits (16 codes), 6 illegal codes excluded. Total BDD vars = Σ bits over all vars, ×2 for present+next. Variable interleaving of present/next copies of the same variable (s, s', t, t', …) is the canonical order that keeps trans BDDs small (Phase 4/12). Arithmetic (+,-,mod,<) compiles to ripple structures of ite over the bit BDDs (Phase 4). =/!= against a constant compiles to a cube/conjunction over the bits.

EncodedFsm (LOCKED) is the handoff record:

pub struct EncodedFsm {
    pub present: VarMap, pub next: VarMap,     // present/next level maps
    pub init: Bdd,                             // initial predicate (over present)
    pub trans: Bdd,                            // transition relation (present & next)
    pub invar: Bdd,                            // state invariant (over present)
    pub fairness: Vec<Bdd>,                    // fairness state-sets (generalized Büchi)
    pub ap: Vec<(Atom, Bdd)>,                  // atomic prop -> state-set
}

8.7 Worked example — a 2-bit modulo-4 counter with reset

A small, exhaustively checkable model showing SMV source → encoding → init/trans BDDs (conceptually) → a CTL spec and its fixpoint evaluation → a counterexample. (A mutual-exclusion variant appears in §8.8's malformed cases.)

SMV source:

MODULE main
VAR
  v     : 0 .. 3;          -- Range, 2 bits  (b1 b0)
  reset : boolean;
ASSIGN
  init(v) := 0;
  next(v) := case
               reset      : 0;
               v = 3      : 0;          -- wrap-around (mod 4)
               TRUE       : v + 1;
             esac;
DEFINE
  at_max := (v = 3);
CTLSPEC AG (at_max -> AX (v = 0));      -- from max, next state is 0
CTLSPEC AG AF at_max;                   -- max is reached infinitely often
CTLSPEC AG EF (v = 0);                  -- 0 is always reachable

Encoding. v → 2 present bits {b1,b0} (offset 0, full range so no out-of-range constraint), reset → 1 bit. Next-state copies {b1',b0'}, reset'. Total: 3 present + 3 next BDD levels, interleaved b1,b1',b0,b0',reset,reset'. |S| = 4 × 2 = 8 states.

Symbolic predicates (conceptual — these are BDDs, shown as boolean formulas):

init  ≡  (b1=0) ∧ (b0=0)                                  -- v=0; reset unconstrained (free)
                                                          -- (8 states collapse: 2 satisfy init: reset∈{0,1})
trans ≡  ( reset' arbitrary )
       ∧ case-encoded:
           reset      → v'=0                              -- (b1'=0 ∧ b0'=0)
           ¬reset ∧ v=3 → v'=0
           ¬reset ∧ v≠3 → v' = v+1   (2-bit incrementer over b1,b0 → b1',b0')
at_max ≡ (b1=1) ∧ (b0=1)                                  -- the DEFINE, as a state-set Bdd

node_count(trans) here is tiny (well under 30 nodes); satcount(reachable, 3) = 8 (every counter value × every reset value is reachable).

Fixpoint evaluation of CTLSPEC AG EF (v = 0) = ¬EF¬EF(v=0):

inner = sat(EF (v=0)) = lfp Z. sat(v=0) ∨ preimage(Z)
  iter0: Z0 = false
  iter1: Z1 = {v=0}                              (4 states: v=0, reset∈{0,1}, but counted over all bits)
  iter2: Z2 = Z1 ∪ preimage(Z1) = {v=0} ∪ {v=3} ∪ {reset=1}   (predecessors of v=0)
  iter3: Z3 = Z2 ∪ preimage(Z2) = ALL states     (every state can reach v=0)
  iter4: Z4 == Z3  -> handle-equal -> FIXPOINT.  inner = TRUE (all states)
outer = sat(EF ¬inner) = sat(EF false) = false
AG EF(v=0)  holds  iff  init AND not(¬outer) == false  ->  outer=false -> sat = ¬false = TRUE
verdict: init ⊆ sat  ->  (init AND not TRUE) = (init AND false) = bdd_false  ->  HOLDS ✔

CTLSPEC AG AF at_max likewise HOLDS (AF = ¬EG¬; the counter always advances toward 3 unless reset, and even reset paths re-reach 3 — the gfp EG ¬at_max over fair behavior is empty). CTLSPEC AG (at_max -> AX (v=0)) HOLDS: from any at_max state, every successor sets v'=0 (both reset and v=3 arms of the case force 0).

Counterexample shape. Replace the spec with a FALSE one, CTLSPEC AG (v != 3) (claims the counter never reaches 3). sat(¬spec) = sat(EF (v=3)) includes init ⇒ FAILS. The CtlChecker extracts a shortest counterexample by onion-ring backward sets (Phase 8): v=0 → v=1 → v=2 → v=3, mapping each BDD cube to a readable StateAssignment:

Trace { loopback: None, states: [
  StateAssignment { v: 0, reset: 0 },     // s0  (initial)
  StateAssignment { v: 1, reset: 0 },     // s1
  StateAssignment { v: 2, reset: 0 },     // s2
  StateAssignment { v: 3, reset: 0 },     // s3  -- violates (v != 3)
]}

For an EG/AF-style witness the Trace is a lasso: loopback: Some(i) marks the start of the repeating cycle (e.g. the 0→1→2→3→0 loop witnessing AG AF at_max).

8.8 Malformed models — diagnostics

smv-syntax/smv-model reject ill-formed input with a precise ParseErrorKind (LOCKED). Each example below is minimal and shows the variant emitted:

# Malformed snippet Diagnosis ParseErrorKind
1 next(v) := w + 1; where w never declared in VAR/DEFINE reference to undeclared name UnknownVar
2 VAR p:boolean; ASSIGN next(p) := v + 1; (v:0..3) boolean target assigned an integer expr TypeMismatch
3 VAR x:0..3; INVAR x = wibble; (wibble not an enum atom / not in domain) comparing range var to an unknown/incompatible literal TypeMismatch (or UnknownVar if wibble is a bare ident)
4 `DEFINE a := b & c; b := a c;` a depends on b depends on a — cyclic macro
5 VAR v:0..3; ASSIGN init(v) := 7; 7 ∉ [0,3] OutOfRange
6 VAR s : {idle running done (missing }) enum type list not closed UnexpectedToken
7 VAR v : 3 .. 0; empty/inverted range (lo > hi) BadType
8 CTLSPEC EX EX ; (no operand) / CTLSPEC A[p]; (missing U) malformed CTL — operand or U missing UnexpectedToken (surfaced by smv-logic's CTL parser)
9 LTLSPEC G (a U ); (U missing right operand) / LTLSPEC X X malformed LTL UnexpectedToken
10 ASSIGN next(v) := next(next(v)); next() nested inside next() UnexpectedToken (or TypeMismatch — illegal next-of-next)
11 next(v) := case v = 0 : 1; esac; with v:0..3 (no TRUE: arm; not exhaustive) partial case for a functional assignment ⇒ unconstrained successor model warning → completeness error (NuSMV: leaves v' free; engine flags)
12 two assigns next(v):=0; and next(v):=1; (double-assign) variable assigned twice in the same frame TypeMismatch-class consistency error (multiple next-defs)

Diagnostic requirements: every error carries source span (line:col), the offending token/identifier, and (for TypeMismatch) the expected vs found type; for CircularDefine, the dependency cycle (a → b → a); for OutOfRange, the value and the declared bounds. These diagnostics are conformance-tested in Phase 3/4 against a fixture corpus of malformed models (one fixture per ParseErrorKind).

8.9 Domain invariants (cross-cutting, must hold everywhere)

# Invariant Why
D1 ROBDD canonicity: handle equality ⇔ function equality all equality, validity, and fixpoint-termination tests are O(1) handle compares
D2 strictly increasing level on every root→leaf path ordering precondition for R1/R2 and for restrict/exists correctness
D3 present and next VarMaps are bijective per state var; rename is an involution on them image/preimage [next→present] must round-trip
D4 S excludes out-of-range enum/range codes (constraint folded into invar) otherwise illegal valuations leak into reachable set / counterexamples
D5 a CTL spec φ holds ⇔ (init AND not sat(φ)) == bdd_false the single verdict rule; no per-state enumeration
D6 LTL: M ⊨ Aφ ⇔ no fair cycle in M × A_¬φ reachable from init Büchi-emptiness = fair-cycle-emptiness (Emerson-Lei)
D7 every fixpoint operator is monotone over 2^S Knaster–Tarski ⇒ unique lfp/gfp, terminates in ≤ `

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

Architecture Decision Records. Each row is LOCKED for this blueprint. "ADR #" is stable across revisions; supersede, never renumber. Rationale columns are telegraphic ("X -> Y because Z"). Every choice is load-bearing on either correctness (canonicity, verdict soundness) or performance (ite/apply hotpath, intermediate BDD blowup). Cross-references to phases (## 12..## 25), crates (smv-*), and core types are exact.

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust, edition 2021, MSRV 1.78 C/C++; Python; Java; Haskell; OCaml BDD package = perf-critical, pointer/hash-table-heavy; need memory safety + allocation/cache-layout control. C/C++: manual unique-table memory mgmt is the canonical BDD bug source (use-after-free on GC'd nodes, double-free, dangling node handles) -> rejected. Python/Java: ite/apply is the inner loop -> GC pauses + boxing -> 10-100x slowdown on 10^6-node BDDs -> rejected. Haskell: laziness fights the strict mutable unique-table + computed-cache discipline a BDD pkg requires -> rejected. OCaml: viable but weaker control over inline layout + no fearless-concurrency story for future reorder. Rust enums + exhaustive match model the SMV AST + CTL/LTL formulas directly; index-arena handles map onto ownership and kill cyclic-Rc UAF. C/C++ wins when maximum portability to legacy hardware-verification toolchains is mandatory (e.g., CUDD plugin ecosystem, existing Verilog harnesses calling into a C library), or when the team already owns a heavily-audited C ROBDD reference implementation and rewriting it in Rust costs more than defending the existing memory-safety audit. Python/Java wins when the state space is small (≤10^6 states, <10^4 BDD nodes) and rapid scripting dominates correctness engineering. OCaml wins for a pure research prototype in a lab already using OCaml's BDD bindings (Mlbdd) where proof-assistant interop matters.
ADR-02 Repository structure Single Cargo workspace, seven members: smv-bdd, smv-syntax, smv-model, smv-logic, smv-check, smv-bmc, smv-cli Monolithic single crate; one mega-crate + feature flags; many independent published crates Layered DAG (bdd <- syntax <- model <- logic <- check <- bmc <- cli) enforces dependency direction at compile time; a model cannot reach into cli. Monolith -> no firewall between the BDD engine and the SMV front end, slow incremental builds, harder fuzz isolation. Feature-flag mega-crate -> combinatorial cfg matrix, brittle #[cfg] soup. Many published crates -> premature versioning overhead for a single-owner project. Workspace = one lockfile, shared target/, per-crate test/fuzz/bench isolation. smv-bdd reusable standalone (no SMV deps). Many independent published crates wins when smv-bdd is intended as a widely-adopted community ROBDD library (like petgraph or bitvec), with separate semver cadence from the checker. Monolithic single crate wins for a tiny research throwaway where compile-time is irrelevant and the workspace overhead costs more than it saves. Feature-flag mega-crate wins for a firmware-style embedded consumer that must tree-shake to a single .rlib with a custom feature matrix.
ADR-03 BDD node representation Index/arena: pub struct Bdd(u32) handle into BddManager node table; nodes are SoA/AoS in Vec Rc<Node> / Box pointer graph; Arc<Node> shared; raw *mut Node Pointer graph -> Rc cannot represent the shared-subgraph DAG without leaks unless cycles are impossible (they are, but ref-count churn on every ite is the dominant cost in C++ pointer BDDs); GC of dead nodes needs explicit mark-sweep anyway. u32 index: 4-byte handle (vs 8-byte ptr) halves cache footprint of the unique table + cache; arena gives O(1) mark-sweep (## 14) without traversing pointers; no UAF (a stale Bdd(u32) is at worst a logic bug, never memory unsafety). u32 caps at 4.29e9 nodes (>> any tractable BDD; blowup hits memory wall first). *mut -> unsafe, rejected for soundness. Rc<Node> pointer graph wins in a purely functional setting where structural sharing is achieved by persistent data structures (no GC needed), the BDD stays small (< 10^5 nodes), and Rust borrowing semantics make the arena lifetime awkward — e.g., an interactive theorem-prover assistant that incrementally builds and discards BDD terms. Arc<Node> wins if concurrent read-only BDD evaluation from multiple threads is the dominant pattern and the working set fits in cache (e.g., parallel property simulation). u64 index (vs u32) wins for future designs targeting >4 billion-node BDDs, where the u32 cap would be hit before RAM.
ADR-04 Edge negation scheme Complement edges: high bit of the u32 child slot tags negation; not(f) is O(1) edge flip No complement edges (explicit not-node); separate Bdd + sign field Complement edges halve node count (a function + its negation share one node) and make not O(1) (ADR core-type contract: not(&self, f) -> Bdd is non-&mut). Cost: a canonicity side-rule — forbid a complement on the high (then) edge; push complements to the low edge + terminal so each function has exactly one representation. No complement edges -> not allocates + caches an extra node, doubles unique-table pressure -> rejected. Separate sign field -> breaks O(1) structural-equality handle test (ADR-09). Terminal convention: single bdd_true; bdd_false = not(bdd_true) via complemented edge to the same terminal. No complement edges wins when implementation simplicity is paramount (single-maintainer tutorial BDD package, or a student implementing ROBDD for the first time): removing the complement-edge canonicity side-rule eliminates one of the most common implementation bugs (the "high edge must not be complemented" invariant). The node count doubles for negation-heavy workloads, but for small educational models this is irrelevant. Separate sign field wins in a design where the Bdd handle escapes the manager boundary as a serializable key, and the sign must be independently transmitted or stored (e.g., a distributed BDD cache).
ADR-05 Unique table (hashcons) Open-addressing hash table keyed on (level, low, high), Robin-Hood probing, power-of-two capacity, load factor <= 0.70, grow x2 + rehash std::collections::HashMap; chaining with per-bucket Vec; separate-chaining intrusive linked nodes Unique table enforces canonicity at construction: before inserting a node, look up (level,low,high); if present, return the existing Bdd (merge isomorphic subgraphs); reduction rule "delete node whose low==high" checked before insert. Open addressing -> nodes live inline in the arena, hash slots store u32 indices -> 1 cache line per probe, no pointer chase. std::HashMap -> owns boxed entries, can't co-locate with arena, no control over rehash during GC. Chaining Vec/linked -> pointer indirection per bucket defeats the cache-locality goal. Robin-Hood bounds probe variance; tombstone-free (GC rebuilds table, ADR-06). std::collections::HashMap wins in a prototype or correctness-first research implementation where "just use HashMap" lets you ship in hours; the 20–40% throughput loss on the unique-table hot path is acceptable when correctness is the only metric and the state space is small. Chaining with per-bucket Vec wins when the key distribution is adversarial (e.g., cryptographic hashing of formula structure is expensive and Robin-Hood probe sequences are pathological for particular model families).
ADR-06 Computed (op) cache: structure + eviction Direct-mapped lossy cache keyed on (op_tag, f, g, h); fixed power-of-two size; collision = overwrite (no chaining); cleared/rehashed on GC LRU cache; perfectly-associative cache; unbounded memoization HashMap; no cache The computed cache memoizes ite/apply results -> turns ite from exponential into O(|f||g||h|). It is a correctness-neutral accelerator (a miss just recomputes the canonical result) -> a lossy direct-mapped table is sound: on collision, overwrite the old entry, no eviction bookkeeping. LRU -> per-access metadata + eviction logic that buys little for BDD workloads (temporal locality is already high) -> rejected. Unbounded HashMap -> grows without bound, OOMs on large fixpoints, holds dead-node entries alive -> rejected. Direct-mapped -> 1 probe, 1 cache line. GC (## 14) must purge stale entries referencing collected nodes: simplest sound policy = clear the whole computed cache on every mark-sweep. Cache-tuning (size, hash mix) deferred to ## 24. LRU cache wins when the BDD computation exhibits strong temporal locality skew — e.g., property-checking a dense formula where a small "hot" subgraph of BDD nodes is repeatedly queried across many image steps and LRU's eviction correctly retains them longer than a direct-mapped collision would. This regime arises in model-checking large sequential circuits with repetitive functional blocks. Unbounded HashMap wins for a single-run offline analysis tool with ample RAM (e.g., a one-shot circuit equivalence checker on a machine with 1 TB RAM), where the run terminates before the map exhausts memory and total cache efficiency is more important than per-op memory bound.
ADR-07 Boolean-function primitive Single primitive ite(f,g,h); and/or/xor/imp/not are thin wrappers over ite Per-operator apply(op, f, g) (Bryant's APPLY with an op-table); duplicate hand-written recursion per op ite(f,g,h) = (f AND g) OR (not f AND h) expresses every binary Boolean op + multiplexing; one memoized recursive routine -> one place to get canonicity + termination right, one cache key family (op_tag, f, g, h). Per-op apply -> N near-identical recursions, N chances for a reduction bug, fragmented cache (lower hit rate). Wrappers: and(f,g)=ite(f,g,false), or(f,g)=ite(f,true,g), xor(f,g)=ite(f,not g,g), imp(f,g)=ite(f,g,true) — each is <=1 line, no recursion. Standard-triple normalization of ite arguments (canonical operand order, complement pushing) raises cache hit rate; implemented in ## 13. Per-operator apply table wins when the primary workload is a fixed small set of operations (only and/or at high volume, no multiplexing) and the operation dispatch overhead of the ite ternary form adds measurable cache pressure on the key hashing. In practice, specialized and_apply and or_apply with per-op cache slots can achieve slightly higher hit rates by reducing key space. This trade-off appears in hardware synthesizers that only need conjunction/disjunction over BDDs.
ADR-08 Variable-ordering policy Static interleaved present/next ordering by default (x0 x0' x1 x1' ...); dynamic reordering = sifting, opt-in via bdd-stats/check flags Fixed declaration order, no reordering; force/FORCE static heuristic only; window permutation only; always-on auto-reorder Variable order can swing BDD size between linear and exponential. Interleaving present x with its next-copy x' keeps the transition relation trans compact (related bits adjacent) — the standard NuSMV/CUDD default. Sifting (## 24): move one variable to its locally optimal level by sliding it through all positions, keep best; bounded-cost dynamic reorder. Always-on auto-reorder -> nondeterministic timing + reorder thrash mid-fixpoint -> rejected as default (opt-in only). Window-only -> too local. Reordering must preserve the canonicity invariant: it rebuilds the unique table under the new order; the function each Bdd denotes is unchanged though u32 handles are remapped (caller must re-fetch roots). Always-on auto-reorder wins in an interactive reasoning environment where the user triggers many ad-hoc queries (e.g., an interactive hardware debugger or an exploratory formal-analysis session) and reproducibility is less important than consistently small BDDs; the nondeterminism cost is paid for by avoiding the BDD-blowup that fixed order would hit on unanticipated formulas. Fixed declaration order, no reordering wins for a fully deterministic, reproducible regression gate (CI/automated regression suite) where bit-identical node counts across runs are required for diff-based monitoring and the model family is known to be well-ordered by declaration order (e.g., structured register-transfer-level descriptions).
ADR-09 Equality / canonicity contract Canonicity = fixed total var order + two reduction rules; structural handle equality Bdd == Bdd iff Boolean-function equality; O(1) Hash-based function equality; explicit equiv(f,g) BDD walk; SAT-check equivalence The load-bearing invariant. Reduction rules: (R1) merge isomorphic subgraphs (the unique table, ADR-05), (R2) delete any node with low == high (redundant test). Fixed order + R1 + R2 -> a unique canonical DAG per Boolean function -> f == g is a u32 comparison, fixpoint convergence is "the root handle stopped changing". Hash-equality -> collisions give false positives -> silently wrong verdicts. Explicit walk/SAT -> O(|f|+|g|) or NP per test, defeats the whole BDD value proposition. GRAVEST failure class (## 26 threat model): any bug breaking canonicity corrupts equality + convergence with NO crash -> property test in every phase (## 13 canonicity test, ## 25 fuzz: apply vs truth table). Explicit equiv(f,g) BDD walk wins when the engine is not a single-manager ROBDD but a multi-manager or distributed BDD system where handles from different managers cannot be compared by index (e.g., a cloud-distributed model checker that ships sub-BDDs between nodes). In that regime, a structural xor(f,g) == bdd_false walk is the correct equivalence test. SAT-check equivalence wins for equivalence checking of arbitrary Boolean circuits where BDD construction itself may blow up (e.g., multiplier equivalence), and the SAT query replaces BDD construction entirely.
ADR-10 Scalar/enum variable encoding Binary boolean encoding: a VarType::Enum/Range of cardinality n uses ceil(log2(n)) BDD levels + an out-of-range constraint BDD One-hot (n bits, exactly-one constraint); unary/thermometer; no encoding (explicit-state) Encoder (## 16) maps finite-domain vars to boolean BDD levels (VarEncoding { levels, ty }). Log2 encoding minimizes bit count -> fewer BDD levels -> smaller init/trans. For non-power-of-two cardinality, 2^k - n codepoints are spurious -> add an out-of-range constraint conjoined into invar (and next-invar in trans) so unreachable codes can't satisfy specs. One-hot -> n bits (vs log2 n) + an exactly-one BDD; blows up state-bit count -> rejected (except where a future reorder heuristic prefers it; not now). Explicit-state enumeration is #279, a NON-GOAL: this engine represents state sets as BDDs, never enumerates states. One-hot encoding wins when the number of enum values n is small (≤ 8) and the transitions are dominated by exact-match predicates (state == IDLE, state == RUNNING), because the one-hot BDDs for these predicates are single-variable projections (O(1) BDD size) vs. a log2-encoded conjunction over bits. For small enums with many property checks, the sparser one-hot representation can produce smaller trans BDDs than the bit-compact log2 encoding. Unary/thermometer encoding wins for Range variables where ordering predicates (counter > 5) dominate, since each threshold becomes a single literal in the thermometer code.
ADR-11 Transition-relation representation Conjunctively partitioned trans: a Vec<Bdd> of partition conjuncts (per next-var cluster) with an early-quantification schedule; monolithic trans: Bdd materialized only when small Always-monolithic single trans BDD; disjunctive partitioning only; no partitioning Monolithic trans = AND of all next-var constraints is often the largest BDD in the system and can blow up before any image is taken. Conjunctive partitioning (## 17, ## 24) keeps trans as conjuncts T_1..T_m; image Img(S) quantifies present vars early — interleave and_exists with each conjunct so a variable is existentially removed as soon as no remaining conjunct mentions it -> intermediate BDDs stay small. EncodedFsm.trans is the conceptual relation; the partition Vec<Bdd> + quantification schedule are the operational form. Disjunctive partitioning suits asynchronous/interleaving models -> offered as an option in ## 24, not the default. No partitioning -> OOM on medium models -> rejected. Disjunctive partitioning wins for asynchronous / interleaving models (Petri nets, multi-process SMV with `
ADR-12 Image / pre-image operator and_exists(f, g, cube) (relational product) as a fused primitive, plus rename(f, &VarMap) for present<->next swap Separate and then exists (two passes); BDD-of-relations matrix multiply; explicit substitution Forward image Img(S) = (exists present.(trans AND S))[next->present]; the and immediately followed by exists over the same cube is the dominant cost. Fusing into and_exists (## 14) quantifies during the conjunction recursion -> never materializes the full trans AND S before quantifying -> the early-quantification win (ADR-11) is only realizable with a fused operator. rename swaps next-var levels to present via a VarMap to close the fixpoint. Two-pass and then exists -> builds the (large) conjunction in full first -> defeats the purpose. Pre-image is the dual (and_exists quantifying next); both share the one and_exists routine. Separate and then exists wins when the transition relation trans is reused for many independent image computations over different state sets S, and the intermediate trans AND S can be cached and reused across multiple iterations (e.g., a batch-mode CTL checker that evaluates dozens of properties simultaneously on the same model and caches and(trans, S_i) once per unique S_i). The unfused approach pays the materialization cost once and amortizes it across queries. BDD-of-relations matrix multiply wins for graph reachability engines (e.g., BDD-based transitive closure computation on large relation databases) rather than FSM model checking.
ADR-13 CTL model-checking engine Symbolic BDD fixpoint iteration: CtlChecker::check(f) -> Bdd returns the SAT state-set; EX p = preimage(p), E[p U q] = lfp Z. q OR (p AND EX Z), EG p = gfp Z. p AND EX Z; derive EF/AG/AF/AX/AU Explicit-state CTL labelling (Clarke-Emerson-Sistla over enumerated states); tableau; automata-theoretic CTL The whole point of symbolic MC (Burch/Clarke/McMillan/Dill 1992 — 10^20 states): operate on state sets (BDDs), iterate fixpoints to convergence detected by O(1) handle equality. EU = least fixpoint (start false, grow); EG = greatest fixpoint (start reachable/true, shrink); both reuse the ## 18 fixpoint-iteration framework and preimage (ADR-12). Spec f HOLDS iff init subset sat(f), i.e. (init AND not sat(f)) == bdd_false. Explicit labelling -> enumerates states (#279 territory, NON-GOAL) -> can't reach 10^20. Only the 3 primitives EX/EU/EG (+ Not/And/Or) are evaluated directly; the rest are rewritten (AF p = not EG not p, EF p = E[true U p], AG p = not EF not p, AX p = not EX not p). Explicit-state CTL labelling (Clarke-Emerson-Sistla 1986 algorithm over the reachable state graph) wins for small models (≤ 10^6 states) where building the full explicit state graph is feasible and the overhead of BDD construction exceeds the savings — specifically, a protocol analyzer validating a hand-crafted 4-process dining-philosophers model for teaching purposes. It also wins when the BDD variable ordering is pathological and the symbolic engine hits the node cap, making explicit enumeration the only available path.
ADR-14 LTL-to-Buchi translation GPVW (Gerth-Peled-Vardi-Wolper) tableau construction of a generalized Buchi automaton from the NNF LtlFormula; VWAA as a documented fallback Direct LTL symbolic MC (no automaton); Safra determinization; explicit Buchi via Wring/SPOT-style optimizations only LTL MC (## 21, ## 22): A phi holds iff the product of the model with the Buchi automaton for not phi has NO reachable fair cycle. GPVW = the classic on-the-fly tableau, directly produces a generalized Buchi automaton (multiple acceptance sets) that maps cleanly onto symbolic encoding (state bits + transition Bdd) and onto Emerson-Lei's multi-fairness fixpoint (ADR-15). GPVW is simpler to implement + verify than Safra and avoids full determinization (LTL MC needs only nondeterministic Buchi). VWAA (very-weak alternating automata) noted as a smaller-output alternative for ## 24 optimization, not the baseline. Direct symbolic LTL -> no clean fair-cycle formulation -> rejected. Safra determinization wins when the downstream synthesis (not just verification) requires a deterministic omega-automaton — e.g., if this engine were extended to reactive synthesis (computing a controller strategy), where the nondeterministic Buchi from GPVW is insufficient and a deterministic Rabin automaton is needed. Wring/SPOT-style explicit Buchi wins when the LTL formula is fixed across many models (e.g., a standard property library), the automaton is pre-compiled once off the critical path, and the automaton size matters more than on-the-fly symbolic encoding.
ADR-15 Fair-cycle detection Emerson-Lei greatest-fixpoint algorithm over EX and the fairness/acceptance sets; counterexample = fair lasso Nested-DFS (explicit Courcoubetis-Vardi-Wolper-Yannakakis); SCC enumeration (Tarjan); one-fairness-set special case only Emerson-Lei (1986) computes the set of states on a fair cycle as a nested gfp/lfp purely via EX/preimage and the fairness sets EncodedFsm.fairness: Vec<Bdd> -> stays symbolic (operates on state sets), handles generalized Buchi (the multiple acceptance sets GPVW emits, ADR-14) uniformly. Nested-DFS / Tarjan -> explicit per-state traversal (#279 NON-GOAL) -> can't scale symbolically. Single-fairness special case -> can't represent generalized Buchi without preprocessing. The fair lasso (a reachable fair cycle) is rendered as a Trace { states, loopback: Some(i) } (ADR-17). Reused for Buchi/Streett fairness in ## 24. Nested-DFS (Courcoubetis-Vardi-Wolper-Yannakakis) wins for an explicit-state model checker (like SPIN/Promela) where the state space is small enough to enumerate and the on-the-fly DFS can terminate as soon as a single fair cycle is found — avoiding the full fixpoint iteration cost of Emerson-Lei that computes all fair states. This is the regime of #279 (not this project). SCC enumeration (Tarjan) wins if one needs the full set of SCCs for analysis purposes beyond fair-cycle detection (e.g., computing the set of all recurrent SCCs for quantitative analysis).
ADR-16 SAT integration for BMC SatBackend trait (DIMACS-CnfFormula in, SatResult out) decoupling the solver; a bundled simple DPLL/CDCL plug-in by default; #277 hookable Re-implement a competitive SAT solver inside smv-bmc; hard-link one external solver crate; FFI to MiniSat/CaDiCaL A standalone competitive SAT core is project #277 — a NON-GOAL here. smv-bmc (## 23) consumes a CNF/DIMACS solver via trait SatBackend { fn solve(&mut self, cnf: &CnfFormula) -> SatResult } so #277 (or any external solver) plugs in without touching the unroller. A bundled minimal DPLL/CDCL ships so the crate is self-contained + testable in isolation; it is explicitly NOT tuned to compete. Hard-linking one solver -> couples BMC to a dependency + licensing; FFI -> build complexity, unsafe boundary. SatResult::Sat(Vec<bool>) -> decoded back to a counterexample Trace; Unsat at depth k -> no length-k counterexample. FFI to MiniSat/CaDiCaL wins in a production deployment where the BMC performance is dominated by the SAT solver and the best known industrial solver (CaDiCaL) would provide 5-10× speedup on real industrial circuits, and the project is already using other C/C++ FFI (e.g., for NuSMV oracle integration), so adding one more FFI boundary is low marginal cost. Hard-linking one external solver crate wins if the project evolves past prototyping and the SatBackend trait abstraction is no longer needed (only one solver will ever be used, simplifying the dependency graph).
ADR-17 CNF generation for BMC Tseitin transform (definitional CNF, fresh vars per gate) over the k-step unrolling I(s0) AND conj T(si,si+1) AND not-prop_k Naive distribute-to-CNF (exponential blowup); Plaisted-Greenbaum polarity-optimized only; And-Inverter-Graph then sweep Unroller (## 23) builds I(s0) AND conj_{i<k} T(s_i, s_{i+1}) AND or_{i<=k} not p(s_i) as a CnfFormula { clauses: Vec<Vec<i32>>, nvars }. Tseitin introduces one fresh variable per subformula/gate -> CNF size linear in formula size (vs exponential for naive distribution) at the cost of equisatisfiable (not equivalent) — fine, BMC only needs satisfiability. DIMACS i32 literals (sign = polarity, magnitude = var id) match the SatBackend contract directly. Plaisted-Greenbaum (polarity-aware, fewer clauses) is a ## 24 optimization layered on Tseitin, not the baseline. AIG+sweep -> extra infra (#277 territory). Completeness: safety checking complete once k reaches the reachability/recurrence diameter (completeness threshold, ## 24). Plaisted-Greenbaum wins when the clause budget is severely constrained (e.g., an embedded solver with limited memory) and the formula is predominantly positive (not negated), so the polarity-aware half-Tseitin reduction achieves 20–30% fewer clauses at negligible computational cost. In practice this optimization is a strict improvement over vanilla Tseitin when the solver is clause-count-sensitive (some CDCL solvers scale with clause count). AIG + sweep wins in a setting that already computes an And-Inverter-Graph (e.g., formal equivalence checking of RTL circuits) and can re-use the AIG as a circuit for BMC unrolling without re-encoding to BDDs or CNF first, making Tseitin the final step of an already AIG-centric pipeline.
ADR-18 Counterexample / witness representation Trace { states: Vec<StateAssignment>, loopback: Option<usize> } (a lasso); BDD cubes mapped back to readable StateAssignment over original SMV vars Raw BDD-cube sequence (no decode); per-logic ad-hoc structures; only a boolean verdict, no trace One uniform Trace for CTL counterexamples/witnesses (## 20), LTL fair lassos (## 22), and BMC SAT models (## 23). loopback: Some(i) encodes the lasso stem+cycle (LTL/EG witnesses are infinite -> a finite lasso); None = finite safety counterexample (BMC, EF/AG). StateAssignment decodes BDD cubes back through VarEncoding to original VAR names + enum/range values -> human-readable, diff-able against NuSMV output (ADR-19). Onion-ring backward sets (## 20) yield shortest CTL counterexamples. Raw cubes -> unreadable, can't diff vs oracle. Verdict-only -> useless for debugging the model. Raw BDD-cube sequence wins in an embedded library scenario where the caller has its own domain-specific decoding layer and the overhead of StateAssignment allocation for each step is non-negligible (e.g., a high-throughput automated bug-finding loop that discards counterexamples after a quick constraint projection, never rendering them for human inspection). Verdict-only wins for a CI gate that only needs a PASS/FAIL boolean per spec and counterexample details are never needed (maximum throughput per model check, discarding trace extraction cost).
ADR-19 no_std + alloc for the core smv-bdd, smv-syntax, smv-logic are #![no_std] + extern crate alloc; smv-check/smv-bmc no_std+alloc; only smv-cli is std Everything std; everything no_std incl. CLI; #![no_std] with a custom allocator The engine core touches no OS facilities — only heap (Vec, BTreeMap, String) -> no_std + alloc keeps it portable (embeddable, WASM, kernel-adjacent harnesses) and forces a clean separation: no accidental println!/std::time/std::fs in the verifier hotpath. smv-cli is the single std boundary (argv, file IO, stdout, dot export to disk). All-std -> hidden platform deps creep into the engine. All-no_std incl. CLI -> reinvent argument parsing + IO for no gain. Custom allocator -> out of scope; rely on the global allocator. Everything std wins when the project is a single-user desktop tool never intended for embedded or WASM deployment, and the team wants to use std::time, std::fs, std::thread freely throughout for profiling, logging, and parallel image computation — the portability benefit of no_std is irrelevant and the discipline overhead (replacing std::HashMap with manual alloc-based alternatives) is pure cost. Custom allocator wins in a safety-critical firmware context (automotive, aerospace) where the OS allocator is not certified and the BDD arena must use a statically-allocated pool with bounded worst-case allocation time (e.g., a WCET-bounded verifier for real-time software).
ADR-20 Error handling discipline Result<T, E> everywhere in library crates; typed errors (ParseError/ParseErrorKind, encoder/check errors); NO panic!/unwrap/expect on user-reachable paths; panics only for broken internal invariants panic! on bad input; Box<dyn Error> everywhere; anyhow in library crates; Option to signal errors A verifier must never crash on a malformed model — it must diagnose. smv-syntax returns ParseError { kind: ParseErrorKind, .. } with UnexpectedToken/UnknownVar/BadType/TypeMismatch/CircularDefine/OutOfRange. Library crates expose concrete error enums (no anyhow/Box<dyn Error> — callers must pattern-match, and no_std precludes std::error::Error). panic! is reserved for impossible states that signal an engine bug (e.g. canonicity violation caught by a debug assert) — a panic there is correct (fail loud, never emit a wrong verdict). unwrap on user paths banned by CI lint (clippy::unwrap_used deny in libs). smv-cli may use anyhow at the top level for ergonomics. anyhow in library crates wins for a rapid-prototyping or internal-tooling context where the library will never be embedded in a no_std environment and the caller does not need to programmatically distinguish error kinds (only needs a human-readable message). In a single-team internal prototype where exact error variant matching is never written, anyhow's ergonomics outweigh its type-erasure cost. Box<dyn Error> everywhere wins in the same scenario when no_std portability is required but anyhow is too heavyweight, and all callers only want a Display representation of the error for logging.
ADR-21 Differential-testing oracle NuSMV / nuXmv as the golden oracle: same verdict + comparable counterexample on a model+spec corpus Hand-written expected outputs only; cross-check against own re-implementation; SPIN (LTL only, explicit-state) NuSMV is the canonical SMV reference (matching language + symbolic CTL/LTL) -> the highest-value oracle for catching silent wrong-verdict bugs (ADR-09 gravest class). ## 25 validation: run the corpus through both, assert identical SAT/UNSAT verdicts and counterexample compatibility (NuSMV traces -> compare lasso structure + reachable prefix; exact trace may differ, the verdict must not). Hand-written expecteds alone -> low coverage, author-biased. Self-cross-check -> tests the engine against its own bugs. SPIN -> explicit-state + LTL-only + Promela (not SMV) -> wrong language + can't cover CTL -> rejected as primary oracle (usable as a secondary LTL sanity check). Hand-written expected outputs wins for a pure-research BDD-only engine that targets a novel logic or property language not supported by any reference tool, where no external oracle exists and the expected outputs are derived from first-principles proof and paper reviews. This is the only valid option when the language is novel (e.g., a custom μ-calculus fragment with no existing reference checker). SPIN as secondary LTL oracle wins for augmenting the LTL corpus when NuSMV is unavailable (licensing, build environment) and the model family is small enough for SPIN's explicit-state enumeration (≤ 10^7 states) — its LTL semantics match the Pnueli/Baier-Katoen specification exactly.
ADR-22 Fuzzing harness cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) targets: (a) SMV parser (no panic, no UB on arbitrary bytes), (b) BDD apply vs brute-force truth table, (c) canonicity (round-trip build -> equal functions share a handle) No fuzzing; AFL only; property tests (proptest) only; manual corpus only The canonicity invariant + parser robustness are exactly what coverage-guided fuzzing excels at. Target (b)/(c) directly attack the gravest failure class (ADR-09): generate random Boolean functions over few vars, build via ite/apply, compare to an exhaustive truth-table evaluation AND assert that two syntactically different constructions of the same function yield Bdd == Bdd. proptest (## 25) covers shrinking + structured generators; cargo-fuzz covers raw-byte/coverage-guided depth — complementary, both kept. AFL -> heavier setup, redundant with libFuzzer here. Parser fuzz seeds from the NuSMV corpus + grammar-aware generators. AFL (American Fuzzy Lop) wins when the project runs on Linux in a CI environment where cargo-fuzz/libFuzzer is unavailable (e.g., RHEL 7 with a pinned old clang), or when the team already has a mature AFL corpus and infrastructure from another project and wants to reuse it. AFL's persistent-mode can achieve comparable throughput to libFuzzer for byte-sequence inputs. proptest only wins for a team that values shrinkable, human-readable failing examples over raw coverage, and where the fuzz targets are small-domain (≤ 8 variables) exhaustively checkable — proptest can explore the same space deterministically without a fuzzer.
ADR-23 Benchmarking harness criterion for statistically-rigorous microbenchmarks (ite/apply throughput, image step, fixpoint iteration) + a macro corpus timing harness in smv-cli bdd-stats std::time ad-hoc timers; #[bench] (nightly-only); no benchmarking Performance targets (## 25) are first-class: ite/apply throughput, BDD node counts, reachable-state counts, fixpoint wall-time vs targets. criterion gives confidence intervals, regression detection across runs, warmup/outlier handling -> guards against silent perf regressions from a reorder/cache change (## 24). #[bench] -> nightly-only, fragile, no statistics. Ad-hoc std::time -> noisy, no regression gate. Macro benchmarks (whole model+spec timing) run via smv-cli subcommands + the bdd-stats reporter (node counts, cache hit rate, GC stats). #[bench] nightly wins in a nightly-CI environment where the benchmark suite is a simple "did this break?" gate, not a statistical regression detector, and the team accepts nightly toolchain lock-in. The nightly compiler's test::Bencher requires zero additional dependencies and is suitable for coarse-grained timing. No benchmarking / manual timing wins for a pure research prototype where BDD performance is not a deliverable and the team's iteration loop is too fast for benchmark overhead to be justified.
ADR-24 Continuous integration GitHub Actions: matrix over {stable, MSRV 1.78} x {Linux, macOS}; jobs = build, fmt --check, clippy -D warnings, test, no_std build check, cargo-fuzz smoke (bounded), differential corpus subset No CI; CI on default toolchain only; Linux-only; manual pre-commit only CI is the enforcement point for every other ADR: clippy -D warnings enforces ADR-20 (unwrap_used deny in libs); a dedicated no_std target (thumbv7em-none-eabi build) enforces ADR-19 (catches accidental std leakage in core crates); MSRV-pinned job enforces ADR-25; a bounded fuzz-smoke + differential-corpus subset run per PR catches canonicity/verdict regressions early (full fuzz/corpus on nightly cron). macOS in the matrix because the owner develops on Darwin (catches platform-specific UB). Default-toolchain-only -> MSRV drift undetected. Linux-only CI wins for an internal enterprise deployment behind a firewall where macOS GitHub Actions runners are not available or cost-prohibited, and the project is only ever deployed on Linux servers. The platform-specific behavior difference for this workload (BDD arithmetic, no OS-specific I/O) is negligible. Manual pre-commit only wins for a solo research prototype in a short-lived academic sprint (weeks, not months) where CI setup time dominates the project lifetime and the researcher manually runs the test suite before every submission.
ADR-25 Minimum supported Rust version MSRV 1.78, pinned in rust-toolchain.toml + the CI matrix; bumps are deliberate + changelog-noted Latest stable, floating; nightly required; no declared MSRV A from-scratch verifier needs no bleeding-edge features; pinning 1.78 maximizes reproducibility + downstream embeddability (no_std consumers may lag). Floating-latest -> a toolchain bump can silently change behavior/lints mid-project. Nightly-required -> non-reproducible, blocks stable downstreams (and cargo-fuzz already isolates its own nightly need to the fuzz job only, not the engine). MSRV is a CI gate (ADR-24), not a comment. Track nightly wins when the project is a pure research prototype (a Rust-lang contributing experiment or LLVM-integration study) where cutting-edge language features (async trait, specialization, generic const expressions) are the explicit subject of study, stability guarantees for downstream consumers are out of scope, and the team controls all consumers. No declared MSRV wins for an in-house tool consumed by a single team that upgrades the toolchain centrally on every CI run — the MSRV matrix overhead with no downstream consumers is pure waste.
ADR-26 Dependency policy Minimal, audited deps; engine core (smv-bdd/syntax/model/logic/check/bmc) target ZERO non-alloc runtime deps; dev-deps (criterion, proptest, libfuzzer-sys) and smv-cli deps (arg parser) allowed Pull in an existing BDD crate; depend on a parser-generator; heavy framework deps; vendored C libs The project's purpose is a from-scratch ROBDD + checker -> using an existing BDD/parser crate defeats it (and re-introduces ADR-03's memory-model risk). Zero runtime deps in the engine -> smaller attack surface, faster builds, trivial no_std story, no transitive-version churn, supply-chain hygiene. The recursive-descent Parser (## 15) is hand-written (no lalrpop/pest) -> full control over ParseError quality + no_std. Dev/test/bench deps + a small std arg parser in smv-cli are pragmatic exceptions (not linked into the engine). Pull in an existing BDD crate (e.g., biodivine-lib-bdd) wins when the project's purpose is NOT to implement a BDD engine but to use BDDs as infrastructure — for example, a domain-specific analysis tool where BDD implementation correctness is assumed from a well-tested library and the value-add is the analysis logic layered on top. The from-scratch constraint of this blueprint is intentional learning; a production symbolic analysis service should reuse a maintained BDD library. Parser-generator (lalrpop/pest) wins when the grammar changes frequently (e.g., evolving language spec, multiple competing input formats), the team has heavy PL expertise, and parse-error UX quality is better served by a grammar-level specification than hand-written recursion.
ADR-27 Licensing Dual MIT OR Apache-2.0 on every crate; SPDX-License-Identifier headers + LICENSE-MIT + LICENSE-APACHE GPL/copyleft; MIT-only; Apache-only; unlicensed MIT OR Apache-2.0 is the Rust-ecosystem standard -> maximal downstream compatibility (Apache's explicit patent grant + MIT's simplicity), lets consumers pick. Copyleft -> blocks proprietary downstream embedding of smv-bdd (a deliberately reusable standalone package, ADR-02). Single-license -> needlessly narrows reuse. Per-crate SPDX headers keep the workspace machine-auditable. GPL/AGPL copyleft wins when the project is funded by a public institution (university, government lab) with an open-science mandate, and the explicit goal is ensuring all derivative tools remain freely available — GPL copyleft maximizes community reciprocity and prevents private forks that never contribute back. GPL v3 with commercial exception wins for a dual-licensing business model (open-source community edition + proprietary enterprise license), common in commercial verification tooling (cf. Synopsys / Cadence commercial EDA vs. open-source academic tools).

9.1 ADR Reading Notes

  • Correctness-critical ADRs (a violation -> wrong verdict, possibly silent): ADR-04, ADR-05, ADR-09, ADR-10 (out-of-range constraint), ADR-13 (init subset sat(f) test), ADR-15, ADR-17 (equisatisfiability), ADR-18 (lasso decode). ADR-09 is the keystone — see ## 26 Threat Model + ## 25 fuzz targets.
  • Performance-critical ADRs (a violation -> blowup/OOM/timeout, not a wrong answer): ADR-06 (cache), ADR-07 (single primitive -> hit rate), ADR-08 (ordering/sifting), ADR-11 (conjunctive partitioning), ADR-12 (fused and_exists + early quantification). These are tuned in ## 24.
  • Soundness-neutral accelerators (safe to disable/degrade without affecting verdicts): the computed cache (ADR-06), sifting (ADR-08), conjunctive partitioning (ADR-11), Plaisted-Greenbaum/AIG layers (ADR-17). A correctness bug here can only slow down, never falsify — useful for differential bisection.
  • Scope-boundary ADRs (encode NON-GOALS): ADR-13/ADR-15 (symbolic state-set engine, NOT explicit-state #279), ADR-16 (consumes a SatBackend, NOT a competitive SAT core #277). No row introduces SMT (#278), theorem proving (#280), or infinite-state/timed/probabilistic features.
  • Supersession protocol: to revise a decision, add ADR-NN (supersedes ADR-MM) as a new row, mark the old row [SUPERSEDED by ADR-NN] in its Decision cell, never reuse a number. Keeps the ledger append-only + auditable.

10. Repository Layout

Single Cargo workspace, seven smv-* members under crates/, plus governance, conformance, fuzz, docs, and ops scaffolding. Directory boundaries mirror the dependency DAG (Architectural Overview) and the 14-phase plan (Phases 0–13 → ## 12..## 25): one module file per locked concept, one crate per architectural box. No file exists without a job; every leaf below carries its purpose and the phase that first lands it.

10.1 Top-level tree

  • symbolic-model-verifier/
    • Cargo.tomlworkspace root: [workspace] members + resolver="2", shared [workspace.dependencies], lints
    • Cargo.lockcommitted (workspace ships the smv binary) → reproducible CI/bench/fuzz builds
    • rust-toolchain.tomlpin channel=1.78 (MSRV), components=rustfmt,clippy; targets thumbv7em (no_std proof)
    • deny.tomlcargo-deny: license allowlist (MIT/Apache-2.0), ban duplicate/yanked, advisory gate
    • Makefilecanonical entry: build test lint fmt fuzz bench conformance dot docs cover msrv nostd
    • Dockerfilemulti-stage: rust:1.78 builder → distroless runtime; ships smv + NuSMV (diff oracle)
    • docker-compose.ymlservices: smv (CLI), nusmv-oracle (Phase 13 differential), bench (criterion run)
    • README.mdwhat/why, 60-sec quickstart (smv check model.smv), crate map, status table
    • CONTRIBUTING.mdbranch flow, "no source in blueprint" rule, density mandate, review gates, DCO/sign-off
    • CODE_OF_CONDUCT.mdContributor Covenant v2.1
    • SECURITY.mdthreat model pointer (docs/threat-model.md), report channel, untrusted-.smv stance
    • CHANGELOG.mdKeep-a-Changelog; SemVer; per-phase entries; canonicity-regression callouts
    • BLUEPRINT_LOG.mdappend-only: phase gate dates, ADR refs, verdict-diff deltas vs NuSMV, perf snapshots
    • BUILD_PLAN.mdthe 14-phase build order, exit criteria per phase, dependency rationale
    • LICENSE-MITdual license
    • LICENSE-APACHEdual license (Apache-2.0, default for crates)
    • .gitignore/target, /fuzz/corpus, *.dot, perf/*.json, oracle scratch
    • .github/
      • workflows/
        • ci.ymlfmt + clippy -D warnings + test (all crates) + MSRV 1.78 + no_std build (smv-bdd/syntax/model/logic) + deny
        • fuzz.ymlnightly cargo-fuzz: bdd_apply / parse / encode_check, 15 min each, corpus artifact upload
        • bench.ymlcriterion on tagged push; regression gate vs notes/perf-baseline.json (±10%)
        • release.ymltag → build static smv (musl), checksum, attach LICENSE-*, publish docs/
    • crates/§ 10.2 — the 7 workspace members
    • benches/§ 10.3
    • conformance/§ 10.4
    • fuzz/§ 10.5
    • examples/§ 10.6 — sample .smv models (also conformance inputs)
    • docs/§ 10.7
    • templates/blueprint/ADR/runbook skeletons; PR template; .smv model template
    • scripts/§ 10.8 — dev/CI shell + python helpers
    • tools/§ 10.9 — out-of-tree helper binaries (oracle harness, dot viewer)
    • notes/design scratch, perf baselines, reorder experiments (non-normative)
    • papers/offline PDFs of the canonical references (Bryant86, BCMDH92, BCCZ99, EL86)
    • legal/license-scan reports, third-party NOTICE, SPDX manifest
    • brand/logo, ASCII banner for smv --version, dot-render color palette

10.2 crates/ — the seven members

Build order = dependency order: smv-bdd → smv-syntax → smv-model → smv-logic → smv-check → smv-bmc → smv-cli. Crates 1–5 are no_std + alloc (proven by the thumbv7em CI target); smv-bmc is no_std + alloc for the unroll/Tseitin core (the bundled DPLL gate behind std feature); smv-cli is std.

  • // Part 1 of 2: smv-bdd, smv-syntax, smv-model (ROBDD engine + front-end crates)
  • crates/
    • smv-bdd/Phase 0–2,12 — ROBDD package; the load-bearing canonicity engine
      • Cargo.tomlno_std + alloc; deps: hashbrown (no_std hashcons); feature "dot" pulls fmt helpers
      • src/
        • lib.rs#![no_std]; re-export Bdd, BddManager, VarMap; module wiring; crate-level invariants doc
        • node.rsnode table arena: Node{level:u32, low:Bdd, high:Bdd}; Bdd(u32) handle; terminals 0/1
        • unique_table.rshashcons map (level,low,high)->Bdd; mk() enforces reduction rules; rehash on growth
        • cache.rscomputed/op cache keyed by (op,f,g,h); ite memo; lossy fixed-size table; clear-on-GC
        • ite.rsite(f,g,h) THE primitive; not; and/or/xor/imp as ite specializations; terminal cases
        • quant.rsrestrict, exists/forall over cube, and_exists (relational product), rename, satcount
        • reorder.rsPhase 12 — dynamic variable reordering (sifting); swap_adjacent_levels(); schedule
        • gc.rsPhase 2 — mark-sweep GC of dead nodes; free-list reuse; cache invalidation
        • dot.rsPhase 2 — Graphviz export (solid=high, dashed=low, box=terminal); feature-gated
      • tests/
        • canonicity.rsPROPERTY (Phase 1): two formulas equal as functions ⇒ identical Bdd handle
        • apply_truthtable.rs# PROPERTY (Phase 1): ite/and/or/xor vs brute-force truth table for ≤10 vars
        • quant_gc.rsrestrict/exists/forall/and_exists/rename + satcount; GC preserves all live fns
  • │
    • smv-syntax/Phase 3 — SMV-style modeling language front end
      • Cargo.tomlno_std + alloc; no external parser deps (hand-written)
      • src/
        • lib.rs#![no_std]; re-export SmvModule, Parser, Lexer, ParseError
        • token.rsToken enum (kw VAR/ASSIGN/DEFINE/INVAR/TRANS/CTLSPEC/LTLSPEC, ident, int, ops)
        • lexer.rsbyte-scanning Lexer -> Iterator<Token>; comment skip; number/ident DFA
        • parser.rsrecursive-descent Parser; Pratt expr; produces SmvModule; CircularDefine DFS
        • ast.rsSmvModule, VarDecl, VarType{Boolean,Enum,Range}, AssignDecl, DefineDecl, Expr
        • error.rsParseError{kind:ParseErrorKind, span:Span}; Display w/ caret
      • tests/
        • parse_ok.rsgolden ASTs for VAR/ASSIGN/DEFINE/INVAR/TRANS/CTLSPEC/LTLSPEC across all VarType
        • parse_err.rsone fixture per ParseErrorKind; span correctness; CircularDefine cycle detected
  • │
    • smv-model/Phase 4–5 — flatten + boolean-encode; build the symbolic FSM
      • Cargo.tomlno_std + alloc; deps: smv-bdd, smv-syntax
      • src/
        • lib.rs#![no_std]; re-export Encoder, VarEncoding, EncodedFsm
        • varmap.rsVarMap (present<->next level bijection for rename); allocate_pair(); level policy
        • encode.rsEncoder: Boolean(1 bit), Enum(ceil(log2 n) bits + OOR constraint), Range bits
        • fsm.rsEncodedFsm{present,next,init,trans,invar,fairness,ap}; conjunctive partitioning
      • tests/
        • encode_vars.rsbit counts, out-of-range constraint correctness, satcount of legal-state predicate
        • build_fsm.rsinit/trans round-trip: partition product == monolithic trans
  • // Part 2a of 3: smv-logic, smv-check (temporal logic + symbolic engine)
  • crates/ (continued)
    • smv-logic/Phase 7,9 — temporal logic front end + AP binding
      • Cargo.tomlno_std + alloc; deps: smv-bdd, smv-model
      • src/
        • lib.rs#![no_std]; re-export CtlFormula, LtlFormula, parse_ctl/parse_ltl
        • ctl.rsCtlFormula{Ap(Bdd),Not,And,Or,Ex,Eg,Eu}; AX/AU/AG/AF/EF derived
        • ltl.rsLtlFormula{Ap(Bdd),Not,And,Or,X,U,R,F,G}; F p≡true U p (explicit)
        • parse.rsCTLSPEC/LTLSPEC body parsers; atomic Expr->Bdd binding via EncodedFsm.ap
        • nnf.rsnegation-normal-form + normalization; shared LTL prep for Buchi (Phase 9)
      • tests/
        • ctl_parse.rsCTL operator precedence; derived-form expansion equivalence
        • ltl_nnf.rsNNF idempotence; ¬(p U q) rewrite; AP->Bdd handle identity
  • │
    • smv-check/Phase 6–8,10 — the symbolic engine
      • Cargo.tomlno_std + alloc; deps: smv-bdd, smv-model, smv-logic
      • src/
        • lib.rs#![no_std]; re-export CtlChecker, LtlChecker, Trace, StateAssignment
        • reach.rsPhase 6 — image via and_exists+rename; reachable = lfp Z. init ∨ Img(Z)
        • ctl_check.rsPhase 7 — EX/EU/EG fixpoints; holds iff (init ∧ ¬sat(f))==bdd_false
        • buchi.rsPhase 9/10 — symbolic generalized Büchi: state bits + trans Bdd + accept sets
        • ltl_check.rsPhase 10 — product EncodedFsm × Buchi(¬φ); verdict = no fair cycle reachable
        • emerson_lei.rsPhase 10 — fair-states gfp/lfp over EX + fairness/acceptance sets
        • trace.rsPhase 8 — Trace{states, loopback}; onion-ring backward sets → shortest CE
      • tests/
        • reach_fixpoint.rsreachable-set satcount vs hand-enumerated small Kripke structures
        • ctl_oracle.rsEX/EU/EG + derived verdicts vs hand-computed truth on toy models
        • ltl_faircycle.rsG/F/U/R verdicts; fair-cycle present/absent; lasso loopback correctness
  • // Part 2b of 3: smv-bmc, smv-cli (BMC + CLI crates)
  • crates/ (continued)
    • smv-bmc/Phase 11 — SAT-based Bounded Model Checking
      • Cargo.tomlno_std + alloc; feature "dpll" (std) bundles CDCL; deps: smv-model, smv-logic
      • src/
        • lib.rs#![no_std]; re-export Unroller, CnfFormula, SatBackend, SatResult
        • unroll.rsI(s0) ∧ ⋀ T(si,si+1) ∧ ⋁ ¬prop(si); incremental k; completeness check
        • tseitin.rsTseitin transform: BDD structure → equisatisfiable CNF; fresh aux vars
        • cnf.rsCnfFormula{clauses:Vec<Vec<i32>>, nvars:u32}; DIMACS literals; read/write
        • sat_backend.rstrait SatBackend; enum SatResult{Sat(Vec<bool>),Unsat}; bundled DPLL
      • tests/
        • unroll_cnf.rsk-step unroll clause count; DIMACS round-trip; equisatisfiability
        • bmc_oracle.rslength-k counterexample found at expected k; UNSAT below threshold
  • │
    • smv-cli/CLI surface — the smv binary (depends on all 6)
      • Cargo.tomlstd; deps: all smv-* + clap + anyhow
      • src/
        • main.rsarg dispatch; exit codes (0 hold / 1 fail / 2 error); --version
        • cmd_check.rssmv check — CTL/LTL: parse, encode, run checker, print verdict + Trace
        • cmd_bmc.rssmv bmc — unroll to k, call SatBackend, print Trace or UNSAT-up-to-k
        • cmd_reachable.rssmv reachable — reachable lfp; satcount; node count
        • cmd_simulate.rssmv simulate — path through trans from init; print StateAssignment sequence
        • cmd_bdd_stats.rssmv bdd-stats — unique-table size, cache hit rate, node counts, peak live
        • cmd_encode.rssmv encode — dump VarEncoding (var→levels), init/trans node counts
        • cmd_dot.rssmv dot — emit Graphviz of init/trans/sat-set Bdd

10.3 benches/ — Phase 13 performance gate

10.7 docs/ — normative design records
