10.10 Layout invariants (CI-enforced)

# Invariant Enforced by
L1 Crate deps form the locked DAG; no upward/back edge (e.g. smv-bdd never depends on any smv-*). cargo tree check in ci.yml; deny.toml
L2 Crates smv-bdd, smv-syntax, smv-model, smv-logic, smv-bmc build on thumbv7em-none-eabi (no_std+alloc). scripts/check-nostd.sh in CI
L3 std appears only in smv-cli (and smv-bmc dpll feature). grep gate in ci.yml
L4 Every module file name matches a locked concept; no extra public type names beyond Core Schema and Core Types. review + cargo public-api diff
L5 Each phase (## 12..## 25) maps to ≥1 file/dir above; new code lands in its phase's module. BUILD_PLAN.md cross-ref
L6 No full implementation source in docs/, templates/, blueprints (illustrative ≤2-line snippets only). review (lprompt CRITICAL RULE 10)
L7 conformance/expected/* is regenerated only via scripts/run-conformance.sh against the NuSMV oracle. runbook + review
L8 Cargo.lock committed; MSRV 1.78 pinned in rust-toolchain.toml; deny.toml license allowlist green. ci.yml msrv + deny jobs

Directory ↔ phase mapping (summary): smv-bdd/{node,unique_table,cache,ite} → Phases 0–1; smv-bdd/{quant,gc,dot} → Phase 2; smv-bdd/reorder → Phase 12; smv-syntax/* → Phase 3; smv-model/{varmap,encode} → Phase 4, smv-model/fsm → Phase 5; smv-check/reach → Phase 6; smv-check/ctl_check → Phase 7; smv-check/trace → Phase 8; smv-logic/* + smv-check/buchi → Phase 9; smv-check/{ltl_check,emerson_lei} → Phase 10; smv-bmc/* → Phase 11; benches/, conformance/, fuzz/ → Phase 13.

11. Core Schema and Core Types

Normative API surface. Every phase (## 12–## 25) builds against these exact signatures. Types grouped by crate. Signatures + doc comments only; illustrative bodies ≤2 lines. Drift is forbidden: these names are load-bearing.

11.1 Cross-cutting contracts (apply to all crates)

Property Rule Rationale
Handle validity A Bdd is a u32 index valid only against the BddManager that minted it mixing handles across managers reads foreign nodes → silent corruption
Mutation locus BddManager is the sole mutable owner of all node storage; ops take &mut self hashcons + computed cache + GC all mutate shared tables
Copy semantics Bdd, levels (u32), CNF literals (i32) are Copy; AST/formula nodes are owned (Box) handles are cheap; trees own their children
Concurrency BddManager is !Sync by design; single-threaded engine a lock-free concurrent unique table is out of scope; !Sync makes misuse a compile error
Immutability post-build EncodedFsm is constructed once, then read-only; checkers borrow &EncodedFsm fixpoints must not mutate the model; aliasing-safe shared borrows
Serialization Only two external formats: dot (BDD/automaton graphs) and CNF/DIMACS (BMC) no bincode/serde of the node arena — handles are manager-relative, non-portable
Error model Parsing/encoding return Result<_, ParseError>; BDD ops are infallible (panic only on misuse/OOM) a malformed model is recoverable; a stale handle is a bug
no_std smv-bdd / -syntax / -model / -logic are no_std + alloc; smv-check/-bmc/-cli are std core is embeddable; checkers/CLI use threads/IO/clock

Ownership graph (who borrows whom at check time):

BddManager (&mut, single owner of nodes/cache/GC)
   ^                ^
   | &mut           | &mut
EncodedFsm(&) <-- CtlChecker<'a>{ &mut BddManager, &EncodedFsm }
   ^                ^
   | &              | &mut
   +----------- LtlChecker<'a>{ &mut BddManager, &EncodedFsm }
EncodedFsm(&) <-- Unroller<'a>{ &EncodedFsm }  ---Tseitin---> CnfFormula --SatBackend--> SatResult

11.2 smv-bdd — Bdd, BddManager, VarMap

// Part 1 of 2: BddManager core types and Boolean/quantification API
/// Canonical handle: an index into the owning BddManager's node table.
/// Copy. Valid ONLY against its minting manager; comparing/using a Bdd
/// against a different manager is a logic bug (foreign-node read).
/// Equality of two Bdds from the SAME manager == boolean-function equality
/// (the canonicity contract — P1/P2).
pub struct Bdd(u32);

/// The ROBDD package. Single mutable owner of: the node table (level, low,
/// high per node), the unique table (hashcons on (level, low, high) — P2),
/// the computed/op cache (memoizes ite results — P2), and the fixed total
/// variable order. !Sync by design (single-threaded). All structural ops
/// take &mut self; pure queries take &self.
pub struct BddManager { /* node table, unique table, computed cache, var order */ }

impl BddManager {
    /// Terminal nodes. Distinct handles: bdd_true() != bdd_false() (smoke test, Phase 0).
    pub fn bdd_true(&self) -> Bdd;
    pub fn bdd_false(&self) -> Bdd;

    /// Projection function for the variable at `level`: the BDD with high=true,
    /// low=false. Allocates/levels lazily; idempotent under hashcons.
    pub fn ithvar(&mut self, level: u32) -> Bdd;

    /// THE primitive (P2). ite(f,g,h) = (f AND g) OR (not f AND h).
    /// Memoized in the computed cache keyed by (f,g,h). O(|f|*|g|*|h|) with cache.
    pub fn ite(&mut self, f: Bdd, g: Bdd, h: Bdd) -> Bdd;

    /// Boolean connectives — all special cases of ite (P2): and=ite(f,g,⊥), etc.
    pub fn and(&mut self, f: Bdd, g: Bdd) -> Bdd;
    pub fn or(&mut self, f: Bdd, g: Bdd) -> Bdd;
    pub fn xor(&mut self, f: Bdd, g: Bdd) -> Bdd;
    pub fn imp(&mut self, f: Bdd, g: Bdd) -> Bdd;

    /// Negation. O(1): complement edge (or trivial cache hit). &self — never
    /// allocates a node. not(not(f)) == f structurally.
    pub fn not(&self, f: Bdd) -> Bdd;

    /// Cofactor / restrict: f with variable `level` fixed to `val` (P9).
    /// Eliminates that level from the result.
    pub fn restrict(&mut self, f: Bdd, level: u32, val: bool) -> Bdd;

    /// Existential / universal quantification over the variables in `cube`
    /// (a conjunction-of-positive-literals BDD). exists = OR of cofactors;
    /// forall = AND of cofactors. Foundation of image/pre-image (P9).
    pub fn exists(&mut self, f: Bdd, cube: Bdd) -> Bdd;
    pub fn forall(&mut self, f: Bdd, cube: Bdd) -> Bdd;
}
// Part 2 of 2: BddManager relational product, rename, satcount, and node count
impl BddManager {
    /// Relational product: exists cube . (f AND g). Fused — never materializes
    /// the full (f AND g) conjunction. The hot path for image computation
    /// (conjunctive partitioning + early quantification ride on this — P9/P10).
    pub fn and_exists(&mut self, f: Bdd, g: Bdd, cube: Bdd) -> Bdd;

    /// Variable substitution per `map` (present<->next swap for image/preimage).
    /// map MUST be order-respecting or a reorder pass is implied.
    pub fn rename(&mut self, f: Bdd, map: &VarMap) -> Bdd;

    /// #-of-satisfying-assignments over `nvars` variables (P11). u128: handles
    /// up to 2^127 models without overflow (target reachable-state counts ~10^20).
    pub fn satcount(&self, f: Bdd, nvars: u32) -> u128;

    /// Number of distinct BDD nodes reachable from `f` (DAG size, not paths).
    pub fn node_count(&self, f: Bdd) -> usize;

    /// GC: mark from external roots, sweep dead nodes, purge computed cache (P11).
    pub fn ref_inc(&mut self, f: Bdd);
    pub fn ref_dec(&mut self, f: Bdd);
    pub fn gc(&mut self);

    /// Dynamic variable reordering — sifting algorithm (P7). Triggered when
    /// live_nodes > gc_threshold. Preserves all function semantics / handles.
    pub fn sift_all(&mut self);

    /// Graphviz DOT export for debugging BDD structure.
    pub fn to_dot(&self, roots: &[Bdd]) -> String;

    /// Statistics snapshot: node counts, cache hit/miss rates, GC stats.
    pub fn stats(&self) -> BddStats;
}

GC & introspection (Phase 2). Mark-sweep over externally-rooted handles reclaims dead nodes and rebuilds the unique table; the computed cache is invalidated across a GC. dot export (fn to_dot(&self, roots: &[Bdd]) -> String) emits Graphviz with complement edges dashed — the only BDD serialization. No node-arena serde: indices are manager-relative and meaningless across processes/managers.

11.3 The canonicity contract (load-bearing invariant)

INVARIANT (ROBDD canonicity):
  fixed total variable order
  + reduction rule R1: no node with low == high (redundant-test elimination)
  + reduction rule R2: no two structurally-identical nodes (isomorphic merge)
  ==> exactly one canonical DAG per boolean function
  ==> Bdd handle equality  <=>  boolean-function equality   (O(1) test)
  • Enforced at construction by the unique table: every (level, low, high) lookup hashconses; R1 short-circuits before insertion.
  • Consequences other types depend on: fixpoint convergence is detected by handle equality (Z_{i+1} == Z_i); init ⊆ sat(f) is checked as and(init, not(sat_f)) == bdd_false(). Both are wrong if canonicity breaks.
  • GRAVEST failure class: any canonicity bug corrupts equality silently → wrong verdicts, no crash. Phase 1 property test (apply vs truth table; structural equality of equivalent builds) is the guard.
/// Variable substitution table for rename (present<->next). Maps source levels
/// to target levels (e.g. present var level -> its next-state companion level).
/// Built once from EncodedFsm.present / .next; reused every image step.
pub struct VarMap { /* level -> level mapping, order-aware */ }

11.4 smv-syntax — AST + errors

// Part 1 of 2: smv-syntax module-level types (SmvModule, VarType, AssignKind)
/// Parsed SMV module (one MODULE). Owned tree; produced by Parser, consumed by
/// the Encoder. Vec-per-section preserves source order (matters for ASSIGN /
/// DEFINE dependency analysis and CircularDefine detection).
pub struct SmvModule {
    pub vars: Vec<VarDecl>,        // VAR section: name : VarType
    pub defines: Vec<DefineDecl>,  // DEFINE: macro := Expr (no extra state)
    pub assigns: Vec<AssignDecl>,  // ASSIGN: init/next/plain := Expr
    pub trans: Vec<Expr>,          // TRANS: boolean relation over present+next
    pub invar: Vec<Expr>,          // INVAR: state invariant constraint
    pub specs: Vec<SpecDecl>,      // CTLSPEC / LTLSPEC / INVARSPEC
}

/// Finite-domain variable type. ALL types are finite (symbolic encoding needs
/// a bounded boolean encoding). No reals/unbounded ints (non-goal: infinite state).
pub enum VarType {
    Boolean,           // 1 bit
    Enum(Vec<Atom>),   // scalar: ceil(log2 N) bits + out-of-range constraint
    Range(i64, i64),   // bounded integer [lo, hi]: ceil(log2(hi-lo+1)) bits
}

/// Kind of an ASSIGN. init(x):=e seeds the initial predicate; next(x):=e
/// defines the transition relation for x; plain x:=e is an invariant define.
pub enum AssignKind { Init, Next, Plain }
// Part 2 of 2: smv-syntax expression and error types (Expr, SpecDecl, ParseError)
/// Expression AST. Next(.) is legal only inside next() / TRANS context
/// (refers to the next-state value); the Encoder rejects Next() in init/invar.
pub enum Expr {
    Var(Atom),
    Const(Value),
    Not(Box<Expr>),
    Bin(BinOp, Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>),   // & | -> <-> = != < <= + - ...
    Case(Vec<(Expr, Expr)>),            // case g1:e1; g2:e2; ... esac
    Next(Box<Expr>),                    // next-state reference
}

/// A specification clause. Ctl/Ltl carry the raw property string parsed later
/// by smv-logic (defers temporal-operator parsing out of the model grammar);
/// InvarSpec is a pure boolean Expr checked as AG over reachable states.
pub enum SpecDecl {
    Ctl(String),
    Ltl(String),
    InvarSpec(Expr),
}

/// Recoverable parse/static-check failure. Carries kind + source span (not shown).
pub struct ParseError { /* kind: ParseErrorKind, span */ }

pub enum ParseErrorKind {
    UnexpectedToken,  // lexical/grammar
    UnknownVar,       // name resolution
    BadType,          // ill-formed VarType
    TypeMismatch,     // e.g. bool used where Range expected
    CircularDefine,   // DEFINE dependency cycle (Phase 3 must detect)
    OutOfRange,       // Range/Enum literal outside its domain
}

Lexer, Token, Parser, VarDecl, DefineDecl, BinOp, Atom, Value are companion types (Phase 3). Atom = interned identifier symbol; Value = bool / int / enum-member literal. All AST nodes are owned + Box-recursive; no shared/interior mutability.

11.5 smv-model — encoding + symbolic FSM

/// Boolean encoding of ONE finite-domain variable: the ordered BDD levels its
/// bits occupy, plus its source VarType (for decode / out-of-range constraints).
/// levels.len() == ceil(log2(domain size)); Boolean => 1 level.
pub struct VarEncoding {
    pub levels: Vec<u32>,  // BDD levels (MSB-first) carrying this var's bits
    pub ty: VarType,       // original domain (for StateAssignment decode)
}

/// The fully-built symbolic finite-state machine. IMMUTABLE post-build: every
/// field is a Bdd handle into the shared BddManager. Checkers borrow &EncodedFsm.
pub struct EncodedFsm {
    pub present: VarMap,        // present-state variable map
    pub next: VarMap,          // next-state variable map (companion levels)
    pub init: Bdd,             // initial-state predicate = init() AND INVAR
    pub trans: Bdd,            // transition relation = next() AND TRANS AND next-INVAR
    pub invar: Bdd,            // state invariant (conjoined into init/trans)
    pub fairness: Vec<Bdd>,    // generalized-Buchi fairness constraints (per-set)
    pub ap: Vec<(Atom, Bdd)>,  // atomic-proposition name -> its state-set Bdd
}
  • Encoder (Phase 4): maps each VarDecl → VarEncoding; allocates present + next levels (interleaved present/next order keeps trans BDD small); compiles Expr → Bdd over present (and next within next(...)). Out-of-range enum/Range codes are excluded by an added constraint conjoined into invar.
  • trans built with conjunctive partitioning (Phase 5): stored conceptually as Vec<Bdd> partitions internally, exposed as the conjoined trans: Bdd; partitions feed early-quantification in and_exists.
  • ap binds property atoms to state sets at encode time so smv-logic can wrap them as CtlFormula::Ap(Bdd) / LtlFormula::Ap(Bdd) without re-resolving names.

11.6 smv-logic — temporal formulas

/// CTL formula over state-set BDDs. Minimal adequate set {EX, EU, EG, Not, And/Or};
/// AX/AU/AG/AF/EF/AF are DERIVED (NNF rewrite, Phase 7) — not stored variants.
/// Ap holds a state-set Bdd already bound via EncodedFsm.ap (so checking is
/// pure BDD work, no name lookup).
pub enum CtlFormula {
    Ap(Bdd),
    Not(Box<CtlFormula>),
    And(Box<CtlFormula>, Box<CtlFormula>),
    Or(Box<CtlFormula>, Box<CtlFormula>),
    Ex(Box<CtlFormula>),
    Eg(Box<CtlFormula>),
    Eu(Box<CtlFormula>, Box<CtlFormula>),   // E[p U q]
}

/// LTL formula. X/U are primitive; R/F/G provided (F p = true U p; G p = not F not p;
/// p R q = not(not p U not q)). NNF normalization (Phase 9) pushes Not to APs before
/// LTL->Buchi translation.
pub enum LtlFormula {
    Ap(Bdd),
    Not(Box<LtlFormula>),
    And(Box<LtlFormula>, Box<LtlFormula>),
    Or(Box<LtlFormula>, Box<LtlFormula>),
    X(Box<LtlFormula>),
    U(Box<LtlFormula>, Box<LtlFormula>),
    R(Box<LtlFormula>, Box<LtlFormula>),
    F(Box<LtlFormula>),
    G(Box<LtlFormula>),
}

Property parsers (parse_ctl(&str) -> Result<CtlFormula, ParseError>, parse_ltl(&str) -> Result<LtlFormula, ParseError>) take the raw SpecDecl::Ctl/Ltl strings + &EncodedFsm.ap and emit AP-bound formulas. Owned Box-trees; immutable once parsed.

11.7 smv-check — checkers, traces

/// Symbolic CTL engine. Borrows the manager mutably (fixpoints allocate nodes)
/// and the FSM immutably. Lifetime 'a ties both borrows to the call site.
pub struct CtlChecker<'a> { /* &mut BddManager + &'a EncodedFsm */ }

impl CtlChecker<'_> {
    /// Evaluate `f` to the state-set Bdd of states where f holds.
    /// EX p = preimage(p); E[p U q] = lfp Z. q OR (p AND EX Z);
    /// EG p = gfp Z. p AND EX Z. Spec HOLDS iff init ⊆ check(f),
    /// i.e. and(init, not(check(f))) == bdd_false().
    pub fn check(&mut self, f: &CtlFormula) -> Bdd;

    /// Reachable least-fixpoint: lfp Z. init OR Img(Z). Cached; shared with checks.
    pub fn reachable(&mut self) -> Bdd;

    /// Shortest counterexample/witness via onion-ring backward sets (Phase 8);
    /// EG uses a lasso witness. None if the spec holds (no counterexample).
    pub fn counterexample(&mut self, f: &CtlFormula) -> Option<Trace>;
}

/// A finite or lasso-shaped trace. states[0] is an initial state; if loopback
/// is Some(i), the trace loops from the last state back to states[i] (a lasso,
/// for EG / LTL fair cycles). loopback == None => a finite (safety) witness.
pub struct Trace {
    pub states: Vec<StateAssignment>,
    pub loopback: Option<usize>,
}

/// Symbolic LTL engine: product of EncodedFsm with the Buchi automaton for
/// (not phi), then Emerson-Lei fair-cycle detection (nested gfp/lfp over EX +
/// acceptance/fairness sets). Model |= A phi iff NO fair cycle is reachable.
pub struct LtlChecker<'a> { /* product + Emerson-Lei */ }

StateAssignment (companion type, Phase 8): a decoded full state — Vec<(Atom, Value)> mapping each original SMV variable to its concrete value, obtained by reading the bit-level cube off a BDD path and inverting each VarEncoding. This is the human-readable boundary; BDD cubes never leak to the CLI raw.

11.8 smv-bmc — unrolling + SAT boundary

/// Bounded Model Checking unroller. Borrows the FSM immutably; produces CNF.
pub struct Unroller<'a> { /* &'a EncodedFsm */ }
// Builds, for bound k:  I(s0) AND (conj_{i<k} T(s_i, s_{i+1})) AND (or_{i<=k} not p(s_i))
// as a CnfFormula via a Tseitin transform (fresh vars for BDD/Boolean subterms).
// Incremental k supported: clauses for step k reuse prior structure.

/// A CNF formula in DIMACS literal convention: each clause is a Vec<i32> of
/// non-zero literals (+v / -v, 1-based); nvars = highest variable index.
/// The ONLY serialization boundary to a SAT solver. Owned; portable across
/// processes (unlike BDD handles).
pub struct CnfFormula {
    pub clauses: Vec<Vec<i32>>,
    pub nvars: u32,
}

/// Pluggable SAT solver boundary: DIMACS CNF in, assignment out. Lets #277's
/// solver or a bundled DPLL/CDCL satisfy the same trait (non-goal: smv-bmc does
/// NOT ship a competitive solver). &mut self: a solver carries mutable state.
pub trait SatBackend {
    fn solve(&mut self, cnf: &CnfFormula) -> SatResult;
}

/// SAT outcome. Sat carries a full assignment indexed by (var-1); Unsat means
/// no length-<=k counterexample (and, at the completeness threshold, none at all).
pub enum SatResult {
    Sat(Vec<bool>),
    Unsat,
}

A Sat(model) is decoded back through the per-step variable mapping into a Trace (Phase 11): each unrolled step s_i → one StateAssignment, giving a concrete length-k counterexample to G p. Unsat plus k ≥ the reachability/recurrence diameter (completeness threshold, Phase 12) proves the safety property.

11.9 Type-to-phase normativity map

Type Defined/first-used Mutated by Read by
Bdd, BddManager, VarMap Phase 0/1/2 (## 12–14) every BDD op (&mut) all crates
SmvModule, Expr, VarType, SpecDecl, ParseError Phase 3 (## 15) Parser only smv-model, smv-logic
VarEncoding, EncodedFsm Phase 4–5 (## 16–17) Encoder (build only) smv-check, smv-bmc, smv-logic
CtlFormula, LtlFormula Phase 7 / 9 (## 19, ## 21) parser (build only) checkers
CtlChecker, Trace, StateAssignment Phase 7–8 (## 19–20) &mut BddManager smv-cli
LtlChecker Phase 10 (## 22) &mut BddManager smv-cli
Unroller, CnfFormula, SatBackend, SatResult Phase 11 (## 23) solver (&mut) smv-cli

All signatures above are frozen; phases may add private fields/helpers but MUST NOT change these public shapes, names, or semantics.

12. Phase 0 — Skeleton, workspace, and the "build a one-variable BDD" smoke test

Phase 0 stands up the scaffold only: a compiling Cargo workspace with all seven smv-* crates, the LOCKED core type shells (no algorithms yet), error enums, CI/Makefile/Dockerfile, no_std+alloc gating on the four core crates, the NuSMV differential-oracle scaffold (feature-gated, dormant), and ONE end-to-end smoke test. The smoke test is the contract: ithvar(0) yields a 2-node BDD, bdd_true() != bdd_false(), and smv-syntax parses a 1-variable VAR/ASSIGN model into an SmvModule. Nothing else does real work — ite/apply/fixpoints/encoding all unimplemented!() or return placeholders. The gate: make ci green on a clean checkout.

0.1 Objectives

  • Compiling 7-crate workspace (smv-bdd, smv-syntax, smv-model, smv-logic, smv-check, smv-bmc, smv-cli) with the LOCKED dependency edges wired and acyclic.
  • Type shells present and named EXACTLY per spine: Bdd(u32), BddManager{node table,…}, SmvModule/Expr/VarType/AssignKind/SpecDecl, CtlFormula/LtlFormula, VarEncoding/EncodedFsm, Trace/CnfFormula/SatBackend/SatResult, all error enums (ParseErrorKind).
  • no_std+alloc enforced on the 4 core crates (smv-bdd/smv-syntax/smv-model/smv-logic); std only in smv-check(GC/dot host-side ok), smv-bmc, smv-cli.
  • CI pipeline: fmt --check → clippy -D warnings → build --all → test --all; reproducible in Docker; make ci one command.
  • Smoke test passes (a real 2-node BDD from ithvar(0), terminal distinctness, 1-var model round-trips to AST) AND the NuSMV oracle scaffold compiles but stays dormant behind --features nusmv-oracle (dev-only) — absence of the NuSMV binary must NOT break make ci.

0.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Workspace layout (LOCKED paths):

  • symbolic-model-verifier/

smv-bdd shells (node.rs/manager.rs/bdd.rs):

pub struct Bdd(pub(crate) u32); struct Node { level: u32, low: u32, high: u32 } // level==u32::MAX sentinel for terminal
pub struct BddManager { nodes: Vec<Node>, /* unique table, computed cache: Phase 1 */ }
impl BddManager {
    pub fn new() -> Self;                        // pre-seed indices 0 = FALSE, 1 = TRUE
    pub fn bdd_false(&self) -> Bdd; pub fn bdd_true(&self) -> Bdd; // Bdd(0)=FALSE, Bdd(1)=TRUE terminals
    pub fn ithvar(&mut self, level: u32) -> Bdd; // ONLY real method this phase: push (level,0,1)
    pub fn node_count(&self, f: Bdd) -> usize;   // DAG-walk count incl. reachable terminals
    // ite/and/or/not/restrict/exists/forall/and_exists/rename/satcount -> unimplemented!()
}
  • new(): reserve nodes[0]=FALSE, nodes[1]=TRUE (level u32::MAX); terminals are NOT in the unique table. (ADR-03: index-arena design; ADR-19: no_std + alloc constraint.)
  • ithvar(l): push Node{level:l, low:0 /*FALSE*/, high:1 /*TRUE*/}, return its index → exactly 3 nodes total (2 terminals + 1 internal), node_count of that handle = 2 (internal + 2 terminals counted once each → define count = distinct reachable nodes = 3; smoke asserts internal-node count via helper, see 0.4). (P1: fixed total variable order established here via level field.)
  • Bdd: Copy + Eq + Hash derive — equality is the load-bearing O(1) handle compare (canonicity proven Phase 1; shell already enforces struct equality semantics). (P1/P2: handle equality ↔ function equality is the canonicity invariant.)

smv-syntax shells (ast.rs/token.rs/lexer.rs/parser.rs/error.rs): all LOCKED enums/structs (SmvModule,VarDecl,VarType{Boolean,Enum,Range},AssignDecl,AssignKind{Init,Next,Plain},DefineDecl,InvarDecl,TransDecl,Expr,SpecDecl,ParseError,ParseErrorKind). Minimal real lexer (whitespace/comment skip, ident/keyword/number/symbol tokens) + recursive-descent parser handling EXACTLY: MODULE main, VAR x : boolean;, ASSIGN init(x):=…; next(x):=…;. Everything beyond → ParseErrorKind::UnexpectedToken. Atom = interned String (alloc). smv-model/smv-logic/smv-check/smv-bmc shells: types only (VarEncoding,EncodedFsm,VarMap,CtlFormula,LtlFormula,CtlChecker,LtlChecker,Trace{states,loopback},StateAssignment,Unroller,CnfFormula{clauses,nvars},SatBackend trait,SatResult{Sat,Unsat}); methods unimplemented!(). smv-cli: clap subcommand stubs (check/bmc/reachable/simulate/bdd-stats/encode/dot) printing "unimplemented: <cmd>", exit 0.

no_std gating: each core crate lib.rs top: #![cfg_attr(not(feature = "std"), no_std)] + extern crate alloc;; default feature set excludes std; CI builds core crates with --no-default-features. NuSMV oracle scaffold: smv-check/tests/oracle.rs behind #[cfg(feature = "nusmv-oracle")]; helper run_nusmv(model: &str) -> Option<Verdict> shells out to $NUSMV (env, default NuSMV); returns None (test ignored) if binary absent → never fails CI. Dev-dependency only.

0.6 Pitfalls

  • Terminal nodes leaking into the unique table (later ithvar/ite could hashcons a "terminal", breaking canonicity — root cause: treating TRUE/FALSE as ordinary nodes; fix: reserve indices 0/1, sentinel level==u32::MAX, unique table NEVER stores them, debug via assert nodes[0].level==u32::MAX) and low/high swapped in ithvar (silently inverts the variable's polarity; every downstream verdict wrong, no crash — the GRAVEST class; root cause: ROBDD convention is high=var-true=TRUE terminal, low=var-false=FALSE; fix: A4 pins low==0,high==1; debug: dot export (Phase 2) of ithvar(0)).
  • no_std crate transitively pulling std via a dependency's default features (masks a portability break; root cause: un-gated default-features; fix: default-features=false on every core-crate dep, CI runs --no-default-features, A2) and cyclic crate dependency (e.g. smv-model → smv-logic → smv-model, cargo refuses to build; root cause: AP-binding in the wrong crate; fix: respect LOCKED edges, smv-logic depends on smv-model, never the reverse).
  • Oracle test hard-failing in CI when NuSMV missing → red pipeline on every machine. Root cause: unwrap() on run_nusmv. Fix: return Option, #[ignore] when None; A7 enforces.
  • derive(PartialEq) on Bdd giving the wrong invariant if a future field is added → equality must remain pure u32 compare. Root cause: scope creep on the handle. Fix: Bdd stays a single u32; comment-lock it.

0.7 Performance Concerns

  • Phase-0 perf is trivial (smoke runs in <1 ms), but lock the shapes now: nodes: Vec<Node> is the future cache-resident arena — keep Node ≤ 16 bytes (u32×3 + pad) so a node fits and arrays stay dense; no Box/Rc (index handles only). Atom=String interning deferred to Phase 3; smoke's single var makes allocation negligible.
  • make ci wall-clock target: < 90 s on a clean checkout (4-core); incremental cargo test --all < 10 s. CI uses --locked + a cargo registry cache layer in Docker.

0.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty model (MODULE main only) → valid SmvModule, empty vectors (fixture empty.smv); NOT an error.
  • ithvar with a large level (e.g. 1_000_000) → still 1 internal node (level is just stored, no per-level allocation yet); and bdd_true()/bdd_false() called on a fresh BddManager::new() → must already be valid (pre-seeded), independent of any ithvar call.
  • Trailing whitespace / -- line comments / blank lines in .smv → lexer skips, no token emitted; CRLF vs LF line endings → lexer normalizes, line/col in ParseError counts logical lines.
  • Repeated init(x) for same var → Phase 0 parser accepts (semantic dup-check is Phase 3); record both AssignDecls.

0.9 Failure Analysis

Symptom Likely root cause First probe
bdd_true() == bdd_false(), or smoke node-count ≠ 3 terminals not pre-seeded/same index; or extra/missing terminal/dup node in walk inspect nodes[0..2] after new(); dump reachable set from ithvar(0) handle
--no-default-features build fails on core crate un-gated std dep cargo tree -e features -p <crate>
make ci red only on contributors' machines NuSMV oracle not ignored run cargo test -- --ignored --list
clippy red, or Docker make ci red with host green unimplemented!() flagged/unused-import; or toolchain drift clippy --all locally (allow unimplemented in shells); confirm rust-toolchain.toml honored in image

Gravest-class guard (canonicity) is not yet exercised (no ite), but the terminal-reservation + low/high invariants (A4/A5) are its foundation; mis-set here ⇒ silent wrong verdicts for the whole project.

13. Phase 1 — ROBDD core (unique table, reduction, ite)

Load-bearing phase: every downstream verdict trusts canonicity. Build the smv-bdd node table, the unique table (hashcons enforcing both reduction rules), ite as the single primitive, and not/and/or/xor/imp derived from ite. Output: a BDD package combining boolean functions with O(1) equality.

1.1 Objectives

  • Node table: (level: u32, low: Bdd, high: Bdd) arena indexed by Bdd(u32); two reserved terminals.
  • Unique table: hashcons on (level,low,high) → no duplicate node (R2); construction-time check low==high → return low → no redundant test (R1).
  • ite(f,g,h) = THE primitive; not/and/or/xor/imp/bdd_true/bdd_false/ithvar all reduce to it (and to constants).
  • Computed cache keyed (f,g,h) → ite amortized O(|f|·|g|·|h|); without it, exponential.
  • Canonicity invariant: fixed total var order + R1 + R2 ⇒ structural handle equality iff function equality; prove via property tests. GC/cache/quantification deferred to Phase 2.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files under smv-bdd/src/:

File Contents
node.rs struct Node { level: u32, low: Bdd, high: Bdd }; Bdd(u32); terminal sentinels
manager.rs BddManager { nodes: Vec<Node>, unique: HashMap<(u32,Bdd,Bdd),Bdd>, cache: HashMap<(Bdd,Bdd,Bdd),Bdd>, nvars: u32 }
ite.rs ite, normalization, terminal short-circuits, cache probe/insert
apply.rs not/and/or/xor/imp/nand/nor as ite specializations
lib.rs #![no_std] + extern crate alloc; re-exports
  • Terminals: index 0 = bdd_false, index 1 = bdd_true; sentinel level = u32::MAX (sorts last, below all vars). bdd_true()/bdd_false() → Bdd(1)/Bdd(0) (O(1), no alloc). (P1: terminal nodes are the only non-variable nodes in the ROBDD.)
  • mk(level,low,high) (private): if low==high return low (R1 — Bryant's redundant-test reduction rule, P1); else unique.entry((level,low,high)).or_insert_with(|| push Node) (R2 — Bryant's duplicate-node merge, P1; hashcons per P2). Sole node constructor — all paths funnel here, so canonicity cannot be bypassed. (ADR-03: index u32 not Rc; ADR-05: HashMap unique table.)
  • ithvar(level) = mk(level, bdd_false, bdd_true); assert level < nvars (or grow nvars). (P1: variable ordering is fixed at level allocation time.)
  • top_level(f) = nodes[f].level (terminals → u32::MAX). ite recursion variable = min(top_level(f),top_level(g),top_level(h)). (P2: split on the top-most variable in the fixed order.)
  • ite(f,g,h): (1) terminal rules — ite(true,g,_)=g, ite(false,_,h)=h, ite(f,g,g)=g, ite(f,true,false)=f; (2) cache probe (f,g,h) (P2: computed cache memoization); (3) v=min top level; co_0=restrict each operand to v=false, co_1=v=true (Shannon split: take child if top==v, else operand unchanged); (4) r=mk(v, ite(f0,g0,h0), ite(f1,g1,h1)); (5) cache insert, return. (ADR-07: single ite table not per-operator tables.)
  • not(f) = ite(f, bdd_false, bdd_true) (this phase: explicit, no complement edges — keep canonicity simple; complement-edge option recorded as ADR-04, deferred).
  • and(f,g)=ite(f,g,bdd_false); or(f,g)=ite(f,bdd_true,g); xor(f,g)=ite(f,not(g),g); imp(f,g)=ite(f,g,bdd_true). (P2: all Boolean ops are ite specializations — the single-primitive design.)
  • node_count(f): DFS marking visited indices (terminals excluded); satcount/full GC/dot/restrict/exists → Phase 2. (P11: GC and satcount are Phase 2 concerns.)

1.3 Deliverable

smv-bdd builds + combines boolean functions: cargo build -p smv-bdd clean; library API bdd_true/bdd_false/ithvar/ite/not/and/or/xor/imp/node_count. Demo test constructs (x0 ∧ x1) ∨ ¬x2 two structurally different ways and asserts identical Bdd handle. #![no_std]-clean (alloc only).

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

JSON {vars, build_steps, expect: {handle_eq | node_count | truth_table}} in smv-bdd/tests/fixtures/:

Fixture Expected

canonicity_pairs.json both encodings of (x0∧x1)∨¬x2 → identical handle
xor_parity_20.json 20-var parity → node_count==39
ite_majority.json ite(x0,x1,x2) truth table = majority-of-3
redundant_collapse.json ite(x0,x1,x1) returns x1 handle exactly (R1)
dup_share.json building same subfn twice → one node (nodes.len unchanged on 2nd)
(malformed) bad_var.json ithvar(level>=nvars) → panic/Err; never silent extension
(malformed) cyclic_ref.json step referencing unbuilt Bdd index → rejected at load

1.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Debug
Silent wrong verdicts R1/R2 bypassed by a node ctor that skips mk grep: only mk pushes to nodes; assert in tests
Non-canonical merge terminal level not u32::MAX ⇒ var sorts below terminal ⇒ wrong split var unit-test top_level(terminal)==u32::MAX
Exponential ite cache disabled/keyed wrong (e.g. unnormalized) ⇒ recomputation blowup track recursion count; xor-20 must stay ≈800 calls not 2^20
Cache staleness this phase nodes never freed; but caching dead-index reuse later corrupts — reserve invariant now document: cache valid only pre-GC (Phase 2 flushes)
Hash collision misjoin hashing Bdd/level with weak hasher ⇒ logically distinct keys collide in user code use full (u32,u32,u32) key, HashMap equality not hash-only
Off-by-one terminal index swapping Bdd(0)/Bdd(1) flips all logic constants FALSE=Bdd(0), TRUE=Bdd(1) in one place

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • ite = O(|f|·|g|·|h|) with cache; cache hit = HashMap lookup ~O(1). Without cache → exponential; cache non-optional.
  • Node table Vec<Node> (12 B/node, contiguous) → cache-friendly DFS. Unique HashMap rehash amortized; reserve capacity hint.
  • Targets: 20-var parity build < 5 ms; 100k-node build < 50 ms; equality test < 10 ns (u32 cmp). Hashcons insert dominates; FxHash/ahash over SipHash (ADR: non-DoS-critical internal table).
  • Memory: 1 M nodes ≈ 12 MB arena + ~40 MB unique map; acceptable, GC reclaims dead nodes in Phase 2.

1.8 Edge Cases

  • ite(f,g,g) for any f → g (idempotent collapse, distinct from R1 but must also fire pre-recursion).
  • All-true / all-false operands: and(bdd_true,bdd_true)=bdd_true; or(bdd_false,bdd_false)=bdd_false.
  • Single-variable fn: ithvar(k) then not → mk(k,bdd_true,bdd_false), distinct handle from ithvar(k).
  • nvars==0 model: only terminals legal; ithvar panics — valid for a constant-only FSM.
  • Self-op: xor(f,f)=bdd_false, and(f,f)=f, or(f,f)=f, imp(f,f)=bdd_true — verify via terminal/idempotent rules, not graph rebuild.
  • Deeply skewed var usage (only level 31 of 32 used) → BDD has 1 internal node; top_level skip must not invent intermediate nodes.

1.9 Failure Analysis

  • Gravest class: any path constructing a node off the mk hashcons breaks canonicity silently — no crash, equality lies, fixpoints (Phase 6+) appear to converge at the wrong set ⇒ false PASS/FAIL on real properties. Mitigation: single mk chokepoint; post-build canonicity audit walks nodes, asserts no low==high and no dup (level,low,high).
  • Stack overflow on deep recursion (long var chains): ite recursion depth = #levels; bound by nvars (≤ a few hundred typical) — acceptable; note explicit-stack rewrite as future option if nvars > ~10k.
  • u32 index exhaustion at 2^32 nodes → return Err/panic with clear message; not silent wraparound (would alias node 0 = bdd_false, catastrophic).
  • Detection: differential check — recompute every ite result against an exhaustive truth-table oracle for ≤8 vars in CI; mismatch ⇒ canonicity/ite bug localized to operand triple.

1.10 Operational Notes

  • Run: cargo test -p smv-bdd (unit + fixtures + proptest). Property crate: proptest gated behind std/dev-dep, core stays no_std.
  • Observability: BddManager::stats() → {nodes, unique_len, cache_len, cache_hits, cache_misses}; surfaced later via smv bdd-stats.
  • Determinism: fixed var order + deterministic hashcons ⇒ byte-identical node table across runs (seed any randomized hasher with a constant for reproducible node_count).
  • Invariant assertions compiled under debug_assertions only (zero release overhead); CI runs debug + release to confirm release path still canonical.
  • Hand-off to Phase 2: node table + unique table + cache are the substrate for restrict/exists/and_exists/rename/GC/dot; do not alter Node layout without re-auditing canonicity.

14. Phase 2 — BDD operations for model checking (quantification, relational product, GC)

Phase 1 delivered ite + the unique table + terminals + canonicity. Phase 2 builds the model-checking surface on top: cofactor, quantification, the fused relational product, present↔next renaming, counting, cache lifecycle, and reference-counted mark-sweep GC. After this phase smv-model/smv-check can compute images and fixpoints; nothing below this layer is allowed to depend on iteration-order or transient handles.

2.1 Objectives

  • Implement on BddManager: restrict, exists, forall, and_exists, rename, satcount, node_count, plus GC (ref_inc/ref_dec/gc) and to_dot. All in crate smv-bdd (no_std + alloc).
  • and_exists = single fused recursion (AND ⊗ ∃) — never and then exists, because the unquantified conjunction can blow up by orders of magnitude.
  • All ops memoized in the computed cache; cache key tagged by op so distinct ops sharing operands never collide.
  • GC reclaims dead nodes without breaking the canonicity invariant; live Bdd handles survive verbatim (same u32) iff the manager does not compact, or are remapped iff it does.
  • Quantification + relational product property-tested against brute-force truth tables for ≤16 vars.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files (all under crates/smv-bdd/src/):

File Adds
manager.rs restrict, exists, forall, and_exists, rename; VarMap apply; cube-walking helpers
cache.rs extend computed cache: CacheOp tag enum {Ite, AndExists, Restrict, Exists, Forall, Rename}; key = (CacheOp, Bdd, Bdd, Bdd) (unused slots = 0/sentinel)
gc.rs RefCounts (Vec<u32> parallel to node table), ref_inc/ref_dec/gc mark-sweep, free-list reuse
count.rs / dot.rs satcount (u128), node_count (HashSet-of-visited DFS); to_dot(&self, roots: &[Bdd]) -> String (Graphviz; solid=high, dashed=low)
varmap.rs pub struct VarMap { map: Vec<u32> } — level→level permutation (present↔next)

Cube representation (LOCKED): a cube is itself a Bdd — a conjunction of positive literals; quantified variables = the set of levels appearing in the cube. Walk the cube top-down; at each node take the high child (positive literal) to read the next level. (P9: cube representation is the standard for quantification in BDD-based image computation.)

Algorithms (recursion + memo; terminals are base cases; all three operate purely via ite — P2). Each op dispatches on the top variable v = min(var(f),…) and recurses on cofactors; the computed cache is probed first, inserted on the way out.

Op Signature Base cases Recursive dispatch Result
restrict (f, level, val) → Bdd f terminal or var(f) > level → f; var(f) == level → val=1: high(f), val=0: low(f) v = var(f); recurse high/low cofactors of f at level; re-build above level via ite(ithvar(v), r_high, r_low) Cofactor `f
exists (f, cube) → Bdd cube = bdd_true or f terminal → f; var(cube) < var(f) → exists(f, high(cube)) (absent var) v = var(f); if v ∈ cube: result = or(exists(high(f), cube'), exists(low(f), cube')) — OR the two cofactors (∃x. f = f|₁ ∨ f|₀); else: ite(ithvar(v), exists(high(f),cube'), exists(low(f),cube')) ∃{vars in cube}. f
forall (f, cube) → Bdd (decomposes to exists) not(exists(not(f), cube)) — the ∀ = ¬∃¬ identity; cube unchanged ∀{vars in cube}. f
and_exists (f, g, cube) → Bdd f = false or g = false → false; f = true and g = true → true v = min(var(f), var(g)); recurse on both v=1 and v=0 cofactors of f and g; if v ∈ cube: result = or(ae_high, ae_low) (quantify v on-the-fly — early quantification); else: ite(ithvar(v), ae_high, ae_low) ∃{cube}.(f ∧ g) — fused; never materializes and(f,g) in full
rename (f, map) → Bdd f terminal → f Rebuild via ite traversal remapping each level L → map[L]; cache keyed (Rename, f, mapId, 0) Present↔next swap without constructing intermediate nodes

satcount(f, nvars): post-order, count(node)=2^(skipped)·(count(low)+count(high))/2; terminals 1/0; scaled by unmentioned vars to nvars; returns u128 (saturates, no overflow panic). (P11: satcount with shared-node DP is the canonical CUDD-style algorithm.) node_count(f): DFS marking a visited HashSet<u32>, count includes terminals once.

GC: reference counting on external roots only (handles held outside the manager — init, trans, fixpoint accumulators). ref_inc(bdd)/ref_dec(bdd) adjust the root's count; gc() marks from all roots with count>0 (and the two terminals), sweeps unmarked nodes onto a free list, and purges the entire computed cache (cache may reference now-dead nodes). (P11: GC design follows Somenzi's CUDD notes — mark from roots, sweep dead nodes, invalidate entire cache.) Internal child references are not counted — reachability is recomputed at mark time. Trigger gc() when live_nodes > gc_threshold (default 2^20, grows 1.3× each GC if reclaim <25%). (ADR-03: this GC protocol is only possible because we own the node table as an index arena — Rc<Node> would require per-node refcount and could not purge the cache as a unit.)

2.3 Deliverable

smv-bdd exposing the full LOCKED BddManager API (restrict/exists/forall/and_exists/rename/satcount/node_count + ref_inc/ref_dec/gc/to_dot). cargo test -p smv-bdd green: unit tests + the quantification/relational-product truth-table property suite. smv bdd-stats (CLI shim) prints node_count + satcount for a hand-built BDD.

# Criterion Target
A1 and_exists(f,g,cube) ≡ exists(and(f,g),cube) (handle equality) 100% over 5k random triples, ≤12 vars
A2 forall(f,c) ≡ not(exists(not f,c)); exists(f,empty_cube)==f exact
A3 restrict(f,l,v) matches truth-table cofactor 100% over 5k, ≤14 vars
A4 rename(rename(f,m),m_inv)==f (round-trip identity) exact
A5 satcount matches brute-force model count exact, ≤16 vars

Fixture Input Expected
tests/relprod_majority.rs 3-var majority f, g=x0, cube={x0} and_exists == `or(f
tests/exists_all.rs f over {x0..x3}, cube=all 4 bdd_true if f satisfiable else bdd_false
tests/rename_swap.rs f over present {0,2,4}, map→next {1,3,5} levels shifted; round-trip == f
tests/satcount_full.rs bdd_true, nvars=10 1024
tests/gc_reclaim.rs build 10k temp nodes, drop refs, gc() live nodes == roots' cone
tests/dot_golden.rs 2-var xor matches tests/golden/xor.dot
Malformed (3 cases) (a) exists(f, non_cube_bdd) cube w/ negative lit; (b) restrict(f, level,_) with level >= num_vars; (c) rename with non-bijective VarMap (collision) (a) debug_assert! "cube must be positive conjunction"; (b) Err/panic BadLevel; (c) debug_assert! "VarMap must be a permutation"

2.6 Pitfalls

  • and_exists separated into and+exists → exponential blowup on transition relations (the whole reason image is hard). Root cause: lazy reuse of and+exists. Debug: log peak node_count per recursion depth; fused version must never materialize the conjunction.
  • Cache not purged on GC / GC during an in-flight recursion → (1) dangling u32 indices resolving to recycled nodes cause silent canonicity corruption, wrong fixpoints, no crash — root cause stale cache entries; debug via an assertion that every cache operand is a live node post-GC. (2) an intermediate result not yet rooted gets swept if GC re-enters mid-recursion — root cause GC reentrancy; fix: GC only at op entry, or pin a temporary-root stack; debug via a "freed node still referenced" sweep-time assert.
  • Cube walked bottom-up / wrong child → quantifies the wrong variable set. Root cause: cube is a positive-literal conjunction read via high. Debug: print quantified level set vs cube levels.
  • satcount overflow on >64 effective vars → u128 saturates silently masking a bug. Root cause: 2^nvars. Debug: explicit saturating_mul, log when saturated.
  • rename ignores order ⇒ non-canonical result if map crosses levels without re-establishing variable order. Root cause: blind index substitution. Fix: rebuild via ite respecting target levels.

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • ite/and_exists are O(|f|·|g|·|h|) with a working cache; without it, exponential. Cache hit-rate target ≥85% on reachability workloads.
  • Computed cache: open-addressed, power-of-2 size, single-slot-per-key (lossy, no chaining) — eviction is cheap and correctness-neutral. Default 2^22 entries (~64 MB at 16 B/entry).
  • and_exists early quantification (quantify v the instant both cofactors are taken) is the single biggest win — keeps intermediate BDDs near final image size.
  • GC pause: mark-sweep is O(live + dead); amortized by 1.3× threshold growth. Target <50 ms per GC at 2^20 nodes.
  • node_count/satcount allocate a visited set; cache node_count per-root if called in hot loops.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • cube == bdd_true (empty cube) → quantification is identity; and_exists degenerates to and.
  • restrict on a level above the BDD root (var(f) > level) → returns f unchanged.
  • Quantifying a variable not present in f → no-op for that level (skip via next_cube).
  • rename identity map → returns f (cache hit, no rebuild).
  • satcount(bdd_false,_)==0, satcount(bdd_true,n)==2^n.
  • GC with zero dead nodes → no-op except cache purge; GC with zero live roots → only terminals survive; and_exists(f, f, full_cube) (self-product) → satisfiability test of f.

2.9 Failure Analysis

Symptom Likely cause Detection
Wrong reachable set, no crash canonicity broken by stale cache after GC canonicity property test (Phase 1) re-run post-GC
Image computation OOM and_exists not fused, or no early quantification peak-node-count instrumentation
forall ≠ ¬∃¬ not complement-edge bug interacting w/ quantification A2 differential check
satcount off by 2^k skipped-variable scaling error brute-force fixture satcount_full
Use-after-free assert in sweep GC reentrancy / unrooted temporary temporary-root pin + sweep assert
rename non-canonical level-order not re-established round-trip A4 + canonicity assert

GRAVEST: any GC/cache interaction that resurrects a dead node index silently corrupts every downstream verdict — this phase's load-bearing invariant is "post-GC, every reachable node is canonical and every external handle still denotes its original function."

2.10 Operational Notes

  • BddStats { num_nodes, peak_nodes, cache_hits, cache_misses, gc_count, gc_reclaimed } exposed via manager.stats(); surfaced by smv bdd-stats.
  • GC threshold, cache size, growth factor configurable via BddManager::with_capacity(nodes, cache); CLI flags --bdd-cache, --gc-threshold thread through from smv-cli.
  • to_dot output consumed by smv dot; render with dot -Tpng; complemented edges shown as a dot on the edge tail.
  • Feature flag bdd-debug enables canonicity + live-reference asserts on every op (off in release; on in CI and the property suite).
  • Determinism: GC and cache eviction must not change function values, only handles/perf — fixtures assert verdicts are GC-schedule-independent (run suite with --gc-threshold 1 to force GC every op).

15. Phase 3 — SMV modeling language (lexer, parser, AST)

Implement smv-syntax: a Lexer + recursive-descent Parser that turn SMV source text into a validated SmvModule AST, then a post-parse semantic pass (name resolution + type checking + CircularDefine detection). no_std + alloc. This is the front end consumed by smv-model (Phase 4) and smv-logic (spec strings, Phase 9). It does NOT encode to BDDs — no smv-bdd dependency here.

3.1 Objectives

  • Tokenize SMV source: keywords (MODULE VAR ASSIGN DEFINE INVAR TRANS INIT CTLSPEC LTLSPEC boolean init next case esac TRUE FALSE), identifiers, integer literals, operators, :=, .., comments (-- to EOL).
  • Parse to SmvModule { vars, defines, assigns, trans, invar, specs } via recursive descent + Pratt expression parsing (precedence-driven).
  • Cover all VarType (Boolean/Enum/Range), all AssignKind (Init/Next/Plain), DEFINE, INVAR, TRANS, CTLSPEC/LTLSPEC, Case expr, next() operator.
  • Semantic check: resolve every Var(Atom) to a decl/define; type-check expressions; emit precise ParseError/ParseErrorKind with (line,col) span. next() legal only on RHS of next(x):=…/TRANS, never under INIT/init().
  • Invariant: a returned AST is well-formed — every var typed, every name bound, every expr type-consistent, define graph acyclic. Garbage in -> typed ParseError, never panic.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files under smv-syntax/src/:

File Contents
lexer.rs Token, TokenKind, Span{line:u32,col:u32}, Lexer{src,pos,line,col}; fn next(&mut self)->Result<Token,ParseError>; peekable buffer
ast.rs SmvModule, VarDecl{name:Atom,ty:VarType}, VarType, AssignDecl{kind:AssignKind,lhs:Atom,rhs:Expr}, AssignKind, DefineDecl{name:Atom,body:Expr}, InvarDecl/TransDecl=Expr, Expr, BinOp, Value, SpecDecl, Atom(interned)
parser.rs Parser{lexer,peek:Option<Token>}; fn parse_module(&mut self)->Result<SmvModule,ParseError>; section dispatch on keyword
expr.rs Pratt parser fn parse_expr(&mut self,min_bp:u8)->Result<Expr,ParseError>; Case/next()/Const/Var
sema.rs fn check(m:&SmvModule)->Result<(),ParseError>: symbol table, type inference, CircularDefine (Tarjan/DFS), OutOfRange, next-context guard
error.rs ParseError{kind:ParseErrorKind,span:Span,msg:String}, ParseErrorKind
  • Token enum (TokenKind): Kw(Keyword), Ident(Atom), Int(i64), Assign(:=), DotDot(..), Colon Semi Comma LParen RParen LBrace RBrace, Op(BinOp), Bang(!/not), Eof. Comments + whitespace skipped in Lexer::next.
  • Operator precedence (Pratt binding power, low->high): <-> < -> (right-assoc) < |/or < &/and < =,!=,<,<=,>,>= < +,- < *,/,mod < unary !/-. BinOp ⊇ {And Or Xor Imp Iff Eq Neq Lt Le Gt Ge Add Sub Mul Mod}.
  • VAR: name : Type ; -> VarDecl. Type ::= "boolean" | "{" id ("," id)* "}" | int ".." int. Enum atoms interned; Range(lo,hi) requires lo<=hi else BadType.
  • ASSIGN: init(x):=e -> Init; next(x):=e -> Next; x:=e -> Plain. LHS must be a declared var (checked in sema).
  • DEFINE: name := expr ; -> DefineDecl (no own type; type inferred from body, memoized).
  • INVAR/TRANS: bare Expr; TRANS may contain next().
  • CTLSPEC/LTLSPEC: capture the raw substring to end-of-statement into SpecDecl::Ctl(String)/Ltl(String) (re-parsed by smv-logic Phase 9); INVARSPEC e -> SpecDecl::InvarSpec(Expr) parsed here. (B3: SMV input language syntax; R2: NuSMV reference grammar.)
  • sema pass: (1) build symbol table Atom -> Decl{Var(VarType)|Define}; duplicate decl -> BadType. (2) DFS the DEFINE dependency graph; back-edge -> CircularDefine. (3) type-infer each Expr bottom-up to Ty{Bool|Int|EnumOf(set)}; mismatched BinOp operands -> TypeMismatch; Var not in table -> UnknownVar; int literal outside a Range/enum domain at assignment/comparison -> OutOfRange. (4) next() allowed only inside TRANS or Next RHS; under Init/init() -> ParseErrorKind::UnexpectedToken (next-in-init). (I-P7: errors, never panic on untrusted model input; ADR-26: hand-written parser, no lalrpop/pest.)

3.3 Deliverable

smv-syntax crate exporting parse_module(src:&str)->Result<SmvModule,ParseError> (lex+parse+sema). smv encode --parse-only model.smv (CLI hook, Phase 0 stub now wired) prints a normalized AST dump or the first ParseError with file:line:col: kind: msg. Builds no_std+alloc; cargo test -p smv-syntax green.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Round-trips the full corpus (≥20 models): every valid model -> Ok(SmvModule) with exact var/define/assign/spec counts asserted.
  • Every malformed fixture -> the expected ParseErrorKind at the expected (line,col) (±0 line). No panic on any input incl. fuzz.
  • Pratt precedence: a -> b | c & d parses as a -> (b | (c & d)); a = b = c rejected (non-assoc =) or left-assoc per locked grammar — assert chosen tree.
  • Type checker: x + TRUE (Int+Bool) -> TypeMismatch; next(y) under init() -> rejected; cyclic a:=b; b:=a -> CircularDefine.
  • Lex/parse 10k-line model < 50 ms; sema < 50 ms; total parse memory < 10× source bytes.

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expects
traffic.smv Ok: 3 vars (1 enum, 1 bool, 1 range), 2 defines, 5 assigns, 1 CTLSPEC
mutex.smv Ok: enum {idle,trying,critical}, TRANS with next(), 1 LTLSPEC
counter.smv Ok: Range(0,7), next(c):=(c+1) mod 8, INVAR c<=7
bad_unknown_var.smv Err(UnknownVar) at the offending Var span
bad_type_mismatch.smv Err(TypeMismatch): bool & int
bad_circular_define.smv Err(CircularDefine): p:=q; q:=p
bad_out_of_range.smv Err(OutOfRange): assign 9 to Range(0,7)
bad_next_in_init.smv Err(UnexpectedToken): init(x):=next(y)
bad_unclosed_case.smv Err(UnexpectedToken): missing esac
empty.smv Ok: empty SmvModule (all vecs empty)

Each .smv paired with a golden .ast (AST dump) or .err (kind+span). Diff-tested.

3.6 Pitfalls

  • Maximal-munch lexing: := vs :, .. vs ., <->/<=/< — greedy longest-match; root cause = char-at-a-time tokenizing splits <-> into <,-,>. Debug: dump token stream first.
  • next() keyword vs function: next is both an ASSIGN LHS marker (next(x):=) and the Expr::Next operator; disambiguate by parse context, not token kind. Confusion -> next(x):=e mis-parsed as Next(Var x) := e. Debug: assert LHS arm reached.
  • Define vs var in symbol table: a DEFINE used where a next() target is expected is illegal (defines aren't state); silently allowing it corrupts encoding later. Catch in sema with a Decl tag check.
  • Type inference for enums: comparing two different enum domains (s=t, disjoint sets) must be TypeMismatch, not silently false; root cause = treating all enums as one Ty. Tag EnumOf(domain-id).
  • Pratt right-assoc ->: implementing -> as left-assoc yields wrong CTL/LTL semantics downstream. Verify a->b->c == a->(b->c).
  • Spec substring capture: capturing CTLSPEC body by token boundaries can drop trailing parens; capture by source byte-range to the statement terminator instead.

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • Single-pass lexer, O(n) over bytes; one-token lookahead (peek) — no backtracking in the recursive-descent core.
  • Intern identifiers to Atom(u32) at lex time -> symbol-table lookups and equality are O(1) hash/int compare, not string compare.
  • Pratt parser: O(tokens) with bounded recursion depth = expression nesting; guard depth (≤256) to bound stack on adversarial ((((…)))).
  • Sema type-infer + define-cycle DFS: O(V+E) over the define graph; memoize inferred Ty per define. Target: 10k-line model parsed+checked < 100 ms total.

3.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty module / section-only-keyword (VAR then nothing) -> empty vec, Ok.
  • Range(n,n) single-value -> valid 1-element domain. Range with lo>hi -> BadType.
  • 1-element enum {x}; duplicate enum atoms {a,a} -> BadType.
  • Trailing comment with no newline at EOF; CRLF vs LF; tabs in indentation (col counting).
  • Deeply nested case … esac and chained next(next(x)) (illegal nesting -> UnexpectedToken).
  • Integer overflow on literal beyond i64 -> OutOfRange at lex.
  • INVAR/TRANS/DEFINE appearing multiple times -> concatenated (vecs), legal.

3.9 Failure Analysis

  • Silent mis-typing (e.g. enum-as-bool) -> Phase 4 encoder allocates wrong bit width -> wrong FSM. Mitigation: sema is mandatory; parse_module never returns an unchecked AST.
  • Wrong precedence -> wrong formula tree -> wrong model-checking verdict (no crash). Mitigation: precedence golden-tree tests in 3.4.
  • Panic on malformed input = robustness bug; all error paths return ParseError. Fuzz target asserts no panic.
  • Span drift (off-by-one line/col) -> misleading diagnostics; tracked by Span unit tests on multi-line fixtures.
  • Atom interner collision/leak -> aliasing distinct names; use a collision-free interner keyed by exact bytes.

3.10 Operational Notes

  • Telemetry on AST dump: counts of vars/defines/assigns/specs, max expr depth, define-graph size — feeds smv bdd-stats/encode sizing.
  • ParseError Display impl emits file:line:col: <kind>: <msg> (gcc-style) for CLI; machine-readable kind for the differential harness (Phase 13 vs NuSMV parse acceptance).
  • Crate is no_std+alloc: no std::fs; CLI passes &str source. Deterministic — identical source -> byte-identical AST dump (stable iteration order, sorted symbol table dump).
  • Logged at debug: token stream + post-sema symbol table behind a trace-parse feature flag for debugging precedence/span issues.

16. Phase 4 — Boolean encoding of finite-domain variables

Bridges smv-syntax AST to smv-bdd: lower every finite-domain VarDecl to a contiguous block of boolean BDD levels (VarEncoding), build present/next VarMaps, and compile every Expr to a Bdd over present levels (over next levels inside Next(..)). Output is the substrate Phase 5 conjoins into init/trans. Load-bearing correctness: encode then decode is identity over the original variable domains.

4.1 Objectives

  • Encoder in smv-model: allocate per-var VarEncoding { levels, ty } covering Boolean (1 bit), Enum(atoms) / scalar (ceil(log2 |atoms|) bits + out-of-range invariant), Range(lo,hi) (ceil(log2 (hi-lo+1)) bits, offset lo).
  • Interleaved present/next level layout s0 s0' s1 s1' … per encoded bit → small trans BDDs (Phase 5/6 dependency); produce present: VarMap, next: VarMap.
  • compile_expr(&Expr) -> Bdd over present levels; Next(e) recurses with the present→next VarMap. Case → right-fold nested ite. Arithmetic/relational BinOp → ripple-carry/comparator BDD circuits.
  • Decode: cube_to_state(&Bdd) -> StateAssignment inverts the encoding; round-trip is total over in-range valuations.
  • Invariant: encoding is injective per var; padding codes (Enum/Range whose count is not a power of two) are excluded by the out-of-range constraint, surfaced into EncodedFsm.invar.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files (per §10 repo layout, crate smv-model):

File Contents
src/encoding.rs / src/varmap.rs VarEncoding, bit-width math, encode/decode of a single var value ↔ bit vector; re-export smv-bdd::VarMap, build interleaved present/next maps, swap/rename helper
src/encoder.rs Encoder struct, level allocator, compile_expr, Case/BinOp circuit builders, out-of-range constraint
src/state.rs StateAssignment, cube_to_state decode

Core signatures (smv-model):

pub struct Encoder<'m> { mgr: &'m mut BddManager, enc: HashMap<Atom, VarEncoding>,
                         present: VarMap, next: VarMap, next_level: u32 }
impl<'m> Encoder<'m> {
    pub fn declare(&mut self, v: &VarDecl) -> Result<(), EncodeError>; // allocates levels, builds maps
    pub fn width(ty: &VarType) -> u32;                                 // 1 / ceil(log2|E|) / ceil(log2 span)
    pub fn out_of_range(&mut self) -> Bdd;                             // AND of per-var legal-code masks
    pub fn compile_expr(&mut self, e: &Expr, ctx: Ctx) -> Bdd;         // Ctx = Present | Next
    pub fn encode_const(&mut self, enc: &VarEncoding, v: &Value) -> Bdd; // value -> minterm over its bits
}

Algorithms:

  • Width: Boolean→1; Enum→ceil(log2 max(1,|atoms|)); Range(lo,hi)→ceil(log2 (hi-lo+1)), store lo offset. |atoms|==1 ⇒ 0 bits forbidden; clamp to 1 bit + constant constraint. (ADR-10: log2-encoding chosen over one-hot; see ADR-10 for when one-hot wins.)
  • Level alloc: for each declared bit, present gets level 2k, next level 2k+1; present[level]→next[level] VarMap for rename. mgr.ithvar(level) lazily realizes projection. (I-P9: variable ordering is critical; interleaving present/next is the canonical heuristic for synchronous models.)
  • encode_const: integer code c (Range: v-lo; Enum: index in declared order) → AND over bits b_i of ithvar or its not, MSB-first. Returns a single-minterm Bdd over that var's bits. (R2: NuSMV's encoder in src/enc/ uses the same bit-coding strategy.)
  • Var read in Expr: Var(a) → multi-bit "value bus" reified on demand for comparisons; equality Var==Const → encode_const; Var==Var → bitwise XNOR-conjunction.
  • BinOp circuits: Eq/Ne via XNOR/XOR fold; Lt/Le/Gt/Ge via borrow-chain comparator (MSB→LSB) emitting one ite per bit; Plus/Minus (Range) via ripple-carry adder producing a result bus, then re-encoded — width-checked vs target var, overflow → OutOfRange. (B4 §2: CNF/BDD encoding of arithmetic constraints via ite-based circuits.)
  • Case: list [(g1,e1)..(gn,en)] right-folds ite(g1, e1, ite(g2, e2, … default)); missing default ⇒ synthesize bdd_false guard sentinel and emit TypeMismatch if uncovered at non-boolean type. (I-P3: ite is the universal primitive — Case compilation is direct.)
  • out_of_range: per Enum/Range var, legal-code set = OR of its 2^width valid minterms; mask = that set; conjoin all ⇒ domain constraint, handed to Phase 5 for invar. (I-P1/ADR-10: out-of-range constraint is mandatory to prevent phantom reachable states that corrupt CTL/LTL verdicts.)

4.3 Deliverable

smv-model::Encoder compiling any type-checked SmvModule var section into VarEncodings + present/next VarMaps, and any Expr (incl. Case, Next, arithmetic BinOp) into a Bdd. cube_to_state decode included. CLI smv encode model.smv prints per-var level ranges, total bit width, and node_count/satcount of a sample compiled expression. Unit-runnable; no checking engine yet.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Round-trip for every in-range valuation v: cube_to_state(encode_const(v)) == v (exhaustive ≤ 2^16 states)
Bit width Range(0,255)→8 bits; Enum of 5→3 bits; Boolean→1; matches ceil(log2)
Out-of-range Enum of 3 (2 bits) ⇒ satcount(out_of_range_var, 2) == 3; 1 illegal code excluded
Expr correctness compiled Bdd for ≥50 random Exprs equals brute-force truth table over all valuations
Interleaving present level 2k, next level 2k+1 for bit k; verified by VarMap dump
Const encode encode_const yields exactly one minterm: satcount == 1 over the var's bits

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
enc/bool_var.smv VAR x: boolean → 1 present level, 1 next level; out_of_range ≡ bdd_true
enc/enum5.smv VAR s: {a,b,c,d,e} → 3 bits; 3 illegal codes; satcount(legal,3)==5
enc/range.smv VAR n: 3..10 → 3 bits, offset 3; n=10 decodes to value 10
enc/case_expr.smv nested case compiles to ite chain; matches reference truth table
enc/next_expr.smv next(x)=!x compiles over next levels; references next VarMap only
enc/add_overflow.smv + enc/case_uncovered.smv (malformed) next(n):=n+5 with n:0..3 ⇒ OutOfRange at compile, no panic; case with no default at scalar type ⇒ TypeMismatch
enc/enum_pow2.smv {a,b,c,d} (4) → 2 bits, 0 illegal codes; out_of_range ≡ bdd_true

4.6 Pitfalls

  • Bit-order inconsistency — encode MSB-first, decode LSB-first (or vice-versa) silently maps value v to a different code; round-trip passes if symmetric but Lt/adder circuits break. Root cause: two ad-hoc loops. Fix: single canonical MSB-first helper used by both; assert in round-trip fuzz.
  • |atoms|==1 → 0-width — ceil(log2 1)=0 allocates no levels; the var vanishes from trans, fixpoints ignore it. Fix: clamp width ≥1, add var == only_value constant constraint. Debug: width() unit test on singleton enum.
  • Forgotten out-of-range constraint — non-power-of-two Enum/Range leaves illegal codes as reachable phantom states ⇒ spurious counterexamples in Phase 7. Root cause: constraint built but never conjoined into invar. Debug: satcount(reachable) exceeds prod(domain sizes).
  • Present/next aliasing — compiling next(x) against the present VarMap makes trans reference present levels twice ⇒ image quantifies the wrong vars. Fix: Ctx::Next threads the next map; assert compiled Bdd's support ⊆ next levels.
  • Range offset dropped (Range(3,10) encoded as raw 0..7 then compared without +3 offset ⇒ off-by-lo in relational ops; fix: offset normalization at encode_const/decode only, circuits operate on normalized codes) and adder/comparator width mismatch (n+m with different operand widths needs zero-extension to max(w)+1, truncating drops carry; debug: differential vs Python int math on random valuations).

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • Encoding is one-time, pre-fixpoint; not hot. Still: compile_expr is memoized by Expr ptr-identity to avoid rebuilding shared DEFINE subtrees.
  • Interleaved present/next order keeps later trans node_count ~linear in total bits; non-interleaved (all present then all next) blows and_exists intermediates exponentially — locked choice.
  • Comparator/adder circuits are O(width) nodes each; cap practical var width at ~32 bits (Range span ≤ 2^32) — wider domains warn (bit-width > 32) since BMC/BDD memory grows with bus width.
  • out_of_range built once and cached; rebuilding per phase wastes 2^width minterm constructions per var.

4.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty Enum (|atoms|==0) → reject upstream (BadType) — never reaches Encoder; Range(lo,hi) with lo==hi → 1 value, clamp to 1 bit + constant constraint (same as singleton enum).
  • Range with negative lo (e.g. -2..2) → offset by lo, encode span hi-lo+1=5 → 3 bits.
  • Boolean already canonical — width 1, out_of_range contributes bdd_true (no illegal code).
  • Power-of-two domains (|E|∈{2,4,8,…}) → zero illegal codes; out_of_range_var ≡ bdd_true (assert, don't allocate).
  • next(const) / Next wrapping a constant → context-independent Bdd; Next(Next(..)) → reject (UnexpectedToken, only single-step next supported).

4.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Symptom Detection Recovery
Non-injective encoding round-trip loses values exhaustive round-trip test abort build; spec corrupt
Missing range constraint phantom states reachable satcount > domain product conjoin out_of_range; re-verify
Wrong Ctx for next() image quantifies wrong vars support-set assertion on compiled Bdd fix Ctx threading
Arith overflow undetected wraparound value in trans width check vs target raise OutOfRange at compile
Bit-order mismatch comparators give wrong order differential vs int math unify MSB-first helper

Gravest here: a non-injective or under-constrained encoding feeds every downstream phase wrong state sets — verdicts are wrong with no crash. Encode/decode round-trip + domain satcount are the load-bearing gates.

4.10 Operational Notes

  • smv encode model.smv --dump-levels prints var → [present levels], [next levels], width, offset, #illegal-codes; smv encode model.smv --dot expr exports a chosen compiled Expr's BDD via smv-bdd dot export for inspection.
  • Determinism: level allocation follows VarDecl source order; identical input ⇒ identical level assignment (reproducible BDDs, stable golden files).
  • RUST_LOG=smv_model=debug logs per-var width decisions and out_of_range satcount.
  • Metrics emitted for Phase 13: total state bits, per-var widths, illegal-code counts, sample-expr node_count — fed into bdd-stats.

17. Phase 5 — Building the symbolic FSM (init + transition relation)

Consumes Phase 4's Encoder (boolean-encoded vars, present/next VarMaps, compile_expr -> Bdd); emits the EncodedFsm that every later phase (reachability, CTL, LTL, BMC) treats as ground truth. This phase turns syntax (init()/next()/TRANS/INVAR/FAIRNESS) into four/five BDDs + a partitioned transition relation. Boundary: encoding of individual Exprs belongs to Phase 4; assembling them into init/trans/invar/fairness lives here. No fixpoints yet (Phase 6).

5.1 Objectives

  • Build EncodedFsm { present, next, init, trans, invar, fairness, ap } for any well-typed SmvModule — the single FSM artifact all checkers consume.
  • init = conj of init(x):=e predicates AND all INVAR AND default-init for unconstrained vars (full domain); over present vars only.
  • trans = conj of next(x):=e relations AND TRANS clauses AND next-state INVAR(next) AND functional-totality for unconstrained vars; relates present↔next.
  • Functional vs relational unification: next(x):=e → x' ↔ enc(e); TRANS expr → arbitrary relation; both fold into the same trans.
  • Conjunctive partitioning: store trans as Vec<TransPart> (per-var/per-clause partial relations + each part's quantifiable cube), NEVER materialize the monolith — image (Phase 6) quantifies part-by-part.
  • fairness: Vec<Bdd> = one state-set Bdd per FAIRNESS/JUSTICE constraint, for Emerson-Lei (Phase 10/12).
  • Forward ap (atomic-prop → Bdd) from DEFINE/named props for smv-logic binding.

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Crate smv-model, new module src/fsm.rs (alongside Phase 4's encode.rs). New/extended types and free functions:

// smv-model/src/fsm.rs
pub struct TransPart { pub rel: Bdd, pub quant_cube: Bdd, pub support: SupportMask } // partial relation + vars existable after it
pub struct FsmBuilder<'a> { enc: &'a Encoder, mgr: &'a mut BddManager }     // wraps Phase-4 Encoder
impl FsmBuilder<'_> {
    pub fn build(&mut self, m: &SmvModule) -> Result<EncodedFsm, EncodeError>;
    fn build_init(&mut self, m: &SmvModule) -> Bdd;          // init() ASSIGNs ∧ INVAR ∧ defaults
    fn build_trans_parts(&mut self, m: &SmvModule) -> Vec<TransPart>;   // one part per next-assign/TRANS clause
    fn conjoin_invar_next(&mut self, invar: Bdd) -> Bdd;     // rename(present→next) of INVAR
    fn build_fairness(&mut self, m: &SmvModule) -> Vec<Bdd>;
}
pub fn monolithic_trans(mgr:&mut BddManager, parts:&[TransPart]) -> Bdd; // DEBUG/BMC only — never on hot path

Steps (ordered):

  1. INVAR fold: invar = AND_i compile_expr(INVAR_i) over present; mgr.bdd_true() if none. (I-P5: symbolic — state sets are BDDs, not lists.)
  2. init: for each AssignKind::Init x:=e → eq(enc(x), compile_expr(e)) via XNOR over x's bit-levels; AND together; AND invar. Vars with no init() stay unconstrained (full domain) — init must not pin them. (B6 §2: McMillan's original SMV encoding of init() as a predicate over state 0.)
  3. next-relation per var: AssignKind::Next x:=e → part rel = (x' ↔ compile_next(e)) where compile_next evaluates e mapping any Next(_)/var refs through the next VarMap; bit-wise XNOR of next-bits(x) with enc(e). Plain x:=e (combinational) inlines into both init and trans as x ↔ e (present-only equality), not a frame. (R2: NuSMV src/enc/BoolEnc.c uses the same XNOR-per-bit equality encoding.)
  4. TRANS clauses: TRANS expr → part rel = compile_expr(expr) (already mixes present+next()); each its own TransPart. (ADR-11: conjunctive partitioning; P10: Burch-Clarke-Long partition strategy.)
  5. next-INVAR: part rel = rename(invar, present→next) so successors also satisfy invariants. Quantify-last.
  6. unconstrained-frame: vars with neither next() nor a TRANS mention them → free (any next value within domain) plus out-of-range constraint from Phase 4; do NOT auto-add a stutter/identity frame (SMV semantics = unconstrained, not frozen). (I-P1: spurious states via missing out-of-range constraint is a canonicity/reachability bug.)
  7. partition assembly: each TransPart.quant_cube = present-bits of vars whose every occurrence is subsumed by parts ≤ current (early-quant candidates); leave conservative (empty cube) here, refine schedule in Phase 12. (P9/P10: early quantification schedule deferred to Phase 12 sifting.)
  8. fairness: each SpecDecl/section FAIRNESS f → compile_expr(f) present-only Bdd; collect. (P4: Emerson-Lei fair-cycle detection requires the fairness predicates as BDDs.)
  9. assemble: EncodedFsm { present, next, init, trans: monolithic_trans(parts) OR store parts, invar, fairness, ap }. Store Vec<TransPart> as the primary form; trans: Bdd field holds either the monolith (small models) or a sentinel; image path reads parts. (ADR-11: never materialize monolith on the hot path; I-P5: symbolic reachability consumes parts via and_exists.)

5.3 Deliverable

smv encode model.smv (CLI subcommand encode) prints, for any well-typed model: init node count, #trans parts + per-part node count, total support, fairness count, and bdd-stats. FsmBuilder::build returns a valid EncodedFsm whose init/parts/invar/fairness are construction-canonical BDDs. --dot init|trans exports via smv-bdd dot. Library API stable for Phase 6+.

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Correctness init/trans agree with hand-computed truth tables on all ≤12-bit fixtures (exhaustive)
Monolith equivalence monolithic_trans(parts) ≡ reference monolithic build (O(1) handle eq) on every fixture
Partitioning trans stored as ≥1 part; no code path materializes monolith during build for models >16 state bits
Default-init every var lacking init() is free in init (proved: exists otherbits . init over that var's bits == its domain)
INVAR enforcement init AND not invar == bdd_false; (exists present.trans) AND not rename(invar,p→n) == bdd_false
Determinism byte-identical stats across 100 runs (no hashmap-order leak into node order)
Build time 1000-var model FSM build < 2 s; trans parts total < 5e5 nodes

5.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Content Expected
tests/fixtures/mod3.smv x: 0..2; init(x):=0; next(x):=(x+1) mod 3 init={x=0} (1 cube); trans deterministic, 3 part-cubes; satcount(trans)=3
tests/fixtures/twobit_counter.smv 2 booleans, ripple next trans functional, 4 successors total
tests/fixtures/invar_clip.smv Range(0,5) + INVAR x<=3 init/trans exclude x∈{4,5}; next-INVAR drops bad successors
tests/fixtures/mutex.smv 2 procs, TRANS interleaving trans relational (nondeterministic), ≥1 state has 2 successors
tests/fixtures/fair_proc.smv FAIRNESS running fairness.len()==1, equals enc(running)
tests/fixtures/no_next.smv var with no next/TRANS var free in trans (both next-values reachable)
Malformed bad_next_self.smv (next(x):=next(y) cycle); next_init.smv (init(x):=next(y)) EncodeError::CircularNext; reject — next() illegal in init()
Golden *.fsm.txt snapshots: init/trans/fairness node counts + satcount exact match

5.6 Pitfalls

  • Frame fallacy: silently adding x'==x for unmentioned vars → wrong semantics (SMV leaves them free). Symptom: missing successors, spurious "AG safe" passes. Debug: satcount(trans) too low; diff vs NuSMV reachable set.
  • INVAR only on present: forgetting renamed next-INVAR lets fixpoints walk into invariant-violating states → bogus counterexamples. Debug: check successor of a boundary state violates INVAR.
  • next() evaluated against present map: compile_next must route var refs through the next VarMap only inside Next(_), primary occurrences stay present; mixing them corrupts every relation. Debug: trans support has wrong present/next split.
  • Premature monolith conjunction: AND-ing all parts during build defeats the whole point and blows up on real models (>2^30 nodes). Symptom: OOM at build, not at check. Keep parts as Vec.
  • Nondeterministic part order (iterating a HashMap<var,..> for parts → non-reproducible node counts + nondeterministic GC timing; use the encoder's stable var index order) and out-of-range leak (enum/range encodings from Phase 4 have unused bit patterns; if init/trans don't AND the out-of-range constraint, fixpoints reach phantom states — debug via satcount(reachable) > product of declared domains).

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • Build cost dominated by repeated and/xnor; reuse compute cache — do not drop/rebuild manager mid-build.
  • Conjunctive partition node count << monolith: target each part ≤ a few k nodes; monolith can be 10^6–10^9. Never call monolithic_trans except on ≤16-bit debug models.
  • XNOR-per-bit equality is O(bits); for a w-bit var that is w ites — cheap. Range arithmetic (mod,+) from Phase 4 may dominate; cache encoded subexprs.
  • Order parts by ascending support to help Phase 6 early-quant; cheap to precompute SupportMask (bitset over levels) per part here.
  • fairness BDDs are small (state predicates); negligible.

5.8 Edge Cases

  • Model with no next/TRANS at all → trans = all vars free ∧ next-INVAR (every in-range successor); valid, not empty.
  • init empty (no init(), no constraining INVAR) → init == bdd_true (all states initial).
  • INVAR false → init == bdd_false (no initial states); checkers must treat as vacuous, not crash.
  • Single combinational Plain x:=e with no state vars → trans over empty present/next cube = bdd_true.
  • TRANS FALSE → trans deadlock (no successors); legal, flagged in bdd-stats (deadlock warning), feeds Phase 8.
  • Boolean-only model → present/next maps 1:1, no out-of-range bits; two init(x) for same x → type checker (Phase 3) rejects (MultipleAssign), fsm assumes single.

5.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Canonicity break upstream (Phase 1/2 bug) golden node counts drift; monolithic_trans(parts) ≠ ref abort: poison FSM, surface as internal error — never emit wrong verdict
BDD blowup on real trans part per-part node-count cap exceeded warn + continue (partitioning saves it); suggest reorder (Phase 12)
CircularNext/next-in-init dependency DFS over assigns before building EncodeError with var cycle path; no partial FSM
OOM during build; or empty init ∧ user expects states manager alloc fail/GC churn; or satcount(init)==0 logged fail fast with stats (which part, node count), deterministic/reproducible; or non-fatal warning, vacuous-pass semantics documented

5.10 Operational Notes

  • smv encode model.smv --stats → init/trans/fairness node counts, #parts, support sizes, satcount(init); --dot init|trans|part:N.
  • EncodedFsm is the snapshot boundary: golden *.fsm.txt files freeze build output; regressions caught pre-fixpoint.
  • Logging: SMV_LOG=fsm emits per-part node count + cumulative support as parts are built (spot blowup early).
  • Determinism contract: identical input ⇒ identical part order, node counts, satcount; CI asserts byte-identical stats.
  • Feature flag force-monolith (debug only) builds the monolith for differential checks against parts; off in release. Forward to Phase 6: image reads Vec<TransPart> + next/present VarMaps; nothing else recomputes the relation.

18. Phase 6 — Symbolic reachability and the fixpoint framework

Forward/backward image over the partitioned trans (from Phase 5) + the reachable lfp, plus a reusable fixpoint engine that Phase 7 (CTL) consumes verbatim. State SETS are BDDs; never enumerate (NON-GOAL #279). All work lands in smv-check.

6.1 Objectives

  • Img(S) = (exists present.(trans AND S))[next->present] via and_exists over partitioned trans + early-quantification schedule; pre-image is the dual (quantify next).
  • Reachable = lfp Z. init OR Img(Z), iterated to BDD-handle convergence (new == prev, O(1)).
  • Frontier simplification: image only the new frontier (reached AND not prev), not the whole set; preserves least fixpoint.
  • Factor a generic Fixpoint framework (lfp/gfp, monotone step, convergence test) reused unchanged by CtlChecker::check (Phase 7) — single iteration engine, no duplication.
  • NON-GOAL here: CTL/LTL semantics (Phases 7/10), counterexamples (Phase 8). This phase = reachability + the engine only.

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • smv-check/src/image.rs — pub struct Image<'a> { mgr: &'a mut BddManager, fsm: &'a EncodedFsm }; precompute present-cube pcube & next-cube ncube (fold and over ithvar) once at construction. (P3: image computation is the core of symbolic model checking — "reachable = lfp Z.init∨Img(Z)".)
  • fn forward(&mut self, s: Bdd) -> Bdd — for each conjunct Ti in fsm.trans_parts (Phase 5 Vec<Bdd>): acc = and_exists(acc, Ti, quant_i) per the schedule, then rename(acc, &fsm.next->present) (inverse of fsm.present/next VarMaps). Monolithic fallback when trans_parts.len()==1. (P9/ADR-12: and_exists fused relational product with early quantification is the hot path.)
  • fn backward(&mut self, s: Bdd) -> Bdd — pre-image: rename s present→next first, then and_exists conjuncts quantifying ncube; symmetric to forward. Used by CTL EX. (B1 §5: pre-image is ∃V'. T(V,V') ∧ S(V') renaming V'→V.)
  • smv-check/src/schedule.rs — pub struct QuantSchedule { order: Vec<usize>, quant: Vec<Bdd> }; build(parts, support, pcube) orders conjuncts by support-overlap heuristic and assigns each level to the earliest conjunct after which no later conjunct mentions it (early quantification → smaller intermediates). Cube quant_i = vars dead after conjunct i. (P10: Burch-Clarke-Long early-quantification schedule.)
  • smv-check/src/fixpoint.rs — generic engine: (I-P5/I-P13: fixpoints on the BDD lattice; Tarski guarantees termination on the finite powerset lattice.) pub fn lfp<F: FnMut(&mut BddManager, Bdd) -> Bdd>(m:&mut BddManager, start:Bdd, mut step:F) -> Bdd pub fn gfp<F: ...>(m:&mut BddManager, start:Bdd, mut step:F) -> Bdd // start = bdd_true for gfp loop: next = step(m, z); if next == z { return z } z = next; — convergence is O(1) handle eq (canonicity, Phase 1). (I-P1: handle equality ↔ set equality makes the convergence test O(1).)
