  • smv-check/src/reach.rs — pub fn reachable(mgr, fsm) -> Bdd: lfp(init, |m,z| { let fr = and(z, not(prev)); let img = Image::forward(fr); or(z, img) }) with prev tracked across iters (frontier form). CtlChecker::reachable() delegates here. (P3: "Reachable = lfp Z.init∨Img(Z)" is the paper's exact construction.)
  • smv-cli reachable subcommand: parse→encode→reachable→print satcount(R, nvars) + node_count(R) + iteration count. (I-P7: recoverable error if node cap hit; never panic on legal model.)

6.3 Deliverable

smv reachable model.smv computes the reachable state-set BDD, terminating at convergence, and prints reachable-state count (satcount), BDD node_count, and #iterations. Image::{forward,backward} + fixpoint::{lfp,gfp} + reach::reachable are public, tested, and consumed by Phase 7.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Convergence reachable terminates; iters ≤ BFS diameter+1
Correctness satcount(R) matches hand-computed count on ≤4-var fixtures (exact)
Frontier == full frontier-form R ≡ naive lfp Z.init OR Img(Z) (handle eq) on all fixtures
Pre-image duality backward(forward(S)) ⊇ S reachable subset; EX true == preimage of reachable sanity
Engine reuse CtlChecker (Phase 7) calls fixpoint::lfp/gfp with zero forks
Scale 24-bit counter (16.7M states) reaches fixpoint <500 ms, peak node_count <50k

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
mod3_counter.smv (Range 0..2, +1 mod 3) satcount(R)=3, iters=3
counter8.smv (8-bit, init 0) satcount(R)=256, iters=256, monotone growth
two_proc_mutex.smv reachable ⊂ full; both-critical state ∉ R
disconnected.smv (unreachable enum value) that value's cube AND R == bdd_false
bidir_ring.smv forward then backward round-trips reachable core
Malformed: empty_trans.smv (trans=bdd_false) R==init, iters=1, warn "deadlock: no transitions"
Malformed: false_init.smv (init=bdd_false) R=bdd_false, satcount 0, iters=0

6.6 Pitfalls

  • Quantify wrong cube → renaming present→present (forgot rename) or quantifying next in forward image: silently wrong reachable set, often a superset → log support before/after each conjunct; assert support(Img) ⊆ present.
  • Frontier loses states if prev updated before computing img of current frontier, or frontier = not z (unbounded) instead of z AND not prev → R undercounts; debug by diffing frontier-form vs naive-lfp BDDs per iter.
  • Non-convergence / infinite loop from a non-monotone step (e.g. and instead of or in reachable, or stale GC handle) → assert or(z, next) == next (monotone) each iter; cap iters at 2^nvars+panic.
  • Stale cube after GC — pcube/ncube/quant_i are dead Bdd handles after mark-sweep (Phase 2) reclaims them → keep cubes rooted in a GC root set, or rebuild post-GC; symptom: panic/garbage on next and_exists.
  • Early-quant schedule unsound — quantifying a var still needed by a later conjunct drops constraints (wrong, not just slow) → schedule must only quantify vars absent from all remaining conjuncts; unit-test schedule against monolithic and_exists for equality.
  • bdd_false frontier treated as live → wasted iteration; short-circuit if fr == bdd_false { converged }.

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • Intermediate BDD blowup is THE bottleneck; partitioned and_exists + early quantification keep peak node_count orders below monolithic trans. Target: peak ≤ 4× final-R node count.
  • Frontier simplification cuts redundant re-imaging of converged states; expect ≥10× fewer and_exists ops on deep models vs full-set imaging.
  • Computed cache (Phase 2) hit-rate dominates; reachability reuses Img subresults across iters — do NOT clear cache between iters.
  • Conjunct order from QuantSchedule is the single biggest knob (can swing peak nodes 100×). bdd-stats reports per-iter peak node_count to tune it.

6.8 Edge Cases

  • init == bdd_false → R = bdd_false, satcount 0, iters 0.
  • trans == bdd_false (no enabled transitions) → R = init, iters 1 (deadlock).
  • Self-loop only (trans = identity) → R = init, fixpoint at iter 1.
  • Full reachability (R == bdd_true, all 2^nvars states) → satcount == 2^nvars; verify no overflow (u128 from satcount).
  • Single var, no INVAR → 2-state machine, iters ≤2.
  • Invariant restricts state space: image must intersect fsm.invar (already folded into Phase-5 trans/init) — assert R AND not invar == bdd_false.

6.9 Failure Analysis

  • Wrong satcount, no crash — gravest: broken canonicity (Phase 1) or wrong quant cube makes == lie → fixpoint converges to wrong set. Detect via differential check vs naive-lfp form and vs ≤4-var brute-force enumeration in tests.
  • Premature convergence — handle eq true while sets differ ⇒ canonicity bug upstream; cross-check satcount strictly increases until convergence in the counter fixtures.
  • OOM / node explosion — bad schedule or monolithic fallback on a wide trans; mark-sweep GC + reorder (Phase 12) mitigate; emit peak-node trace before abort.
  • Diverging iters > diameter — non-canonical or non-monotone step; panic with iter count + node_count snapshot.

6.10 Operational Notes

  • smv reachable --stats model.smv → per-iteration table (iter, frontier node_count, R node_count, cumulative satcount).
  • RUST_LOG=smv_check::reach=debug traces each fixpoint step + support set; --dot R.dot exports final R via BddManager dot export (Phase 2).
  • Cube/VarMap construction is one-time per model; reachability is the hot inner loop — profile and_exists/rename, not setup.
  • Determinism: fixed var order + fixed conjunct schedule ⇒ identical R handle and satcount across runs; required for Phase 13 differential testing vs NuSMV.
  • fixpoint::{lfp,gfp} is the reuse contract — Phase 7 EU=lfp, EG=gfp call it directly; any change here is an API break for smv-check.

19. Phase 7 — CTL symbolic model checking (EX/EU/EG and derived)

CTL semantics over the symbolic Kripke structure: evaluate every CtlFormula to the state-set Bdd of states satisfying it (sat(f)). Builds directly on Phase 6's preimage + lfp/gfp iteration framework. Verdict for a CTLSPEC f: holds iff init ⊆ sat(f), i.e. (init AND not sat(f)) == bdd_false. No state enumeration — sets only.

7.1 Objectives

  • CtlChecker::check(&CtlFormula) -> Bdd — bottom-up structural recursion over NNF formula, returns sat(f) (state-set over present vars).
  • Three fixpoint primitives: EX p = preimage(p); E[p U q] = lfp Z. q OR (p AND EX Z); EG p = gfp Z. p AND EX Z.
  • Derive EF/AF/AG/AX/A[U] via the locked identities (table in 7.2) — no independent fixpoints for derived ops.
  • Verdict API: CtlChecker::holds(&CtlFormula) -> bool via init AND not sat(f) == bdd_false.
  • All sat-sets restricted to reachable states is optional optimization (7.7), NOT a semantics change: full state space is the universe; restriction only shrinks intermediate BDDs.
  • Out of scope: counterexample/witness traces (Phase 8), LTL (Phases 9–10), fairness in CTL (EG here is unfair; fair-CTL deferred to Phase 12).

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Crate smv-check, files: src/ctl.rs (CtlChecker, check, holds), src/fixpoint.rs (Phase 6 lfp/gfp helpers, reused). CtlChecker<'a> holds mgr: &'a mut BddManager, fsm: &'a EncodedFsm, and a memo cache: HashMap<*const CtlFormula, Bdd> (pointer-keyed, NNF formula is immutable during a check). (B1 §4/B2 §6.3: CTL semantics over Kripke structures; B3 §3.4: CTL* fragment we implement.)

Primitive signatures (lightweight):

fn ex(&mut self, p: Bdd) -> Bdd;               // preimage(p) = (∃ next. trans ∧ p[present→next])   (P3/B1 §5)
fn eu(&mut self, p: Bdd, q: Bdd) -> Bdd;       // lfp Z. q ∨ (p ∧ ex(Z)), seed bdd_false             (P3: EU as lfp)
fn eg(&mut self, p: Bdd) -> Bdd;               // gfp Z. p ∧ ex(Z),       seed bdd_true (or p)       (P3: EG as gfp; Tarski justification in MM5)

ex = Phase 6 preimage: rename p present→next, and_exists(trans, p_next, next_cube) quantifying next vars; one call. eu iterates Z₀=⊥, Zₙ₊₁ = q OR (p AND ex(Zₙ)) until Zₙ₊₁ == Zₙ (O(1) handle eq). (I-P1: handle equality makes convergence test O(1).) eg iterates Z₀=⊤ (or Z₀=p), Zₙ₊₁ = p AND ex(Zₙ) until fixed. (EG requires gfp not lfp — see Hard Questions Q9 for the 3-state example showing why.)

Derived-operator lowering (applied during check recursion, NNF-driven):

Formula Identity (in terms of EX/EU/EG/¬/∧/∨)
EF q E[true U q] = eu(⊤, q)
AX p ¬ EX ¬p
AG p ¬ EF ¬p = ¬ eu(⊤, ¬p)
AF p ¬ EG ¬p
A[p U q] ¬( E[¬q U (¬p ∧ ¬q)] ∨ EG ¬q )
EG p eg(p) (primitive)
EX p ex(p) (primitive)
E[p U q] eu(p, q) (primitive)

Ap(Bdd) returns the bound AP Bdd verbatim (Phase 4/smv-logic). Not/And/Or map to mgr.not/and/or on child sat-sets. holds: let s = self.check(f); self.mgr.and(self.fsm.init, self.mgr.not(s)) == self.mgr.bdd_false().

7.3 Deliverable

CtlChecker::check + holds in smv-check, wired into smv-cli check (CTL path). smv check model.smv prints per-spec CTLSPEC <f> ... true/false (counterexample line is a Phase-8 stub here). All 12 derived/primitive operators evaluated by structural recursion to a canonical state-set Bdd; verdict via init ⊆ sat(f).

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Every operator (EX EU EG EF AF AG AX A[U] + boolean) produces the same verdict as NuSMV on the 7.5 corpus (≥ 30 spec/model pairs).
  • eu/eg fixpoints converge in ≤ diameter+1 iterations (eu) / ≤ node_count(p)-bounded steps (eg); assert monotonic node-set growth (eu) / shrink (eg) — non-monotone iteration is a bug.
  • holds(AG p) == holds(¬EF ¬p) and holds(AF p) == holds(¬EG ¬p) (duality self-check) on all fixtures.
  • sat(EX ⊤) == preimage(⊤) == { s | ∃ successor } (= all states with outgoing edge); sat(EF ⊥)=⊥, sat(EG ⊤)=⊤.
  • Verdict for a model with |reachable| = 10^6–10^9 states computes in < 2 s with node counts logged via bdd-stats.

7.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Spec(s) Expected
tests/ctl/mutex.smv AG !(c1 & c2), AG AF c1 true, true
tests/ctl/counter4.smv AF (val=0), EF (val=3), EG (val<4) true, true, true
tests/ctl/traffic.smv AG (g -> AX !r), EX g true, true
tests/ctl/until.smv E[req U ack], A[req U ack] true, false (req may stall)
tests/ctl/deadlock.smv EX true, AG EX true false at sink, false
tests/ctl/golden/*.out NuSMV -dcx verdict lines byte-diff after normalization

Malformed: spec referencing undeclared var → UnknownVar (Phase 3); EX with non-state arg, temporal op in a DEFINE → BadType; empty reachable set + AG p → vacuously true (assert, not error).

7.6 Pitfalls

  • Greatest-fixpoint seed: seeding eg with bdd_false (lfp seed) instead of bdd_true collapses to ⊥ — wrong verdict, no crash. Root cause: copy-paste from eu. Debug: assert Z₀ ⊇ Z₁ ⊇ … (shrinking) for gfp.
  • Pre-image vs image confusion: EX must quantify next vars after renaming present→next; using forward image (quantify present) silently computes successors-of, not predecessors-of. Debug: sat(EX p) on a known 2-state chain.
  • AP not over present vars: an AP Bdd accidentally built over next-var levels makes and(init, …) always ⊥. Root cause: wrong VarMap in Phase 4 binding. Debug: node_count/level dump of each ap entry.
  • Cache stale across formulas: pointer-keyed memo not cleared between top-level check calls on different formula trees that reuse an address → wrong sat-set. Fix: per-check memo or content hash.
  • not over the wrong universe: not p complements over all 2^nvars valuations including unreachable/illegal encodings; AG/AF derivations stay correct only because init/trans exclude them — never intersect sat with reachable before a not unless consistently. Debug: compare holds with and without reachable-restriction.
  • Vacuous truth on empty preimage: model with a deadlock sink (EX true == false there) makes AG AF p subtly false; engineers expect true. Not a bug — NuSMV agrees. Document.

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • ex is the hot op (called every fixpoint iter): reuse Phase 5 conjunctively-partitioned trans + early quantification so intermediate BDDs stay < 5× the operand size.
  • Restrict every sat-set to reachable (mgr.and(s, reachable)) before/after ex — shrinks eu/eg working sets by orders of magnitude on sparse models; semantics-preserving for the init ⊆ test.
  • eg converges from above; cap iterations and bail with diagnostic if > 10^4 (likely canonicity bug, not slow convergence).
  • Computed-cache hit rate target > 90% during fixpoints (consecutive ex calls share subgraphs); log misses in bdd-stats.
  • Memoize sat-sets of shared subformulas (DAG-CSE on CtlFormula) — AG p then AF p reuse sat(p).

7.8 Edge Cases

  • init == bdd_false (unsatisfiable init / vacuous model): every spec holds vacuously (⊥ ⊆ anything). Assert, don't divide-by-zero on satcount.
  • Tautology/contradiction specs: EF true == reachable≠∅, EG false == ⊥, AG true == ⊤ — short-circuit not required but verify.
  • Single-state self-loop model: EX p, EG p, AG p all reduce to p at that state; classic 1-node sanity fixture.
  • Deeply nested EX^k: depth-k preimage chain; ensure no recursion-depth blowup (iterative recursion over formula, fixpoints are loops).
  • Formula already in NNF with double negation: normalize once (Phase 9 NNF reused) before check to keep the derived-op table total.

7.9 Failure Analysis

Symptom Likely cause Detection
Verdict disagrees with NuSMV on AG only not/duality bug in derived lowering holds(AG p) vs holds(¬EF¬p) self-check
Fixpoint never converges / iterates nvars+ times broken canonicity (Phase 1) → handle eq fails iteration-count assert; canonicity property test
All EX-based specs false trans empty or wrong present/next cube node_count(trans), sat(EX ⊤) ≠ ⊥
Correct on small, wrong on large models reachable-restriction applied inconsistently around not run with restriction disabled; diff
Wrong verdict, no crash, fast sat-set computed over wrong var universe dump levels of init, ap, sat

Gravest: a canonicity break (Phase 1/2) makes fixpoint == lie → silent wrong verdicts here. CTL layer cannot self-detect it; relies on the canonicity property test as the upstream gate.

7.10 Operational Notes

  • smv check model.smv runs all CTLSPECs; --spec "AG p" checks an ad-hoc formula; --stats dumps per-spec iteration count + peak node count.
  • Emit one line per spec: -- specification <f> is true/false, NuSMV-compatible phrasing for the Phase 13 differential diff.
  • RUST_LOG=smv_check::ctl=debug traces each fixpoint iteration's node_count (convergence curve).
  • Determinism: identical model+spec ⇒ identical verdict and identical sat-set node count across runs (no reorder during a check; reordering is Phase 12, off by default here).
  • Counterexample output is a stub (-- (trace in Phase 8)) until Phase 8 wires counterexample.

20. Phase 8 — Counterexample and witness generation

Turns every NO verdict from CtlChecker (Phase 7, ## 19) into a concrete, re-simulatable Trace over the original SMV variables. A bare boolean verdict is undebuggable; the value of a model checker is the witness. Two extraction engines: onion-ring backward path (shortest finite path, for EX/EU/EF/reachability/AG failures) and lasso (stem + cycle, for EG/AF failures). All cube→StateAssignment decoding lives here. No source code; signatures and algorithm sketches only.

8.1 Objectives

  • CtlChecker::counterexample(&mut self, f: &CtlFormula) -> Option<Trace> — Some(trace) iff init AND not sat(f) != bdd_false; None iff spec holds.
  • Shortest finite counterexample/witness via onion rings (pre-image frontiers R_0..R_d); BFS-optimal length = d (fixpoint iteration count from Phase 6/7), not merely a path.
  • Lasso Trace { states, loopback: Some(j) } for EG p witnesses: stem s_0..s_{j-1} + cycle s_j..s_n -> s_j, all in sat(EG p).
  • Decode each on-path BDD cube → StateAssignment over original SMV vars (boolean/enum/Range), inverting VarEncoding (Phase 4).
  • Self-validation: every emitted Trace re-simulates (replay init/trans/spec on the path) and provably violates the property — a wrong trace is a bug, not a warning.
  • Property→witness duality table; pick stem/cycle vs path per top-level operator (after NNF push from ## 19).

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • Files (smv-check): src/trace.rs (Trace, StateAssignment, decode, replay); src/onion.rs (backward-frontier path); src/lasso.rs (stem+cycle); src/witness.rs (dispatch on CtlFormula → engine). src/ctl.rs gains counterexample. (ADR-18: Trace { states, loopback } is the LOCKED counterexample type; I-P8: counterexamples are first-class.)

  • Types:

    pub struct StateAssignment { pub bits: BTreeMap<u32, bool>,            // BDD level -> value
    pub vars: Vec<(Atom, Value)> }                                    // decoded SMV var -> value
pub struct Trace { pub states: Vec<StateAssignment>, pub loopback: Option<usize> }  // lasso

  • Onion-ring path (target set T = sat(EF p)-style violating set; e.g. T = not sat(p) for failing AG p): recompute the pre-image frontiers as a recorded lfp — R_0 = T AND reachable, R_{k+1} = R_k OR (preimage(R_k) AND reachable); stop at fixpoint, depth d. Locate the start ring R_d containing an init state. (B1 §5: onion-ring backward-reachability for shortest counterexamples.)

  • Backward walk: pick one s_0 ∈ init AND R_d (cube-pick, below). For k = d..1: choose s_{d-k+1} ∈ Img({s_{prev}}) AND R_{k-1} until reaching T. Each step uses and_exists(trans, cube(s_prev), present_cube) then rename next→present, intersect with the next inner ring. Yields path length d+1. (P3: image via and_exists + rename.)

  • Cube-pick (pick_one_state): from a non-false state-set Bdd, descend the DAG choosing a value at every present level (low if reachable-nonfalse else high), producing a minterm (fully-assigned cube) — never a partial cube, so replay is deterministic. (I-P6: determinism — same var order → same picked state.)

  • Lasso for EG p: S = sat(EG p) (Phase 7 gfp). Stem = onion path init → S inside reachable. From the entry state s_j, walk forward within S (Img({s}) AND S, pick) recording visited states; when a successor equals an already-visited state set loopback. Termination guaranteed: S finite, every state in S has a successor in S (gfp closure). (P4: Emerson-Lei lasso = fair cycle in EG; this is the CTL analog.)

  • Decode (StateAssignment::decode(bits, &[VarEncoding]) -> Vec<(Atom,Value)>): per var, gather its levels, read bits, invert encoding — boolean = 1 bit; enum/scalar = ceil(log2 |dom|) bits → index → Atom; Range = bits → i64 + lo. Don't-care levels (absent from minterm — should be none after pick_one_state) default 0 with a debug-assert. (ADR-18: StateAssignment decodes to human-readable SMV var names for NuSMV diff-ability.)

  • Replay/self-check (Trace::simulate(&EncodedFsm, &mut BddManager) -> bool): assert s_0 ⊆ init; assert each (s_i, s_{i+1}) ⊆ trans (conjoin present+next cubes, and with trans, expect != bdd_false); assert the spec-specific violation holds on the path; for lasso assert (s_n, s_loopback) ⊆ trans. (I-P8: every emitted trace is validated before printing — "never silent".)

  • Dispatch (witness.rs): EX/EU/EF/AG/AF-as-EG-dual → onion path; EG and inner EG of AF failure → lasso. NNF (from ## 19) guarantees negation is pushed to APs so the top operator is one of the canonical seven. (B3 §3.4: NNF derivation for CTL operators; B1 §4: path quantifier / temporal operator structure.)

8.3 Deliverable

smv check model.smv on any false CTLSPEC prints a numbered Trace: per-state StateAssignment lines (var = value, original SMV names), a -- loop starts here marker at loopback, and -- counterexample length d. CtlChecker::counterexample returns Some(Trace) for every NO and None for YES. Every emitted trace passes Trace::simulate internally before printing (CLI exits non-zero + diagnostic if replay fails — indicates an extractor bug).

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Criterion Target
A1 Path length minimal onion path length == d (BFS pre-image depth); no path of length < d exists
A2 Replay validity 100% of emitted traces pass Trace::simulate (init ⊆, all transitions ⊆ trans, spec violated)
A3 Lasso well-formed loopback = Some(j), j ≤ n, (s_n,s_j) ⊆ trans, all states ∈ sat(EG p)
A4 Decode faithfulness re-encoding each StateAssignment back to a cube equals the picked minterm (round-trip identity)
A5 Completeness counterexample returns Some iff init AND not sat(f) != bdd_false, None otherwise — no false None
A6 Determinism same model+spec+var-order ⇒ byte-identical Trace across runs

8.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Content Expected
mutex_ag_violation.smv AG !(c1 & c2) on a buggy mutex reaching c1&c2 onion path len = BFS dist to the bad state; final state c1=TRUE c2=TRUE
counter_ef.smv EF (x=7) true on a 0..7 counter witness path of length 7; states x=0..7; replay OK
eg_loop.smv EG (state=idle) true on a self-loop FSM lasso, loopback=Some(0), single-state cycle
af_fails_lasso.smv AF done false (a non-progress cycle avoids done) lasso witness for EG !done; cycle never sets done
init_violates.smv AG p where init itself sets !p path length 1 (states.len()==1, no transitions)
holds_no_trace.smv a true AG spec counterexample → None; CLI prints verified
malformed_partial.smv (synthetic) force a partial cube into decode debug-assert fires; release: deterministic 0-fill + warning
bad_replay.smv (synthetic, fault-injected pick) corrupt one chosen state Trace::simulate returns false; CLI exits non-zero

Golden .trace files store expected StateAssignment sequences; diff = exact match.

8.6 Pitfalls

  • Suboptimal path — walking pre-images without re-intersecting the recorded inner ring R_{k-1} lets a step jump to an outer ring, inflating length. Root cause: greedy Img pick. Debug: assert each s_k ∈ R_{d-k}; log ring indices.
  • Partial cube replay non-determinism — pick_one_state returning a non-minterm leaves don't-cares; replay/decode then ambiguous. Root cause: stopping descent at a terminal before all present levels assigned. Debug: assert node_count of picked state matches nvars path; round-trip A4.
  • Reachability omitted — extracting the path over sat(f) without intersecting reachable can pick an unreachable start state ⇒ replay fails A2 (s_0 ⊄ init). Root cause: forgot AND reachable. Debug: A5 + simulate.
  • present/next swap in backward step — using trans without rename next→present after and_exists walks the relation the wrong direction. Root cause: VarMap confusion. Debug: a 2-state directed (asymmetric) transition fixture exposes reversed traces.
  • Lasso never closes — forward walk in S picks fresh states forever because S was computed with a stale gfp (off-by-one iteration). Root cause: gfp not at fixpoint / wrong S. Debug: cap walk at |S|-bounded satcount; assert closure Img(S) AND S != false for every cycle state.
  • Enum decode index overflow — ceil(log2 |dom|) bits encode values ≥ |dom| (out-of-range codes); decoding such a code panics on Vec index. Root cause: ignoring the out-of-range constraint from Phase 4. Debug: traces only visit reachable states, so codes are in-range — assert and map stray codes to a <oor> sentinel.

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • Onion rings recompute the lfp once (already done in Phase 7 — cache frontiers R_k from the check pass instead of recomputing; saves a full fixpoint). Memory: storing d frontier BDDs is O(d * peak_nodes); cap by re-deriving on demand for very large d.
  • pick_one_state is O(nvars) DAG descent per state, O(d * nvars) per path — negligible vs fixpoint cost.
  • Per-step and_exists(trans, cube, present) is the hot op; single-minterm cube keeps the relational product tiny (< 1k nodes typically). Lasso forward walk ≤ |S| steps.
  • Target: trace extraction < 5% of total check time; < 50 ms for models with reachable diameter d ≤ 100 and ≤ 40 boolean levels.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • init itself violates AG p → path length 1, loopback = None, zero transitions to replay.
  • Self-loop only → lasso loopback = Some(0), states.len() == 1, cycle (s_0,s_0) ⊆ trans.
  • Multiple init states satisfying the target — pick is deterministic (first by var-order descent) per A6.
  • EG p where the entry state itself is on the cycle (stem length 0) → loopback = Some(0).
  • Vacuous spec AG TRUE / EF FALSE → None (no Some with empty states).
  • Range var with lo != 0 (e.g. -3..5) → decode adds lo; round-trip A4 must hold for negative values.
  • Don't-care levels for vars not constraining the violation — eliminated by minterm pick; if any remain, deterministic 0-fill (documented, asserted in debug).

8.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Symptom Detection Mitigation
Wrong-direction walk replay (s_i,s_{i+1}) ⊄ trans Trace::simulate A2 mandatory pre-print replay; non-zero exit
Non-minimal path Trace longer than d A1 + ring-membership assert re-intersect inner ring each step
Partial cube ambiguous decode, flaky golden diff A4 round-trip, node_count check full-minterm pick invariant
Unreachable start s_0 ⊄ init A5 + simulate AND reachable in frontier recompute
Lasso non-closure hang / infinite states ` S
Decode index OOB panic on enum Vec index fuzz A4 over enum models <oor> sentinel + reachable-only invariant

Gravest: a trace that looks plausible but doesn't actually violate the spec — masked because users trust output. Every trace is replayed against init/trans/spec before emission; replay failure is a hard error, never a silent print.

8.10 Operational Notes

  • CLI: smv check model.smv prints the Trace on NO; --trace-format {human,json} (json = Trace serialized for tooling); --no-trace skips extraction (verdict only, faster).
  • Print format: State 0: block of var = value lines (sorted by declaration order), -- loop starts here (loopback) before s_loopback, footer -- counterexample of length N.
  • RUST_LOG=smv_check::onion=debug logs ring depths d and per-step ring membership; ::lasso=debug logs stem/cycle lengths.
  • --seed-pick low|high flips the cube-descent tie-break (default low) for reproducing alternate (still-minimal) traces during debugging; default stays deterministic (A6).
  • JSON trace is the interchange format consumed by the BMC path (## 23) and the LTL fair-lasso extractor (## 22) — same Trace type, so downstream phases reuse decode/simulate verbatim. No duplicate trace logic.

21. Phase 9 — LTL front end and LTL-to-Buchi translation

Builds the bridge from textual LTL specs to a symbolic (generalized) Büchi automaton (GBA) so Phase 10 can take the synchronous product with EncodedFsm and run Emerson-Lei. Scope: smv-logic LTL parse + NNF + LtlFormula, GPVW on-the-fly tableau (VWAA as documented alternative), and a symbolic encoding (SymbolicBuchi) whose automaton-state bits are extra BDD levels and whose transition / acceptance sets are BDDs over smv-bdd. NON-GOAL here: the product and fair-cycle search (Phase 10); explicit-state automata enumeration (#279 — we stay symbolic). LTL semantics target: A phi (the model satisfies phi on all fair paths); to refute, Phase 10 will check the automaton for ¬phi for a reachable fair cycle.

9.1 Objectives

  • Parse LTL spec strings (SpecDecl::Ltl(String)) into LtlFormula (Pratt parser) with operators X U R F G ! & | -> <->, AP = SMV boolean expr → Bdd via smv-model ap binding.
  • Normalize to NNF: push ! to APs; rewrite -> <-> F G into the X U R Not And Or Ap core so the tableau handles a minimal operator set. Invariant: post-NNF, Not wraps only Ap.
  • GPVW tableau → generalized Büchi automaton: nodes = sets of formulas (Old/New/Next), edges labeled by an AP-Bdd guard, one acceptance set per U subformula (eventuality).
  • Encode automaton symbolically: ceil(log2(|Q|)) fresh automaton-state levels appended above (lower priority than) all model levels; trans_aut: Bdd over (q, ap, q'); accept: Vec<Bdd> (state-sets) per fairness condition.
  • Determinism / canonicity: same formula → identical NNF → identical SymbolicBuchi (state numbering canonical by node-set hash order). Validate on Dwyer LTL patterns.

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

File (smv-logic/src/) Adds Key items
ltl/parser.rs LTL Pratt parser parse_ltl(s:&str, fsm:&EncodedFsm, m:&mut BddManager) -> Result<LtlFormula,ParseError>; binding power: <-><-><`
ltl/nnf.rs NNF rewrite to_nnf(LtlFormula)->LtlFormula; rules below; idempotent; depth-bounded recursion (no realloc per node).
ltl/gpvw.rs tableau struct GpvwNode{old:FormSet,new:FormSet,next:FormSet,incoming:BitSet}; expand() worklist; build_gba(&LtlFormula)->Gba.
buchi.rs abstract GBA struct Gba{states:Vec<GbaState>, init:BitSet, edges:Vec<(usize,Bdd,usize)>, accept:Vec<BitSet>}.
symbolic.rs symbolic encoding struct SymbolicBuchi{ levels:Vec<u32>, trans_aut:Bdd, init_aut:Bdd, accept:Vec<Bdd>, n_states:u32 }; encode(&Gba,&mut BddManager,&EncodedFsm)->SymbolicBuchi.

NNF rewrite rules (apply to fixpoint, then Not only over Ap): (B3 §3.2/B2 §5: LTL operator equivalences used as rewrite rules; I-P3: NNF as preprocessing before tableau.)

!!p        => p
!(p & q)   => !p | !q          !(p | q) => !p & !q
!(X p)     => X !p             !(F p)   => G !p      !(G p) => F !p
!(p U q)   => !p R !q          !(p R q) => !p U !q
F p        => true U p         G p      => false R p
p -> q     => !p | q           p<->q    => (!p|q)&(!q|p)

GPVW expansion (per node new-formula head, NuSMV/Holzmann GPVW): (P8: GPVW tableau is the direct source for this algorithm; R3: LTL2BA/Spot for cross-reference on node expansion.)

Formula head in new Node action New new set New next set Acceptance set note
Ap a / !Ap a If a/!a contradicts old → discard node; else move f to old unchanged unchanged —
p & q Add both conjuncts to new ∪ {p, q} unchanged —
p ∣ q Split: one branch for p, one for q branch 1: {p}; branch 2: {q} unchanged —
X p Move p to next (next-state obligation) remove X p ∪ {p} —
p U q Split: satisfaction branch (q now) and obligation branch (p, defer pUq) branch 1: {q}; branch 2: {p} branch 2: ∪ {pUq} Acceptance set for pUq: nodes where pUq ∈ old and q ∈ old
p R q Split: full-release branch (q and p) and perpetual branch (q only, defer pRq) branch 1: {q, p}; branch 2: {q} branch 2: ∪ {pRq} —

Symbolic encode: assign each Gba state id 0..n_states; allocate k=ceil(log2 n_states) levels via ithvar (record in SymbolicBuchi.levels); build state_pred(q):Bdd (binary code of q); trans_aut = OR over edges (src_pred & guard_ap & dst_pred') using primed automaton vars; init_aut = OR src_pred for Gba.init; each accept[j] = OR state_pred(q) for q in accept[j]. Primed automaton levels interleave with model next via the same VarMap discipline. (P12: Clarke-Grumberg-Hamaguchi symbolic encoding of the automaton as extra state bits — same as SymbolicBuchi.levels; ADR-14: GPVW chosen over Safra for generalized-Büchi output.)

9.3 Deliverable

smv-logic exports parse_ltl, to_nnf, build_gba, and SymbolicBuchi/encode. A CLI debug path smv encode --ltl "<spec>" model.smv --dump-buchi emits the GBA (states, edges as (guard, dst), acceptance sets) and the symbolic stats (n_states, levels, node_count(trans_aut)). Verifiable: every Dwyer pattern → a GBA whose language matches the reference automaton size class.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • All 11 Dwyer/Spec-Pattern LTL formulas (e.g. G(p->F q), G F p, p U q, G(p -> (q U r))) parse, NNF, and produce a GBA; n_states within +/-1 of GPVW reference counts (≤ 2^|sub(phi)|, typically ≤ 8 for patterns).
  • to_nnf idempotent: to_nnf(to_nnf(f)) == to_nnf(f) (structural eq) on 10k random formulas; post-NNF every Not child is Ap.
  • Determinism: encode of same formula yields byte-identical --dump-buchi and equal node_count(trans_aut) across 100 runs.
  • Sanity: G F true ⇒ 1-state GBA, 1 acceptance set, trans_aut = all model transitions; false ⇒ empty automaton (init_aut == bdd_false).
  • node_count(trans_aut) ≤ 5× node_count(EncodedFsm.trans) for pattern formulas (guards are AP-shallow).

9.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
ltl_response.smv LTLSPEC G(req -> F ack) GBA 2–3 states, 1 acc set; NNF `false R (!req
ltl_gf.smv LTLSPEC G F p 1 state, acc={s0}, guard true
ltl_until.smv LTLSPEC p U q 2 states, acc set for pUq
ltl_nested.smv LTLSPEC G(p -> (q U r)) states 3–4; --dump-buchi golden file
ltl_bad_op.txt LTLSPEC p UU q ParseError{UnexpectedToken}
ltl_unknown_ap.txt LTLSPEC X foo (foo undeclared) ParseError{UnknownVar}
ltl_empty.txt LTLSPEC ParseError{UnexpectedToken} (EOF)

Golden --dump-buchi outputs checked into smv-logic/tests/golden/; diff must be empty.

9.6 Pitfalls

  • Eventuality acceptance per-U mishandled → single acceptance set conflates distinct Us → spurious accepting cycles. Root: one accept set instead of one-per-eventuality. Debug: assert accept.len() == count_until_subformulas(phi); dump each set's states.
  • R vs U dual swapped in NNF/expansion → wrong language, often silently accepts everything. Debug: unit-test !(p U q) == !p R !q by GBA language equivalence on small alphabets.
  • Automaton primed levels alias model next levels → and_exists in Phase 10 quantifies wrong vars. Root: shared VarMap not extended. Debug: assert SymbolicBuchi.levels disjoint from present/next; print level→owner map.
  • Non-canonical state numbering → non-deterministic trans_aut, golden diffs flap. Root: HashSet iteration order of node-sets. Fix: sort node-sets by a stable formula hash before id assignment.
  • AP guard captures a Next expr (next(x) inside an LTL AP) → guard wrongly references next-state levels. Root: LTL X is the only temporal step; SMV next() is illegal inside LTLSPEC. Reject with ParseError{TypeMismatch}.
  • Tableau non-termination on G F G F ... style if next-set memoization missing → hashcons GPVW nodes by (old,next) to dedup.

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • GPVW worst case O(2^|sub(phi)|) states; patterns stay ≤ 8 — fine. Guard: cap n_states at 2^16 (levels ≤ 16) else Err(FormulaTooLarge) before encode.
  • encode builds trans_aut by OR of per-edge cubes — O(|edges| · k · |guard|) BDD ops; reuse computed cache; build automaton levels at the top of the order so they sit near the root and don't blow up node_count.
  • AP Bdds memoized in EncodedFsm.ap; do not recompile identical APs across subformulas — dedup by Expr hash.
  • to_nnf/build_gba allocate FormSet as sorted SmallVec<[FormId;8]> to avoid heap churn on pattern-sized formulas.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • LTLSPEC true / false → 1-state-accept-all / empty automaton.
  • Pure-AP spec LTLSPEC p → 1 transient state, acc all (G F vacuous): handled by treating no-eventuality formula with universal acceptance Vec of len 1 = bdd_true.
  • Tautology LTLSPEC G(p | !p) → GBA accepts all; Phase 10 must report HOLDS.
  • Nested X X X p → 4 states (chain), no acceptance constraint beyond the trivial.
  • p R q with p=false ≡ G q — NNF leaves false R q; encode must not special-case-fold incorrectly.
  • Single automaton state but ≥1 eventuality (G F p): accept=[ {s0 with p} ], not [{s0}].

9.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Wrong GBA language (subtle op bug) golden diff + Phase 13 differential vs NuSMV verdict block release; canonicity/language unit tests are gate
n_states blowup (adversarial phi) n_states cap exceeded Err(FormulaTooLarge); suggest BMC path (Phase 11)
Acceptance under/over-count accept.len() assert vs #U hard debug_assert!; fail closed
Level exhaustion (k too large) levels alloc > budget Err; do not silently truncate (would corrupt canonicity)
Aliased primed vars disjointness assert in encode panic in debug; CI catches

GRAVEST tie-in: a mis-encoded trans_aut or accept yields a valid BDD that gives wrong fair-cycle results in Phase 10 with no crash — same silent-corruption class as ROBDD canonicity. Hence golden GBA fixtures + language equivalence tests are mandatory here, before any product is built.

9.10 Operational Notes

  • RUST_LOG=smv_logic::gpvw=debug traces node expansion (split/contradiction/accept assignment); --dump-buchi for the encoded form.
  • Feature ltl-vwaa swaps GPVW for a VWAA (very-weak alternating) construction behind the same build_gba signature — documented alternative, off by default; both must pass identical golden language tests.
  • smv bdd-stats reports trans_aut node_count and automaton-levels count so reorder budgets (Phase 12 sifting) account for them.
  • Determinism contract documented in smv-logic/README: state numbering is canonical; golden files are the regression oracle. Bump golden files only with an explicit reviewed diff justifying a language-preserving change.

22. Phase 10 — LTL symbolic model checking (product + Emerson-Lei fair cycle)

LTL verdict via language emptiness: M |= A phi iff product M x B(¬phi) has no reachable fair cycle. LtlChecker (crate smv-check) consumes the symbolic Buchi automaton from Phase 9 (## 21), forms the synchronous product BDD over present ∪ aut ∪ next ∪ aut' levels, runs the Emerson-Lei greatest-fixpoint to isolate fair states, intersects with reachable, and — if non-empty — extracts a fair lasso Trace. Empty fair-and-reachable set ⇒ property holds.

10.1 Objectives

  • Build symbolic product P = trans_M ∧ trans_B ∧ label_sync over combined level space; product init = init_M ∧ init_B.
  • Emerson-Lei gfp over generalized-Buchi acceptance sets {F_1..F_m} + model fairness EncodedFsm.fairness ⇒ fair: Bdd (states on some fair cycle).
  • Verdict: (reachable_P ∧ fair) == bdd_false ⇒ M |= A phi; else extract a fair lasso counterexample: stem (init→fair) + cycle (fair→same fair state visiting every F_i), project to model vars → Trace { states, loopback }.
  • Correctly merge generalized-Buchi (multiple acceptance sets) + Streett-style model fairness via the unified Emerson-Lei iteration. NuSMV-equivalent verdicts.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Crate smv-check, module ltl.rs; reuses fixpoint.rs (Phase 6 ## 18), image.rs, ctl.rs::ex. (P4: Emerson-Lei fair-cycle detection is the core algorithm; ADR-15: chosen over Nested-DFS for symbolic regime.)

Item Spec
LtlChecker<'a> holds &mut BddManager, &EncodedFsm, &SymbolicBuchi (Phase 9), product VarMaps
fn check(&mut self, phi:&LtlFormula)->bool translate ¬phi→Buchi→product→fair_states()→reachable∩fair empty? (B2 §5: LTL semantics — M⊨Aφ iff M×B(¬φ) has no reachable fair cycle.)
fn product(&mut self)->ProductFsm init=init_M∧init_B; trans=trans_M∧trans_B∧sync; accept:Vec<Bdd> = each F_i lifted; absorb fairness (P12: symbolic product encoding as extra state bits.)
sync constraint for each Buchi edge guard g (Bdd over model AP ap): trans_B already references AP bits; conjoin trans_M so present-AP shared, no rename needed for AP — only aut state bits are fresh present/next pairs
fn fair_states(&mut self,p:&ProductFsm)->Bdd Emerson-Lei gfp below (P4)
fn ex_p(&mut self, s:Bdd)->Bdd product pre-image = and_exists(p.trans, s', cube=next∪aut_next) then rename(next→present, aut_next→aut) (ADR-12: fused and_exists on the product trans.)
fn lasso(&mut self,p,fair:Bdd)->Trace stem via onion-rings (Phase 8 ## 20) from init_P to fair; cycle via fair re-closure (ADR-18: fair lasso encoded as Trace{states, loopback:Some(i)})

Emerson-Lei generalized fair gfp (sets A = {F_1..F_m} = Buchi acceptance ∪ each model fairness[j]) — iteration structure and convergence (P4: Emerson-Lei 1986 — direct source for this nested gfp/lfp structure; B5 §10: survey of automata-based LTL checking confirming this as the standard symbolic approach):

Phase Inputs Operation Output Termination
Outer gfp (shrink) fair = candidate fair-SCC states; acceptance sets A = {F_1 … F_m} For each F_i ∈ A in turn, intersect fair with the lfp below; update fair := fair ∧ Y_i Reduced fair capturing only states that can reach every F_i while staying in fair Converged when fair is unchanged after a full pass over all F_i; handle-equality test is O(1) (P1 canonicity)
Inner lfp (grow) Z = fair snapshot; F_i; product EX_P operator Y = lfp Y'. (F_i ∧ ex_p(Z)) ∨ ex_p(Y') — EU within the product: start from F_i ∩ EX_P(Z), grow by backward steps State-set Y = all product states that can reach F_i in one step while the target remains Z Converges ≤ `
Result — Intersection reachable ∩ fair tested for == bdd_false bdd_false → A φ HOLDS; non-empty → fair lasso witness extracted via lasso(p, fair) —
  • ProductFsm.trans built conjunctively partitioned (Phase 5 ## 17 reused): keep [trans_M, trans_B, sync, fairness…] as Vec<Bdd>, never one monolith — and_exists quantifies per-partition with early quantification. Aut bits: SymbolicBuchi exposes aut_present:Vec<u32>, aut_next:Vec<u32> levels (interleaved after model next in order); cube_next_all = aut_next_cube ∧ next_cube.
pub struct ProductFsm { pub init: Bdd, pub trans: Vec<Bdd>, pub accept: Vec<Bdd>,
    pub present_all: VarMap, pub next_all: VarMap }
impl LtlChecker<'_> {
    // Steps: negate phi → GPVW Büchi (P8) → symbolic product → Emerson-Lei gfp (P4) →
    //        test fair ∩ reach == bdd_false
    pub fn check(&mut self, phi: &LtlFormula) -> bool;
    // Extract fair lasso: stem (init→fair state) + cycle (visits every F_i accept set) → Trace
    pub fn counterexample(&mut self, phi: &LtlFormula) -> Option<Trace>;
}

10.3 Deliverable

smv-cli check --ltl model.smv (or LTLSPEC in file) emits TRUE / FALSE + a fair-lasso Trace (stem states then -- loop back to state N). LtlChecker::check + counterexample complete; product + Emerson-Lei + lasso extraction wired through smv-cli. Generalized-Buchi + model FAIRNESS honored.

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Criterion
A1 F p, G p, G F p, p U q, X p, p R q, nested (G(req→F ack)) — verdicts match NuSMV on corpus, 100%
A2 Emerson-Lei gfp converges in ≤ `
A3 Empty fair set ⇒ TRUE with no trace; non-empty ⇒ lasso with loopback = Some(i), i < states.len()
A4 Lasso replays as valid path: each consecutive pair ⊆ trans_M; loop edge ⊆ trans_M; cycle visits every accept[i] ≥ once
A5 With FAIRNESS f: liveness F g correctly evaluated only over f-fair paths (matches NuSMV -f); AND generalized Buchi with m≥2 acceptance sets passes vs single degeneralized equivalent

10.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Spec Expected
ltl_gfp_eventually.smv LTLSPEC F state=done TRUE (done reachable on all paths)
ltl_gf_fair.smv LTLSPEC G F served + FAIRNESS req TRUE; degenerate without fairness → FALSE+lasso
ltl_response.smv LTLSPEC G(req → F ack) (ack starved) FALSE; lasso loops in req∧¬ack SCC, loopback set
ltl_until.smv LTLSPEC p U q matches NuSMV verdict + counterexample length
ltl_genbuchi.smv LTLSPEC G F a ∧ G F b TRUE/FALSE equals degeneralized run
ltl_release.smv LTLSPEC q R p dual of U, verdict matches
malformed empty Buchi (phi valid, ¬phi unsat → 0 aut states); or model with init=bdd_false (no initial state) product trans = bdd_false, fair = bdd_false ⇒ TRUE, no panic; or reachable_P=false ⇒ vacuously TRUE, warn to stderr

Golden: ltl_*.expected holds verdict + canonical lasso (state cubes over original vars) + outer-iter count.

10.6 Pitfalls

  • Quantifying wrong variable set in ex_p (must quantify model next and aut_next together; forgetting aut bits ⇒ fair states leak across aut transitions ⇒ false counterexamples; debug: assert support(ex_p(s)) ⊆ present_all) and AP rename bug (AP bits are model present levels shared by trans_B; renaming them as if aut-local corrupts sync — only fresh aut state bits get present/next pairs; debug: dump support(trans_B), AP levels must equal model present levels).
  • Degeneralization vs native generalized mismatch: iterating acceptance sets in the inner loop must use the same Z snapshot per outer round; refreshing Z mid-loop breaks gfp monotonicity ⇒ non-convergence/wrong fair set. Root cause: shared mutable Z. Debug: log node_count(fair) per outer iter — must be monotonically non-increasing.
  • Model fairness merged as Buchi acceptance (model fairness[j] is "infinitely often visit", same Emerson-Lei treatment — but it constrains paths, not Buchi acceptance; mixing into accept is correct only because gfp conjoins all; pitfall: omitting them ⇒ spurious counterexamples on unfair paths) and lasso stem/cycle stitch (cycle re-entry state must equal a stem-reachable fair state; using any fair state not reachable through the stem yields a non-replayable trace — use onion-ring from init_P, then close cycle within the reached fair set).
  • F/G not pushed to NNF before Buchi (Phase 9 contract): LtlChecker must call nnf(¬phi); a stray top-level Not over X/U reaches the tableau and mis-translates ⇒ wrong automaton ⇒ wrong verdict.

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • Product blow-up: |P| ≤ |M| × |B|; aut states 2^O(|phi|) worst case → keep trans conjunctively partitioned, never materialize monolithic product trans. Early quantification in ex_p: schedule aut_next + model next quantification interleaved with partition conjunction (reuse Phase 12 ## 24 schedule) — cuts peak nodes 5–50×.
  • Emerson-Lei cost = O(outer × m × |P|) BDD image steps; m = #acceptance sets (Buchi) + #model fairness. Cache ex_p results across inner sets (same Z).
  • Targets: dining-philosophers N=6 LTL response property < 2 s, peak < 50k product nodes; N=10 < 30 s. Reordering (sifting) optional toggle.

10.8 Edge Cases

  • phi is pure state predicate (no temporal op): Buchi for ¬phi = single accepting self-loop on ¬phi; reduces to "is ¬phi reachable" — must still route through product (sanity equals EF ¬phi).
  • Tautology phi = G(p ∨ ¬p): ¬phi unsat ⇒ empty Buchi ⇒ TRUE, no fixpoint iterations; and no fair cycle but reachable accepting states with no self-return: gfp must drop them (Y excludes them) ⇒ TRUE (tests transient-vs-recurrent distinction).
  • Initial state itself a fair self-loop: stem length 0, loopback = Some(0); model with fairness empty + generalized Buchi m=1: degenerates to single-Buchi reachable-fair-cycle (classic EG over accept).
  • X p at trace end / k=0 boundary: handled by Buchi next semantics, not BMC; no special-case here.

10.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Symptom Detection
Canonicity broken upstream (Phase 1) gfp never converges or wrong fair set, no crash property test: fair recomputed twice ⇒ identical handle; cross-check vs NuSMV
Wrong sync (AP bits) random false counterexamples replay-trace validator (A4) fails: a consecutive pair ⊄ trans_M
Forgotten model fairness spurious FALSE on fair models A5 differential vs NuSMV -f
Inner-lfp not a true lfp (started ≠ false); or lasso non-replayable misses/overcounts fair states; or trace rejected by simulator assert inner seed = bdd_false, monotone growth; or smv-cli simulate re-runs trace, mismatch flagged
Generalized accept dropped a set passes unfair behavior A6: degeneralized equivalence check

Gravest: silent canonicity corruption (per spine) → fixpoint convergence + equality wrong → wrong verdict, no crash. Guard with redundant equality + NuSMV diff.

10.10 Operational Notes

  • smv-cli check --ltl --stats prints product node count, #aut states, Emerson-Lei outer/inner iteration counts, fair-set satcount, peak live nodes. --dump-product model.dot exports product trans (partitioned, one dot per partition) for debugging.
  • --fairness-from-model (default on) folds EncodedFsm.fairness into acceptance; --no-fairness for diagnostic A/B.
  • Trace output: model vars only (aut bits stripped via project/exists over aut_present), format state i: cube + -- loop back to state N.
  • Log level RUST_LOG=smv_check::ltl=debug traces per-outer-iter fair node counts; assert monotone in debug builds.
  • Reuse fixpoint.rs, image.rs::ex, Phase 8 onion-ring extractor — no LTL-specific fixpoint engine; only the Emerson-Lei nesting is new.

23. Phase 11 — Bounded Model Checking (SAT unrolling)

BMC complements the BDD engine: bug-finding by SAT, not full proof. Crate smv-bmc consumes EncodedFsm (from smv-model) + CtlFormula/LtlFormula (from smv-logic), unrolls the transition relation k steps into a CnfFormula via Tseitin, ships it to a SatBackend, and decodes a Sat(model) into a counterexample Trace. NON-GOAL: a competitive SAT core — #277 is the production SatBackend; the bundled DPLL is a correctness oracle only.

11.1 Objectives

  • Build Unroller<'a> over &EncodedFsm: emit I(s0) AND conj_{i<k} T(s_i,s_{i+1}) AND NOT-prop_k as CnfFormula (DIMACS clauses: Vec<Vec<i32>>, nvars).
  • Tseitin transform BDD-relations → CNF: one fresh aux var per BDD node per timeframe; def clauses encode ite — equisatisfiable, linear in node count.
  • Define trait SatBackend { fn solve(&mut self, cnf:&CnfFormula)->SatResult }; bundle a DPLL/CDCL BundledSat; #277 plugs in unchanged.
  • Incremental k: 0,1,…,k_max; Sat at k ⇒ shortest bug at depth k; Unsat-through-k_max ⇒ no bug ≤ k_max (NOT a proof unless completeness threshold reached — Phase 12).
  • Decode Sat(Vec<bool>) → Trace { states, loopback }; cross-check verdict vs CtlChecker/LtlChecker on shared safety/bounded-liveness fixtures.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files under smv-bmc/src/:

File Contents
unroll.rs Unroller<'a>{ fsm:&'a EncodedFsm }; fn unroll_safety(&self, prop:Bdd, k:u32)->CnfFormula; fn unroll_ltl(&self, buchi:&Buchi, k:u32)->CnfFormula (lasso constraint)
tseitin.rs struct Tseitin{ next_var:u32, clauses:Vec<Vec<i32>>, cache:HashMap<(Bdd,u32),i32> }; fn encode(&mut self, mgr:&BddManager, f:Bdd, frame:u32)->i32
cnf.rs pub struct CnfFormula{ clauses:Vec<Vec<i32>>, nvars:u32 }; fn add(&mut self, c:Vec<i32>); fn to_dimacs(&self)->String
backend.rs pub trait SatBackend; pub enum SatResult{ Sat(Vec<bool>), Unsat }; Sat277Backend (FFI/process to #277 DIMACS)
dpll.rs pub struct BundledSat; unit-prop + watched-literals + 1-UIP CDCL; impl SatBackend
decode.rs fn model_to_trace(model:&[bool], k:u32, fsm:&EncodedFsm, vmap:&FrameVarMap)->Trace
engine.rs fn bmc_check(fsm, prop, k_max, backend)->BmcResult{ Bug(Trace), NoBugUpTo(u32), Proven }
  • Variable layout FrameVarMap: state var level at frame i → CNF var base + i*nstate + level (1-indexed; sign = polarity). Aux Tseitin vars allocated above (k+1)*nstate. (B4 §11.2: the standard BMC variable layout for unrolling; P5: Biere-Cimatti-Clarke-Zhu original unrolling construction.)
  • Tseitin of a BDD f at frame i: post-order over node DAG; terminal bdd_true→ literal forced true, bdd_false→ false; node n=ite(v,hi,lo) → fresh y, emit clauses for y ≡ (x_v ? hi : lo) (6 clauses, or 4 after bdd_true/bdd_false pruning); complement edges → negate literal, no extra clause. Memoize on (node, frame). (B4 §2.3: Tseitin transform; ADR-17: Tseitin chosen over Plaisted-Greenbaum.)
  • T(s_i,s_{i+1}) reuses fsm.trans (a single Bdd over present∪next): frame i maps present→frame i, next→frame i+1. Conjunctive partitions (Phase 5) Tseitin'd independently then AND'd. (ADR-11: partitioned trans reused here; P5: unrolling formula structure.)
  • Property: safety G p ⇒ assert or_{i<=k} NOT p(s_i); bounded F p ⇒ assert and_{i<=k} NOT p(s_i); LTL ⇒ unroll Buchi(NOT phi) product with a lasso back-edge or_{l<=k} (s_k = s_l) + acceptance hit in loop. (P6: LTL BMC loop encoding with loop-selector variables.)
  • Incremental loop engine.rs: for k in 0..=k_max { cnf=unroll(.,k); match backend.solve(&cnf){ Sat(m)=>return Bug(model_to_trace(..)), Unsat=>continue } }. (I-P12: SatBackend trait decouples the SAT solver from the unroller; ADR-16: pluggable SAT backend; I-F9: NoBugUpTo(k) is NOT a proof unless k ≥ completeness threshold.)

11.3 Deliverable

smv-bmc crate + smv bmc -k <K> --prop <SPEC> model.smv CLI subcommand. Given a model violating G p at depth d ≤ k, returns a concrete Trace of length d; given an unviolated property, returns NoBugUpTo(k_max). Emits DIMACS via --dump-cnf for external solvers. Default backend BundledSat; --sat 277 selects Sat277Backend.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • For every shared safety fixture: smv bmc verdict ≡ smv check (CTL AG p) verdict (Bug-found ⟺ CTL-fails), with bmc trace length = onion-ring shortest counterexample length.
  • Tseitin equisatisfiability: satcount-consistency — # of Sat models projected to state vars over f at frame 0 equals mgr.satcount(f, nstate) on ≥20 random f (|nodes| ≤ 200).
  • BundledSat vs Sat277Backend: identical Sat/Unsat verdict on 100% of generated CNFs (models may differ).
  • Bug(Trace) decoded trace replays: each T(s_i,s_{i+1}) holds under fsm.trans (re-substitute via restrict, expect bdd_true).
  • Incremental: returns at the minimum k with a bug (no spurious deeper trace).

11.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Property Expected
mutex_safe.smv AG !(c1 & c2) NoBugUpTo(20) (matches CTL pass)
mutex_buggy.smv AG !(c1 & c2) Bug, trace len = 3, ≡ CTL counterexample
counter8.smv AG (v != 7) Bug at k=7, not k<7
liveness_bounded.smv bounded F ack (k=10) Bug if no ack ≤10, else NoBugUpTo
cnf_roundtrip.dimacs — to_dimacs → external read-back identical

Malformed: prop referencing unknown AP → ParseErrorKind::UnknownVar; k=0 with init ⊨ NOT p → immediate Bug len 1; k_max overflow ((k+1)*nstate > u32::MAX) → explicit error, no wraparound; empty fairness + LTL lasso → no acceptance constraint, documented.

11.6 Pitfalls

  • Frame var-aliasing: reusing the same CNF var for present-at-i+1 and next-at-i is REQUIRED (chaining); reusing it across non-adjacent frames silently merges timesteps → bogus short traces. Debug: dump FrameVarMap, assert present_var(i+1)==next_var(i).
  • Tseitin polarity for complement edges: forgetting to flip the literal sign on a complemented BDD edge inverts the function — SAT/Unsat flips, no crash. Debug: unit-test encode(not f) literal == -encode(f).
  • bdd_true/bdd_false constant clauses dropped: omitting the unit clause forcing a constant subterm leaves it free → over-approximate, false Sat. Detect via satcount cross-check (11.4).
  • Missing invar in each frame: fsm.init/fsm.trans already fold INVAR, but per-frame state-only INVAR must hold at every s_i; dropping it admits invalid states in the trace. Replay-check (11.4) catches it.
  • Off-by-one on property scope: or_{i<=k} vs i<k — excluding frame k misses depth-k bugs, reports NoBugUpTo wrongly. Fixture counter8 (boundary at exactly k) guards this.
  • CDCL learnt-clause var range: 1-UIP clauses must reference only declared nvars; an aux var leaking past nvars corrupts the watched-literal index → panic or wrong model.

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • CNF size ≈ k * (|trans nodes| + sum|partition nodes|) aux vars + ~4–6 clauses each; targets: k=20, nstate≤64 ⇒ ≤ 2M clauses, unroll < 200 ms, BundledSat may time out — #277 is the perf path.
  • Tseitin cache (node,frame) is mandatory — without it, shared BDD subgraphs re-emit, blowing CNF up O(paths) not O(nodes).
  • Conjunctive partitioning of trans → smaller per-partition node counts → fewer aux vars than monolithic trans.
  • Prefer incremental SAT (assumptions/#277 push-pop) to re-solving from scratch each k: reuse learnt clauses for frames 0..k ⇒ near-linear amortized vs quadratic.
  • BundledSat target: solve 10k-var / 50k-clause CNFs < 5 s (correctness oracle, not a benchmark target).

11.8 Edge Cases

  • k=0: CNF = I(s0) AND NOT p(s0); Sat ⟺ init already violates safety (trace len 1).
  • init == bdd_false (vacuous model): CNF unsatisfiable at all k ⇒ NoBugUpTo; warn (no reachable states).
  • prop constant: p == bdd_true ⇒ NOT p clause is empty ([]) ⇒ trivially Unsat; p == bdd_false ⇒ Bug at k=0.
  • Self-loop-only model (trans = identity): traces never grow; F p bounded check saturates at k=0 state.
  • LTL with empty fairness: lasso need only be a reachable cycle (any back-edge); no Büchi acceptance literal.

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Mitigation
Spurious Sat (over-approx CNF) satcount cross-check + trace replay vs fsm.trans reject trace; flag Tseitin/INVAR bug
Spurious Unsat (over-constrained) CTL says Bug, BMC says NoBug at k≥ CTL-len compare; var-aliasing or polarity audit
Backend disagreement BundledSat vs Sat277 differ bisect CNF; treat BundledSat as oracle
k_max reached, still Unsat not a proof escalate to completeness threshold (Phase 12) or BDD check
Decode crash (model too short) model.len() < (k+1)*nstate assert var-count invariant pre-decode

Verdict semantics MUST be tri-valued: Bug (sound, concrete witness), NoBugUpTo(k) (incomplete), Proven (only after threshold) — never report NoBugUpTo as "verified".

11.10 Operational Notes

  • smv bmc -k K --prop "AG p" model.smv → Bug+trace | NoBugUpTo K; --incremental (default on) walks 0..=K; --dump-cnf out.dimacs for offline solving.
  • --sat {bundled|277}; Sat277Backend shells out / FFIs to #277 reading DIMACS on stdin, parsing s SATISFIABLE + v lines.
  • Logs: per-k clause/var counts + solve time (RUST_LOG=smv_bmc=debug); --stats prints unroll vs solve breakdown.
  • Trace output reuses Phase 8 StateAssignment rendering over original SMV vars (decode BDD-level bits → enum/range values).
  • Determinism: fixed var allocation order ⇒ reproducible CNF; BundledSat uses fixed decision heuristic + seed for reproducible models.

24. Phase 12 — Optimizations, partitioning, and fairness

Prerequisite: Phases 0–11 complete (smv-bdd ops, EncodedFsm, CtlChecker, LtlChecker, Unroller). This phase is performance + completeness, not new verdicts: every optimization MUST preserve canonicity and the verdicts of Phases 7/10/11. Scope: Rudell sifting (node-count-triggered dynamic variable reordering), conjunctive/disjunctive partitioned transition relations with an early-quantification schedule, computed-cache + unique-table tuning, Buchi/Streett fairness wiring into CTL/LTL, and the BMC completeness threshold (recurrence/reachability diameter) so smv-bmc certifies bug absence, not just presence.

12.1 Objectives

  • Dynamic variable reordering (Rudell sifting) inside BddManager: variable-by-variable adjacent swap, keep best position, triggered when live node count crosses a growth threshold. Canonicity invariant holds across reorder (handle equality unchanged for equal functions).
  • Replace monolithic trans:Bdd with a partitioned representation: conjunctive (AND_i T_i) for synchronous, disjunctive (OR_a T_a) for asynchronous/interleaving models, plus a per-cluster early-quantification schedule so exists is pushed inward → intermediate BDDs stay small.
  • Tune computed cache (sizing, collision policy, GC interaction) and unique table (load factor, rehash) for hit rate ≥ 90% on benchmarks.
  • Wire Buchi/Streett fairness (EncodedFsm.fairness) into CtlChecker (fair-CTL operators) and LtlChecker (Emerson-Lei already uses it; generalize to Streett pairs).
  • Compute the completeness threshold (recurrence diameter rd, reachability diameter d) so smv-bmc returns a verified-safe verdict when unrolled to k ≥ rd.
  • Target: ≥ 10× reduction in peak BDD node count and ≥ 10× wall-clock speedup on the dining-philosophers / mutex / arbiter benchmark family vs. the naive Phase 5–10 build.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files (extend section-10 layout; LOCKED crate/type names only):

File Adds
smv-bdd/src/reorder.rs pub fn sift(&mut self) -> ReorderStats; adjacent-level swap(l); set_reorder_trigger(growth: f64); enable_dynamic_reorder(bool)
smv-bdd/src/manager.rs reorder hook in node alloc; VarMap level-indirection so external handles survive reorder; reorder_count, peak_nodes stats
smv-bdd/src/cache.rs cache resize policy; clear_on_reorder(); per-op stats (hits, misses, collisions)
smv-model/src/partition.rs pub struct PartTrans { pub parts: Vec<Bdd>, pub kind: PartKind, pub schedule: Vec<Bdd> }; PartKind::{Conj, Disj}; cluster + order builder
smv-check/src/image.rs image_part(&mut self, s: Bdd, pt: &PartTrans) -> Bdd using scheduled and_exists; preimage dual
smv-check/src/fair.rs fair_states(&mut self) -> Bdd; fair-CTL ex_fair/eg_fair/eu_fair; Streett pair handling
smv-bmc/src/completeness.rs recurrence_diameter(&mut self) -> u32; reachability_diameter(&mut self) -> u32; is_complete(k) -> bool

Algorithms:

  • Sifting: maintain doubly-linked level order; cost-O(1) adjacent swap(l) rebuilding only the two affected unique-table sub-buckets; sift each variable through the full order (down then up, or toward nearest boundary), record min-node position, restore it. Lower-bound prune: stop a direction once size exceeds maxGrowth × best (default 1.2). Variables sifted in decreasing-node-count order. (P7: Rudell sifting algorithm — the direct implementation source; ADR-08: automatic sifting chosen over manual / fixed order.)
  • Reorder trigger: after node allocation, if live_nodes > threshold where threshold = max(initial, last_reorder_nodes × growth) (growth default 2.0) and dynamic reorder enabled → call sift(), then threshold ← live_nodes × growth. (P11: Somenzi CUDD notes on GC threshold policy — same growth-factor logic.)
  • Handle stability: Bdd(u32) indexes nodes by identity, not level position. Reorder permutes levels, never node indices → external Bdd handles stay valid (CRITICAL: callers holding init/trans across sift() see identical handles for identical functions). (ADR-03: index-arena design ENABLES this — Rc<Node> pointer would need updating across the entire graph on every level swap; see F8 for the incident class this prevents.)
  • Partitioned image: Img(S) = (exists present. (AND_i T_i AND S))[next→present]. Schedule = order clusters by support; for each cluster conjoin then exists the present vars that appear in no later cluster (early quantification) via and_exists(acc, T_i, quant_cube_i). Disjunctive: Img(S) = OR_a (exists present.(T_a AND S))[next→present] (legal only when parts cover disjoint transition sources — asynchronous composition). (P10: Burch-Clarke-Long; P9: Coudert-Berthet-Madre early quantification.)
  • Fair-CTL: EG_fair p = gfp Z. p AND AND_j EX( E[Z U (Z AND F_j)] ) for Buchi fairness sets F_j; fair_states = EG_fair true; ordinary CTL operators relativized to reachable ∩ fair frontier. (B1 §9: fairness in CTL; P4: Emerson-Lei for fair-cycle computation.)
  • Streett: pairs (assume_i, guard_i); satisfied cycle infinitely visits guard_i whenever it infinitely visits assume_i. Reduce to generalized-Buchi via standard Streett→Buchi or evaluate via nested Emerson-Lei in fair.rs. (B5 §10: Streett acceptance in symbolic model checking.)
  • Completeness threshold: recurrence_diameter rd = longest loop-free path length (BMC query: longest simple path); reachability_diameter d = least n with reach_{n} == reach_{n+1} (from Phase 6 fixpoint iteration count). Safety BMC complete at k = d; liveness/general uses rd. is_complete(k) returns k >= threshold. (P6: Biere et al. completeness thresholds — the theoretical grounding; I-F9: returning NO_COUNTEREXAMPLE_UP_TO_K not VERIFIED unless is_complete(k).)

12.3 Deliverable

smv-bdd::BddManager performs node-count-triggered sifting (smv bdd-stats shows reorder count + peak vs. final nodes); smv-model exposes PartTrans; smv-check image/CTL/LTL run over the partitioned relation with fairness; smv-bmc reports unsafe (cex@k) or safe (verified, k≥threshold). CLI flags: --reorder=off|sift, --partition=mono|conj|disj, --fairness=<expr>..., --bmc-complete. One reproducible benchmark script demonstrating ≥10× node + time reduction.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Canonicity preserved: for 10⁵ random functions, sift() then re-evaluate → handle equality identical to pre-reorder for every equal pair; satcount/node_count of each root function unchanged (reorder changes node count, never the function's satcount).
  • Node reduction: dining-philosophers N=10..40 peak node_count ≥ 10× smaller with --partition=conj --reorder=sift than mono/off; arbiter N=16 reachable-set build ≤ 1/10 the nodes.
  • Speedup: end-to-end CTL check on benchmark suite ≥ 10× faster wall-clock; cache hit rate ≥ 90% (bdd-stats).
  • Fairness: fair-CTL EG_fair/LTL fair-cycle verdicts match NuSMV on fairness-bearing models (mutex with FAIRNESS, ring arbiter).
  • Completeness: for a model proven safe by reachability, smv bmc --bmc-complete returns safe at k = d (and not before); for a buggy model returns unsafe with the shortest cex.

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expect
tests/reorder/random_funcs.rs post-sift handle eq == pre; satcount invariant; reorder reduces nodes on a crafted bad order ≥ 5×
tests/reorder/canonicity_after_swap.smv bdd_true != bdd_false; equal subformulas share one handle after sifting
tests/partition/philos_{10,20,40}.smv + .expect reachable-state count == NuSMV; peak nodes ≤ documented bound
tests/partition/async_ring.smv disjunctive image == monolithic image (same reachable Bdd)
tests/fairness/mutex_fair.smv (+ FAIRNESS running) AG AF crit holds only with fairness; verdict matches NuSMV
tests/fairness/streett_pair.smv Streett accept == generalized-Buchi reduction
tests/bmc/safe_counter.smv --bmc-complete → safe at k=d; k=d-1 → unknown
tests/bmc/buggy_overflow.smv unsafe, cex length == shortest, Trace replays to bad state
Malformed: bad_fairness_undeclared_var.smv ParseErrorKind::UnknownVar
Malformed: partition_empty_cluster.smv builder error, not a silent wrong-Img

12.6 Pitfalls

  • Reorder mid-operation corruption — sifting while an ite/and_exists recursion holds raw level assumptions → inconsistent DAG. Root cause: reentrant reorder. Fix: reorder only at safe points (between top-level ops); guard with a reorder_lock; never trigger inside ite. Debug: assert no in-flight op when sift() entered.
  • Stale computed cache after swap — cache keyed by (f,g,h) handles remains valid (handles stable), BUT any cache entry that encoded level (none should) breaks. Root cause: leaking level into a cache key. Fix: cache keys are handles only; still clear_on_reorder() if any level-dependent op cached. Debug: differential apply-vs-truth-table after each reorder.
  • Early-quantification quantifies too early — exists-ing a present var still needed by a later cluster → wrong (smaller) Img, silently fewer reachable states. Root cause: support analysis off-by-one. Fix: a present var is eliminable at cluster i only if absent from S and from all clusters > i. Debug: compare partitioned Img to monolithic on small models.
  • Disjunctive partition on synchronous model — disjunctive Img is correct only for disjoint-source (interleaving) transitions; applying to synchronous product double-counts or drops. Root cause: wrong PartKind. Fix: choose Disj only for explicitly asynchronous composition; default Conj.
  • Fairness ignored ⇒ spurious liveness pass/fail — omitting fairness makes AF/EG use the full transition relation, yielding NuSMV-mismatching verdicts on models with FAIRNESS/JUSTICE. Root cause: forgot to relativize. Fix: thread EncodedFsm.fairness into every fixpoint; empty fairness ⇒ trivially-fair (no change).
  • Completeness threshold too small — using reachability diameter d for a liveness property (needs recurrence diameter rd ≥ d) reports safe while a longer lasso cex exists. Root cause: wrong diameter for property class. Fix: safety→d, liveness/general→rd. Debug: cross-check with BDD verdict on the same model.

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • Sifting is O(n²) adjacent swaps worst case (n = vars); cap by maxGrowth prune + sift-budget (max swaps per trigger, e.g. 5n); skip variables below a node-count floor.
  • Reorder amortization: trigger geometrically (growth=2.0) so total reorder time stays O(total ops); over-frequent reorder thrashes — measure reorder time fraction, keep < 25% of run.
  • Early quantification is the single biggest lever: a good schedule turns an exponential intermediate AND_i T_i into linear-size partial products; schedule quality dominates raw node-table speed.
  • Cache too small ⇒ recompute storms (hit rate < 70%); too large ⇒ pollutes L2/L3 and slows GC scan. Target hit rate 90%; size cache as a fraction (e.g. 1×) of unique-table entries; power-of-two for masking.
  • Unique-table load factor: rehash at > 0.75; preallocate per benchmark from bdd-stats peak to avoid rehash storms during reachability.

12.8 Edge Cases

  • 0 fairness sets: fair-CTL/Emerson-Lei degenerate to ordinary CTL/EG — assert identical to Phase 7/10 verdicts.
  • 1-variable model: sifting is a no-op (no swap possible); must not divide-by-zero in growth math.
  • Already-optimal order: sift() finds no improvement → returns with swaps>0 but net node delta 0; must restore exact original positions (handle eq preserved).
  • Single-cluster partition: PartTrans { parts: [trans], kind: Conj } must equal monolithic image exactly.
  • Empty reachable set / unsatisfiable init: completeness threshold d=0; BMC safe immediately at k=0.
  • Streett with empty assume: pair (false, guard) ⇒ unconditional Buchi on guard.
  • Reorder during BMC: BMC path uses CNF, not BDDs — sifting MUST NOT run inside Unroller (no BDDs to reorder); guard.

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment
Reorder breaks canonicity (gravest) post-reorder differential: node_count of a known function ↔ recomputed; random apply-vs-truth-table abort verdict; panic!("canonicity violated after sift"); never emit a result
Wrong Img from bad schedule partitioned vs. monolithic reachable-set Bdd mismatch on regression model fall back to mono; log offending cluster order
Fairness verdict diverges from NuSMV Phase-13 differential corpus flags spec mark spec nondeterministic-fairness; do not auto-pass
BMC safe but BDD says unsafe cross-engine consistency check at threshold trust BDD verdict; flag completeness-threshold bug
Reorder thrash (no termination) reorder-time fraction > 50% watchdog disable dynamic reorder, finish with current order, warn
Cache hit rate collapse bdd-stats hit rate < 50% resize cache up; surface as perf warning, not wrong answer

All optimization failures degrade to the correct, slower path; none may change a verdict. Canonicity violation is fail-stop (no result is worse than a wrong result).

12.10 Operational Notes

  • smv bdd-stats reports: reorder_count, peak_nodes, final_nodes, cache hits/misses/hit_rate, unique-table load factor, time in reorder.
  • --reorder=off|sift (default sift for check/reachable, off for bmc); --reorder-growth=<f> (default 2.0); --sift-budget=<n>.
  • --partition=mono|conj|disj (default conj); --schedule=auto|none; --dump-schedule prints cluster order + quant cubes.
  • --fairness="<expr>" (repeatable) supplies extra Buchi constraints; --streett "(a,g)" for Streett pairs; merges with model FAIRNESS.
  • --bmc-complete enables completeness; prints chosen diameter (d vs. rd) and the k at which safe was certified.
  • Determinism: sifting variable-selection order MUST be deterministic (decreasing node count, ties by level index) so runs are reproducible; seedless. Log every reorder decision at -vv for replay.
  • Regression guard: CI fails if peak-node or wall-clock on the benchmark suite regresses > 15% vs. recorded baselines (benches/baseline.json).

25. Phase 13 — Validation: differential testing, fuzzing, benchmarks

The done gate. All prior phases ship code; this phase proves the code correct (vs NuSMV/nuXmv oracle), robust (fuzz, zero crashes for CI budget), and fast enough (criterion vs targets, regression-gated). Nothing here adds engine features — it adds the validation surface and the pass/fail ledger that authorizes release.

13.1 Objectives

  • Differential oracle: same model.smv + CTLSPEC/LTLSPEC corpus through smv and NuSMV/nuXmv → assert identical verdicts (HOLD/FAIL) + comparable counterexamples (same length class, valid Trace).
  • BDD property tests: ite/and/or/xor/not vs brute-force truth table (≤16 vars); canonicity preserved across GC + sifting reorder (handle equality iff truth-table equality).
  • Fuzz targets: bdd_apply, parse, encode_check — no panic / no UB / no canonicity break for the CI fuzz budget.
  • Conformance buckets: reachability, CTL, LTL, BMC each a labeled corpus with golden verdicts → a single pass/fail ledger.
  • Benchmarks: criterion suite (reachable-state scale, BDD node counts, ite throughput, fixpoint + BMC latency) with absolute targets + regression gates; publish report.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

New top-level dirs (not crates — test/bench fixtures + harness binaries):

Path Role
corpus/models/*.smv shared model+spec corpus (CTL, LTL, INVARSPEC)
corpus/golden/*.json golden verdict + trace metadata per (model, spec)
tests/differential/ smv-test-diff bin: run engine + NuSMV, compare
tests/conformance/ #[test] buckets reach_*, ctl_*, ltl_*, bmc_*
fuzz/fuzz_targets/ cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) targets bdd_apply.rs, parse.rs, encode_check.rs
benches/ criterion: bdd_ite.rs, reachable.rs, ctl_fix.rs, ltl_prod.rs, bmc_unroll.rs
scripts/oracle.sh invoke NuSMV/nuXmv, scrape verdict + cex
  • Differential harness (smv-test-diff): for each (model, spec) run smv check/smv bmc → Verdict { holds: bool, cex: Option<Trace> }; run oracle via scripts/oracle.sh parsing NuSMV stdout (-- specification ... is true|false, Counterexample block). Compare: verdict must match; if FAIL, both produce a cex — assert engine Trace length ≤ oracle length (shortest-path CTL cex, §20) and is valid (replay each StateAssignment step satisfies trans, loopback closes a fair lasso for LTL/EG). (I-P11: NuSMV is the oracle; R2: NuSMV 2.6 source is the reference; R6: PRISM/NuSMV model corpus for known verdicts.)
  • Property tests (proptest in smv-bdd/tests): generate random boolean exprs over n≤16 levels; eval engine Bdd via restrict per assignment vs direct truth table; satcount(f,n) == popcount of truth table; not(not f)==f; and/or commutative/associative; canonicity: build f twice via different op orders → identical Bdd handle (==). (ADR-22: proptest as a structured generator; P1/P2: canonicity invariant — the test being validated.)
  • Reorder invariant test: build corpus FSMs, snapshot Bdd truth tables, run sifting (§24), assert every recorded function's truth table unchanged though node_count may shrink; handle equality re-established under new order. (P7/ADR-08: sifting must preserve all function semantics — this test is the invariant assertion.)
  • Fuzz (cargo fuzz): bdd_apply — bytes → op-tape over a bounded var set, replay against truth-table model, assert agreement + no panic; parse — bytes as UTF-8 SMV source → Parser must return Ok(SmvModule) or Err(ParseError), never panic/hang (1s timeout); encode_check — fuzz parseable models → Encoder + CtlChecker::check must not panic, BDD stays canonical (debug assert_canonical()). (ADR-22: cargo-fuzz as coverage-guided fuzzer; I-P7: parser must be total over arbitrary bytes.)
  • Conformance ledger: each bucket test loads corpus/golden/*.json, runs engine, diffs → emit LedgerRow { id, kind, expected, actual, pass }; aggregate to target/conformance.json + a printed table; CI fails on any pass==false. (ADR-21: NuSMV as primary differential oracle; R6: model corpus for known verdicts.)
  • Bench gates: criterion --save-baseline; CI compares to committed baseline, fails if any bench regresses > 10% wall-clock or any node_count metric regresses > 5%. (ADR-23: criterion for statistically-rigorous microbenchmarks.)

13.3 Deliverable

cargo test --workspace green (incl. conformance buckets + property tests); cargo fuzz run <target> clean for the CI budget (default 60 s/target, 3 targets); cargo bench produces target/criterion + a generated BENCHMARKS.md report (table: metric / target / measured / pass); target/conformance.json ledger all-pass; smv-test-diff --corpus corpus/ exits 0 (every verdict matches NuSMV). One command: make validate runs all four and prints PASS/FAIL summary.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Criterion Target
A1 Differential verdict match 100% of corpus (≥ 60 model×spec pairs); 0 mismatches
A2 CTL/LTL/reach/BMC ledger all buckets pass; ≥ 12 cases each
A3 Property tests 4096 proptest cases/target, 0 shrink-failures
A4 Canonicity post-reorder 0 truth-table changes across all snapshots
A5 Fuzz 0 crashes/hangs/canonicity-asserts for CI budget
A6 ite throughput ≥ 5M ops/s single-thread on the 12-var bench
A7 Reachability scale ≥ 10^20 reachable states model verified < 30 s, < 2 GB
A8 BMC latency k=20 unroll+solve on bench model < 10 s
A9 Bench regression gate no bench > 10% slower / > 5% more nodes vs baseline

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Spec Expected
corpus/models/mutex.smv AG !(c1 & c2) (CTLSPEC) HOLD; ledger ctl_mutex pass
corpus/models/mutex.smv AG (t1 -> AF c1) HOLD (with fairness)
corpus/models/counter8.smv EF (val = 255) HOLD; reach cex len = 255 ≤ oracle
corpus/models/sem_bad.smv AG !(c1 & c2) FAIL; cex lasso replays, len matches NuSMV class
corpus/models/req_ack.smv G (req -> F ack) (LTLSPEC) HOLD; LtlChecker no fair cycle
corpus/models/req_ack_bad.smv G (req -> F ack) FAIL; fair lasso loopback==Some
corpus/models/safety.smv INVARSPEC bad=0 BMC k=12 UNSAT; CTL HOLD; agree
Malformed corpus/bad/type_mismatch.smv — Err(ParseErrorKind::TypeMismatch), no panic
Malformed corpus/bad/circular.smv — Err(CircularDefine)
Malformed corpus/bad/oob_range.smv next(x):=99 Err(OutOfRange)
Golden corpus/golden/*.json {id,kind,holds,cex_len,loopback} byte-stable; regenerated only via --bless

13.6 Pitfalls

  • Oracle parsing fragility → NuSMV cex format differs nuXmv vs NuSMV and across versions; root cause: scraping free text. Pin version in scripts/oracle.sh, parse structured -- specification lines; debug by dumping captured stdout to target/oracle/<id>.txt on mismatch.
  • Cex incomparability ≠ bug → multiple valid shortest counterexamples exist; comparing trace equality gives false failures. Compare only: same verdict + engine len ≤ oracle len + engine Trace independently valid (replays through trans). Root cause: nondeterministic BDD cube pick (§20).
  • Truth-table test blows up → property test with n > 20 vars → 2^n table OOM. Cap fuzz/proptest at n ≤ 16 (64 Ki rows); larger functions validated only by differential, not brute force.
  • Reorder test false alarm → comparing Bdd handles before/after sifting fails because indices change; only truth tables / function identity are invariant under reorder, not raw u32. Snapshot truth tables, not handles.
  • Fuzz finds a real canonicity break that "passes" → if assert_canonical() is debug-only and fuzz builds release, silent corruption (the GRAVEST class). Build fuzz targets with debug_assertions=true (in fuzz/Cargo.toml profile) so canonicity asserts fire.
  • Bench variance flagged as regression → cold cache / CPU turbo skews wall-clock past the 10% gate. Pin governor, warm up, use criterion's noise threshold; gate on median not mean.

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • Differential harness dominated by NuSMV process spawn (~50–200 ms each) — run engine side in-process, oracle side parallel (rayon/xargs), cache oracle verdicts in corpus/golden/ so CI default path skips spawning NuSMV (only --refresh-oracle re-runs it).
  • Fuzz throughput: bdd_apply target must build a fresh BddManager per input — pool/reset arena to keep ≥ 100k exec/s; avoid GC churn by capping tape length.
  • Bench harness isolates allocation: report node_count/peak-arena separately from wall-clock so a node-count regression is caught even if time noise hides it.
  • Corpus build (encode all FSMs) is O(seconds) — memoize EncodedFsm per model across buckets to keep cargo test under ~60 s.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty reachable set (unsatisfiable init + INVAR) → reachable = bdd_false; every CTL AG p vacuously HOLD; oracle must agree; assert no divide-by-zero in satcount-based scale metric.
  • init already violates the spec (cex length 0) → engine Trace.states.len()==1, loopback==None; differential must accept len-0/1 cex.
  • LTL spec that is a tautology (G(p|!p)) → no Buchi for !phi reachable; verdict HOLD with no product blowup; bench guard against degenerate-automaton stall.
  • Model with 0 fairness constraints vs many → both must validate; Emerson-Lei degenerates to plain EG true reachability when no fairness.
  • BMC k below completeness threshold returns UNSAT for a true safety prop → correct but inconclusive; ledger marks bmc_* as "no-cex-≤-k" not "HOLD" unless k ≥ threshold (§24).
  • Non-UTF-8 / huge fuzz input to parse → lexer rejects with ParseError, bounded memory; no infinite loop on unterminated case.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Symptom Likely root cause Bisect
Verdict mismatch, engine says HOLD oracle FAIL missing fairness or wrong trans (next-INVAR dropped, §17) dump node_count(trans), replay oracle cex through engine trans
