Verdict mismatch, both FAIL, engine cex longer non-shortest onion-ring (§20 bug) check backward frontier sets monotone
Property test fails canonicity unique-table miss / wrong ite terminal case (Phase 1) shrink to minimal expr, dump (level,low,high) of dup node
Reorder changes a truth table sifting swap not preserving function (§24) snapshot before/after each swap, binary-search the swap
Fuzz panic in encode_check unhandled VarType::Range width / enum cardinality 1 save crashing .smv to fuzz/artifacts/, minimize
Bench node-count regression cache disabled or GC too aggressive diff criterion baseline, check computed-cache hit rate

13.10 Operational Notes

  • CI matrix: make validate on every PR; full fuzz (1 h/target) nightly; criterion baseline committed under benches/baseline/ and refreshed only on tagged release via --bless.
  • corpus/golden/*.json and oracle cache are the authority; regenerate exclusively through --refresh-oracle + --bless (never hand-edited) to keep the ledger reproducible.
  • BENCHMARKS.md published as a CI artifact each run; release notes link the report and the all-pass conformance.json.
  • Reproducibility: pin NuSMV version, Rust toolchain (MSRV 1.78), and proptest/fuzz seeds (PROPTEST_SEED, libFuzzer -seed=) so any failure replays deterministically.
  • Done gate: project ships only when A1–A9 all green, fuzz clean for CI budget, and BENCHMARKS.md meets every target — this is the contract that closes Project 281.

Threat Model

Scope. smv is an offline analysis tool, not a network service: no listener, no auth, no multi-tenant boundary. The realistic adversary is a malicious or malformed input artifact processed by an automated pipeline (CI gate, batch verifier, reduced-model fuzz harness) where a crash, hang, or wrong verdict is a denial-of-service or — worse — a silent integrity failure. We model threats against availability (DoS) and integrity (wrong verdict); confidentiality is out of scope (single-user, no secrets in the BDD package).

25.1 Trust boundaries

   UNTRUSTED                            TRUSTED (our code)                       SEMI-TRUSTED
 +-----------------------+   parse    +--------------------------+   FFI/DIMACS  +------------------+
 | model.smv file        |----------->| smv-syntax (Lexer/Parser)|-------------->| external         |
 | CTLSPEC/LTLSPEC strs  |  Result    | smv-logic (prop parsers) |  CnfFormula   | SatBackend impl  |
 | --order file (var ord)|  only      | smv-model (Encoder)      |               | (#277 / 3rd pty) |
 | k bound, CLI flags    |            | smv-check / smv-bmc      |<--------------| assignment out   |
 +-----------------------+            | smv-bdd (BddManager)     |   SatResult   +------------------+
        B1 (data)                     +--------------------------+    B2 (FFI)
  • B1 — input boundary (primary). Everything crossing it is hostile: the .smv model, every CTLSPEC/LTLSPEC string, an externally-supplied --order variable ordering, the BMC bound k, CLI flags. Crossing B1 is allowed only via Result-returning APIs (Parser, property parsers, Encoder); no panic!/unwrap/expect/array-index-without-bound on B1-derived data.
  • B2 — SatBackend boundary (BMC only). The SatBackend trait is the single seam to foreign code. We hand it a CnfFormula (DIMACS literals) and trust only the returned SatResult. A lying backend (returns Sat with a bogus assignment, or Unsat falsely) is contained by independent verdict re-validation (§25.2 T5b), so even a buggy/adversarial solver cannot silently flip a verdict.
  • Inside the trust boundary the load-bearing invariant is ROBDD canonicity (Phase 1, §13). It is internally enforced, but a logic bug there is the gravest threat (T5) because it corrupts results with no external signal.

25.2 Threat catalog

Severity: Crit = silent wrong verdict (integrity); High = crash/hang/OOM (availability); Med = degraded perf / surprising error.

# Threat Vector across B1/B2 Sev Mitigation Detection Telemetry Recovery
T1 BDD blow-up DoS — model explodes node count → OOM huge Enum/Range (many bits), pathological var order, deep arithmetic / wide Case, multiplier-style relations (exp BDD) High hard max_nodes cap (default 2^26 ≈ 67M nodes ≈ ~2–4 GB) checked in unique-table insert; max_levels cap; GC before grow unique-table insert returns BddError::NodeLimit when next-grow would exceed cap; per-op node_count watermark log peak_nodes, gc_count, unique_table_load, cache_evictions, growing-BDD op typed error to CLI, nonzero exit; never abort()/OOM-kill; suggest --order/sifting/--reorder
T2 BMC SAT-bomb — unrolling to enormous k or clause count adversarial --bmc-k (e.g. 10^9), or model whose per-step clause blow-up makes CnfFormula.nvars/clauses huge High max_k cap (default 10^4); max_clauses (default 2^28) + max_vars caps on CnfFormula; Tseitin var counter is u64 with overflow check; reject before emitting DIMACS Unroller returns BmcError::BoundTooLarge / ClauseLimit; pre-flight estimate nvars*k vs cap log k, nvars, clauses, bytes_emitted, per-step clause delta typed error; advise lower k or --complete threshold mode
T3 Parser DoS / panic — adversarial SMV crashes or hangs front end deep nesting (10^6 parens / Case arms / Next), huge token stream, non-UTF-8, unterminated literals, integer literals overflowing i64 High recursive-descent depth limit (default 1024 → ParseErrorKind::UnexpectedToken/nesting); bounded lexer lookahead (1 token); i64 literal parse is checked → OutOfRange; input size cap (default 64 MiB) Parser/Lexer return Result<_, ParseError>; zero panics on any byte sequence — enforced by fuzzing log tokens, max_depth, input_bytes, parse_ms precise ParseError { kind, span } (line:col); exit 2; no partial mutation of global state
T4 Fixpoint non-termination — reachable/EU/EG/Emerson-Lei never converges none directly: finite BDD lattice ⇒ monotone lfp/gfp must terminate. Risk is a bug (non-monotone step, wrong cube) presenting as an apparent hang Crit/High termination is proven by the finite-height lattice + monotone step (Knaster–Tarski); guard with max_iters watchdog (default 10·num_levels, never hit on a correct engine) iteration counter exceeds watchdog ⇒ CheckError::FixpointDiverged (a bug signal, treated as internal error, files a repro) log per-fixpoint iters, frontier_nodes, monotonicity check (Z_{i+1} ⊇ Z_i for lfp) in debug abort that check with internal-error exit; emit minimized model for the bug tracker; never spin forever
T5 Wrong verdict via canonicity break — gravest, silent not externally injectable, but any smv-bdd bug (reduction rule miss, unique-table hash collision mishandled, stale computed-cache entry surviving GC, complement-edge sign error) corrupts equality ⇒ wrong fixpoint / verdict, no crash Crit reduction enforced at construction (ite only path; merge-isomorphic + delete-redundant); cache keyed by full (f,g,h); GC must flush computed cache of dead entries (canonical bug class); complement-edge canonical-form rule (e.g. high edge never complemented) debug_assert canonicity on insert (no node with low==high; no duplicate in unique table); periodic check_canonical(root) full walk in tests; differential testing vs NuSMV on a corpus (same verdict) log node_count, satcount, verdict + counterexample length per spec for diff harness CI gate: any NuSMV verdict mismatch fails the build; canonicity assertion fires in debug → bug, not a recovery
T5b Lying SatBackend — foreign solver returns wrong SatResult malicious/buggy backend over B2 returns Sat(bogus) or false Unsat Crit re-validate independently: on Sat(model), substitute into CnfFormula and check every clause is satisfied before building Trace; treat trace→model replay as the SAT witness clause-check failure ⇒ BmcError::BackendUnsound; Unsat from BMC only bounds (never proves a CTL/LTL "holds" beyond completeness threshold) log backend id/version, solve_ms, model size, validation result reject the result, fall back to bundled DPLL or error; never trust an unvalidated Sat
T6 Integer overflow in encoding / counting Range(lo,hi) with hi-lo near i64::MAX; satcount of a function over many vars; 2^bits width compute; level/var-id arithmetic Med/Crit ceil(log2) width via checked ops; reject ranges needing > max_var_bits (default 64) → OutOfRange; satcount returns u128 (per spec) and uses checked <</+, saturating at u128::MAX with a truncated flag; var-id space u32 with insert-time bound checked-arithmetic returns Err; debug builds run with overflow checks on (overflow-checks = true) in CI log range_bits, total_state_bits, satcount (+truncated flag) typed EncodeError; refuse to encode rather than wrap silently
T7 Supply-chain — malicious/yanked transitive dep, or hostile external SatBackend crate cargo dependency graph; dynamically/linked 3rd-party solver Med minimal deps (smv-bdd/syntax/model/logic are no_std + alloc, near-zero deps); deny.toml (cargo-deny) bans unknown licenses, duplicate/yanked crates, advisory-DB vulns; Cargo.lock committed; SatBackend is a trait so the only external solver is opt-in and isolated behind B2 + T5b re-validation CI runs cargo deny check (advisories/bans/licenses/sources) + cargo audit; reproducible build hash record dep tree hash, deny.toml policy version pin/patch/replace offending crate; drop optional solver feature; core stays buildable with zero external solver
T8 Adversarial --order file (semi-trusted var order) user supplies a variable order that is a permutation but pathological (worst-case BDD size) or invalid (dup/missing var, out-of-range index) Med/High validate order is a total bijection over declared vars before use → else error; pathological-but-valid orders fall under T1 caps + optional sifting order validator returns EncodeError::BadVarOrder; node caps catch the blow-up log supplied vs derived order, resulting peak_nodes reject invalid order; for valid-but-bad, suggest --reorder (sifting)
T9 Spec formula blow-up — adversarial CTL/LTL formula with deep/wide temporal nesting explodes the Büchi automaton or product BDD independent of model size many nested U/G/F subformulas (GPVW generalized-Büchi state count is exponential in |φ|, C10); deeply alternated AG(AF(EG(...))) High same max_nodes/max_levels caps as T1 apply to the automaton-product BDD (automaton state bits are ordinary levels); pre-flight subformula-count estimate before construction product-BDD construction hits BddError::NodeCapExceeded via the identical T1 path; bdd-stats reports automaton-added level count log subformula_count, estimated automaton states (C10 formula), added state bits typed error; suggest simplifying the spec (fewer nested temporal operators) or raising max_nodes explicitly

25.3 Cross-cutting controls

  • Panic-free B1 contract. All B1/B2-reachable code is #![deny(clippy::unwrap_used, clippy::expect_used, clippy::indexing_slicing, clippy::panic)]; faults surface as ParseError/EncodeError/BddError/CheckError/BmcError. A panic reaching smv-cli is itself a bug report (caught at the binary edge, exit 70 = internal), never a normal failure mode.
  • Resource budget, one place. Every cap (max_nodes, max_levels, max_k, max_clauses, max_vars, depth, input bytes, max_iters) is a field on a single Limits struct, defaulted conservatively, overridable by flag, logged at startup. No unbounded loop or unbounded allocation is reachable from B1.
  • Fuzzing as the parser/encoder guarantee (T3, T1). cargo-fuzz targets: fuzz_parse (arbitrary bytes → Parser never panics, always Result), fuzz_spec (CTL/LTL strings), fuzz_encode (random well-typed SmvModule → Encoder respects node cap, no overflow). Corpus seeded from the NuSMV diff corpus. Goal: zero panics / zero ASan findings.
  • Differential oracle is the integrity guarantee (T5). Because a canonicity bug is silent, the only trustworthy detector at scale is agreement with NuSMV on verdict (and comparable counterexample length) across the Phase 13 corpus, plus randomized BDD apply-vs-truth-table and check_canonical property tests. This is the project "done" gate, not an optional extra.
  • Determinism. No wall-clock, no RNG, no thread nondeterminism on the verdict path ⇒ a wrong verdict is reproducible from the input alone, which makes T4/T5 triage tractable. Sifting (T1 mitigation) uses a fixed, seeded heuristic so reordering never makes a verdict input-nondeterministic.

25.4 Residual risk (accepted)

Risk Why accepted
Valid model genuinely exceeds max_nodes (real exponential blow-up, e.g. a 32-bit multiplier) Inherent to BDDs; surfaced as a clean NodeLimit error with a sifting/BMC suggestion — not a defect.
BMC Unsat at bound k does not prove the property holds By design; only sound up to the completeness threshold (Phase 12). CLI labels the result no counterexample within k, never "holds".
A correctly-validated SatBackend that is merely slow Treated as user-supplied perf, bounded by T2 caps + an optional --sat-timeout; not a security threat.

Failure Modes and Recovery

Taxonomy of every way the smv verifier can go wrong, ranked by severity. Severity scale: S0 = silent wrong verdict (catastrophic — defeats the tool's only purpose); S1 = crash / OOM / abort (loud, recoverable, never wrong); S2 = degraded (slow / partial / typed error); S3 = cosmetic. The two S0 classes — canonicity violation and verdict-soundness drift — dominate this design: a model checker that crashes is harmless, one that lies is worse than useless. Every S0 path below has redundant detection (in-process self-check plus out-of-process NuSMV differential).

26.1 Severity Ledger (master index)

# Failure class Sev Layer (crate) Crash? Wrong verdict risk Primary detector
F1 Parse / type / range error S2 smv-syntax no none (rejects input) ParseError returned
F2 Encoding error (enum/Range) S2 smv-model no yes if undetected out-of-range constraint + roundtrip
F3 Node-cap overflow S1→S2 smv-bdd no (typed) none allocation guard
F4 Computed-cache corruption S0 smv-bdd no yes cache-disabled cross-check
F5 Canonicity violation S0 smv-bdd no yes (gravest) unique-table audit + NuSMV diff
F6 Fixpoint non-convergence S1 smv-check yes (assert) none (aborts) monotonicity + iteration cap
F7 Image variable leak S0 smv-check no yes support-set assertion + diff
F8 Reorder handle invalidation S0/S1 smv-bdd maybe yes if stale handle read epoch tag on Bdd
F9 BMC incompleteness as "verified" S0 smv-bmc/cli no yes (reports false PASS) threshold gate + verdict label
F10 Counterexample fails re-simulation S1 smv-check/cli no none (Trace invalid) mandatory replay validator
F11 Buchi mistranslation (LTL) S0 smv-logic no yes small-formula diff vs NuSMV
F12 SatBackend disagreement S1 smv-bmc no yes if trusted UNSAT-core / model recheck

26.2 F1 — Parse / type / range errors (S2, benign)

  • Symptoms: smv check model.smv exits nonzero; ParseError { kind, span } printed; no FSM built.
  • Root cause: malformed SMV — UnexpectedToken, UnknownVar, BadType, TypeMismatch, CircularDefine, OutOfRange.
  • Detection: structural — Lexer/Parser and the name+type pass reject at source; CircularDefine via DFS over DefineDecl dependency graph (back-edge ⇒ error).
  • Automatic recovery: none needed — invalid input is correctly refused; never proceeds to encoding.
  • Manual recovery: operator fixes model at reported span (line:col, caret underline).
  • Operator actions: smv check --explain dumps the resolved type of each var/define.
  • Forensic artifacts: ParseError with byte span; token stream on --dump-tokens.
  • Long-term mitigation: golden-file corpus of malformed inputs (one per ParseErrorKind); fuzz the Parser (afl) — must never panic, only return ParseError. Invariant: parser is total over arbitrary bytes.

26.3 F2 — Boolean-encoding errors (S2, latent-S0 if undetected)

  • Symptoms: enum/Range var admits illegal value, or two distinct SMV values collide on the same bit-vector.
  • Root cause: VarEncoding allocates ceil(log2(|domain|)) bits ⇒ unused codes (e.g. 5-value enum → 3 bits → 3 spurious codes); Range(lo,hi) likewise. Forgetting the out-of-range constraint lets the BDD trans/init range over phantom states ⇒ wrong reachable set (would become S0).
  • Detection: every enum/Range var emits a constraint BDD valid_v AND-ed into invar; encode/decode roundtrip test — for every legal value decode(encode(v)) == v and every illegal code maps to bdd_false under valid_v. CLI smv encode --check runs this.
  • Automatic recovery: none — constraint is always conjoined; phantom codes are pruned by construction.
  • Manual recovery: widen the declared Range/enum if a legal value was rejected.
  • Operator actions: smv encode prints the VarEncoding.levels and bit budget per var; flags any domain that is not a power of two (where phantom codes exist).
  • Forensic artifacts: per-var level map; valid_v node_count.
  • Long-term mitigation: property test — satcount(invar, nbits) equals the product of declared domain sizes (no phantom states survive).

26.4 F3 — Node-cap / unique-table overflow (S1, graceful typed)

  • Symptoms: BDD growth blows the configured node budget mid-and_exists/ite; operation returns Err(BddError::NodeCapExceeded { cap, requested }) rather than aborting.
  • Root cause: intermediate BDDs (esp. monolithic trans, or a bad variable order) explode — classic exponential blowup.
  • Detection: BddManager checks live-node count against cap before each unique-table insert; GC (mark-sweep of dead nodes) runs first; only a post-GC breach errors.
  • Automatic recovery: (1) trigger mark-sweep GC; (2) if dynamic reordering enabled, attempt one sifting pass; (3) retry the op once. Reordering preserves canonicity (only var order changes, functions invariant).
  • Manual recovery: raise --node-cap; supply a hand variable order via --order file; switch trans from monolithic to conjunctive partitioning with early quantification.
  • Operator actions: smv bdd-stats reports peak live nodes, GC count, reclaimed nodes, table fill factor; suggests reordering when peak/final ratio > 10.
  • Forensic artifacts: node-count timeline; per-cluster sizes of the partitioned trans.
  • Long-term mitigation: default to conjunctive partitioning + early-quantification schedule (Phase 12); sifting on by default with a size-growth guard. No silent truncation — overflow is always a typed error, never a wrong answer.

26.5 F4 — Computed-cache corruption (S0)

  • Symptoms: ite/and_exists returns an answer inconsistent with a cache-disabled recompute; downstream verdicts unstable across runs.
  • Root cause: stale cache entry not invalidated after GC reclaimed a node and its u32 index was recycled (ABA on Bdd handles); or a hash collision treated as a hit.
  • Detection: cache must be a pure memo — --audit-cache runs every memoized op twice (cache-on vs cache-cleared) and asserts identical Bdd; GC must scrub or generation-tag cache entries referencing freed indices.
  • Automatic recovery: flush the entire computed cache on every GC sweep (cache is rebuildable, never authoritative); recompute on miss.
  • Manual recovery: --no-cache forces correctness at a large speed cost (diagnostic mode only).
  • Operator actions: compare verdict with and without --no-cache; any divergence is a confirmed F4 bug.
  • Forensic artifacts: cache hit/miss/collision counters; the offending (f,g,h) key triple.
  • Long-term mitigation: tie cache-entry validity to a GC generation counter; property test that for random formula DAGs, cached and uncached ite agree over ≥10^6 calls. Cache is an accelerator; it must be provably verdict-neutral.

26.6 F5 — Canonicity violation (S0 — GRAVEST CLASS)

The load-bearing invariant: fixed total var order + two reduction rules (no redundant-test node; no duplicate node) ⇒ structural handle equality iff boolean-function equality, equality test O(1). Break it and every equality, subset test, and fixpoint convergence check silently lies — wrong verdict, no crash.

  • Symptoms: none observable from the verdict alone — the verifier confidently returns PASS or a FAIL that is false. May surface only as a NuSMV differential mismatch.
  • Root cause: a duplicate node admitted into the unique table (hashcons miss / bad hash-eq); a redundant node (low==high) not collapsed; not complement-edge bookkeeping inconsistent; terminal sharing broken.
  • Detection (defence in depth):
    1. Unique-table audit (--audit-canon): full table scan asserts (a) no node has low==high; (b) no two distinct indices share (level,low,high); (c) all level values strictly increase root→leaf along every edge; (d) exactly one bdd_true/bdd_false terminal.
    2. Structural-vs-semantic cross-check: for sampled BDD pairs, (handle_eq) == (satcount-based equivalence over xor).
    3. Apply-vs-truth-table (Phase 13): for all functions over ≤8 vars, BDD ite/and/or/xor matches the brute-force truth table.
    4. NuSMV differential (out-of-process): same model+spec ⇒ same verdict; canonicity bugs that escape in-process checks surface as verdict mismatches.
  • Automatic recovery: none possible at runtime — a violated canonicity invariant means all cached results are suspect; the only safe action is to fail closed: abort with BddError::CanonicityViolated rather than emit any verdict. Never "repair" and continue.
  • Manual recovery: bisect the construction op sequence (--trace-build) to the first audit failure; fix the unique-table insert path; rerun the full differential corpus.
  • Operator actions: run with --audit-canon periodically in CI on every model; treat any NuSMV verdict disagreement as S0 until root-caused.
  • Forensic artifacts: the duplicate/redundant node pair (idx_a, idx_b, level, low, high); the op-trace prefix that built it; a dot export of the malformed sub-DAG.
  • Long-term mitigation: canonicity is a type-level discipline — the only node constructor is the hashcons path (mk_node private; Bdd cannot be forged); reduction rules applied unconditionally inside it; randomized canonicity property test (build same function 2 ways, assert identical handle) on every CI run; differential vs NuSMV is the project "done" gate (Phase 13).

26.7 F6 — Fixpoint non-convergence (S1 — a bug signal, not a normal outcome)

  • Symptoms: reachable / EU / EG iteration does not stabilize; iteration counter exceeds a hard cap.
  • Root cause: should be impossible — state space is finite, lfp/gfp are monotone over a finite lattice, so they must converge in ≤ |reachable| steps. Non-convergence ⇒ the iterate is not monotone ⇒ an underlying F4/F5/F7 bug (broken equality or leaking support).
  • Detection: assert monotonicity each step — for lfp Z_{i+1} ⊇ Z_i (i.e. Z_i AND not Z_{i+1} == bdd_false); for gfp dually; hard cap = (#bool state bits + 1); breach ⇒ panic!("fixpoint non-monotone — canonicity/leak bug").
  • Automatic recovery: none — abort loudly; this is a diagnostic tripwire, never silenced.
  • Manual recovery: run --audit-canon and the F7 support check on the same model; the real bug is upstream.
  • Operator actions: capture per-iteration node_count and satcount deltas.
  • Forensic artifacts: iteration log (set size per step); the first non-monotone step's two BDDs.
  • Long-term mitigation: convergence cap + monotonicity assertion are always compiled in (cheap O(1) handle checks); they turn S0 silent loops into S1 loud aborts.

26.8 F7 — Image-computation variable leak (S0)

  • Symptoms: reachable set too large or too small; CTL EX/preimage wrong; verdict flips.
  • Root cause: Img(S) = (exists present.(trans AND S))[next→present] — forgetting to quantify all present vars, quantifying the wrong cube, or omitting the rename present↔next leaves stray variables in the support ⇒ result ranges over phantom or missing states. Pre-image dual: must quantify next.
  • Detection: assert the support set of every image/pre-image result — after and_exists over the present cube, support(Img(S)) ⊆ {next vars} pre-rename and ⊆ {present vars} post-rename; mismatch ⇒ panic. Cross-check reachable() against an explicit-state count for ≤10^4-state corpus models, and against NuSMV for all.
  • Automatic recovery: none — fail closed.
  • Manual recovery: verify the quantification cube is exactly the present (resp. next) VarMap; verify rename map is the full present↔next bijection.
  • Operator actions: smv reachable --support prints the variable support of the fixpoint at each step.
  • Forensic artifacts: support-set diff (expected vs actual var levels); cube contents.
  • Long-term mitigation: the present/next cubes and VarMap are derived once from EncodedFsm, not hand-built per call; support assertion compiled into every image step.

26.9 F8 — Reorder handle invalidation (S0/S1)

  • Symptoms: after dynamic reordering (sifting), a previously-held Bdd(u32) denotes a different function (its index moved) ⇒ silent wrong result, or an out-of-range index ⇒ panic.
  • Root cause: sifting renumbers nodes / changes levels; any Bdd handle cached by a caller across a reorder is stale (the u32 index is reused).
  • Detection: tag every Bdd with a manager epoch; each reorder bumps the epoch; dereferencing a Bdd whose epoch ≠ manager epoch ⇒ BddError::StaleHandle. Long-lived roots (init, trans, invar, fairness, AP BDDs) are registered in a root set that reordering remaps in place so they stay valid.
  • Automatic recovery: registered roots auto-remapped (epoch refreshed); reordering only fires at safe points (between fixpoint iterations, never mid-ite).
  • Manual recovery: --no-reorder to pin the order if a caller holds unregistered handles.
  • Operator actions: smv bdd-stats reports reorder count and order before/after.
  • Forensic artifacts: the variable-order permutation applied; stale handle's epoch vs current.
  • Long-term mitigation: canonicity is preserved across reorder (functions invariant, only order changes) — property test: verdict identical with sifting on/off; root-set registration is mandatory for any BDD escaping a single op.

26.10 F9 — BMC incompleteness reported as "verified" (S0)

  • Symptoms: smv bmc -k N finds no counterexample to G p and the operator concludes the property holds — but absence at depth ≤ N proves nothing beyond N.
  • Root cause: BMC is a refutation engine: UNSAT at bound k means "no counterexample of length ≤ k", not "property holds", unless k ≥ the completeness threshold (reachability/recurrence diameter).
  • Detection: verdict labels are distinct and never conflated — BMC emits NO_COUNTEREXAMPLE_UP_TO_K(k) (UNSAT, threshold not reached) vs VERIFIED(threshold=d) (UNSAT at k ≥ computed diameter) vs FALSIFIED(trace) (SAT). CLI forbids printing "PASS"/"verified" unless the threshold gate fires.
  • Automatic recovery: if completeness sought, escalate k incrementally and compute the diameter via a simple-path / fixpoint-recurrence check; only then upgrade to VERIFIED.
  • Manual recovery: operator runs the BDD CTL/reachability path (sound for full verification) when the BMC threshold is impractically large.
  • Operator actions: smv bmc --complete runs to the completeness threshold or reports the bound reached; --max-k caps effort.
  • Forensic artifacts: the bound k reached; UNSAT core size; computed/estimated diameter.
  • Long-term mitigation: the only "this property holds" claim with full soundness comes from the symbolic BDD engine; BMC's contract is documented as bounded-refutation + optional threshold completeness. Never let an unbounded UNSAT masquerade as a proof.

26.11 F10 — Counterexample that does not re-simulate (S1)

  • Symptoms: Trace (states + optional loopback lasso) is emitted but replaying it through trans/init does not reproduce the violation.
  • Root cause: decode bug — mapping BDD cubes back to readable StateAssignment over original SMV vars mishandles enum/Range bit-vectors, picks an arbitrary value for a don't-care that violates invar, or the lasso loopback index is off.
  • Detection: mandatory replay validator — before any Trace is shown, re-execute it symbolically: each states[i] must satisfy invar; consecutive pairs must satisfy trans; states[0] must satisfy init; lasso edge states[last]→states[loopback] must satisfy trans. Failure ⇒ Err(TraceInvalid), never display.
  • Automatic recovery: re-extract using onion-ring backward sets (Phase 8) for the shortest witness; re-decode with invar-consistent don't-care resolution.
  • Manual recovery: smv simulate --trace file replays a counterexample step-by-step for inspection.
  • Operator actions: inspect per-step satisfaction flags from the validator.
  • Forensic artifacts: the failing step index and which check (init/trans/invar/loopback) failed; the raw cube vs decoded StateAssignment.
  • Long-term mitigation: counterexample extraction and replay share the same trans/decode code; differential check that NuSMV's counterexample, replayed in our model, also passes our validator.

26.12 F11 — Buchi mistranslation (LTL path, S0)

  • Symptoms: LTL verdict wrong while CTL on the same model is right; or a fair lasso reported for a true property.
  • Root cause: GPVW/tableau (or VWAA) LtlFormula→(generalized) Buchi construction is subtle — NNF error, missing R/U expansion, wrong acceptance (fairness) sets ⇒ Emerson-Lei finds spurious / misses real fair cycles.
  • Detection: small-formula differential — for all LTL formulas up to depth 3 over ≤3 APs on random small Kripke structures, our A φ verdict must equal NuSMV's; product + Emerson-Lei fair-states gfp/lfp cross-checked against an explicit nested-DFS on the same tiny product.
  • Automatic recovery: none — fail closed; LTL verdict suppressed if the self-check corpus regresses.
  • Manual recovery: dump the Buchi automaton (--dump-buchi as dot) and verify acceptance sets by hand against the formula.
  • Operator actions: compare CTL-expressible fragments (e.g. G p) computed via both the LTL and CTL paths — they must agree.
  • Forensic artifacts: automaton states/transitions/acceptance sets; the product fair-state set.
  • Long-term mitigation: the LTL corpus diff vs NuSMV is part of the Phase 13 done gate; not φ translation and the product use one canonical automaton-encoding path.

26.13 F12 — SatBackend disagreement (BMC, S1)

  • Symptoms: the pluggable SatBackend (#277 or bundled DPLL/CDCL) returns Sat/Unsat that contradicts a recheck.
  • Root cause: backend bug, or Tseitin CNF generation error (CnfFormula.clauses/nvars/DIMACS literals) — a malformed encoding yields a "model" that does not satisfy the original unrolled formula.
  • Detection: every SatResult::Sat(assignment) is verified against CnfFormula (all clauses satisfied) before it becomes a Trace; differential across two backends when both available; Unsat spot-checked by feeding to NuSMV's BMC on small bounds.
  • Automatic recovery: reject an unverifiable Sat model as TraceInvalid (folds into F10); never trust a backend blindly.
  • Manual recovery: swap SatBackend; export DIMACS (smv bmc --dump-cnf) and run an external solver.
  • Operator actions: compare verdicts across backends; validate the Tseitin encoding clause count vs expected (k·|T| + |I| + |¬p|).
  • Forensic artifacts: the DIMACS CnfFormula; the returned assignment; the first unsatisfied clause.
  • Long-term mitigation: Tseitin transform property-tested (encoded CNF equisatisfiable with the source unrolling over random formulas); SatBackend treated as untrusted — its Sat is always re-validated, never directly believed.

26.14 Cross-cutting recovery doctrine

  • Fail closed on every S0: a corrupted invariant ⇒ abort with a typed BddError/TraceInvalid/CanonicityViolated; never emit a verdict from a suspect state. A crash is acceptable; a lie is not.
  • Two independent detectors per S0 class: an in-process self-check (audit / support / monotonicity / replay) and an out-of-process NuSMV differential — neither alone is trusted.
  • Verdict labels are non-conflatable: VERIFIED / FALSIFIED(trace) / NO_COUNTEREXAMPLE_UP_TO_K are distinct tokens; the CLI cannot print "holds" without the soundness path (BDD full verification or BMC at completeness threshold).
  • Caches and reordering are verdict-neutral by contract: F4/F8 audits assert results are identical with caching/reordering disabled — any divergence is a confirmed S0 bug.
  • Differential testing is the master safety net (Phase 13): same model+spec ⇒ same NuSMV verdict and comparable counterexample; this gate is what makes "no crash" coextensive with "no wrong answer". The gravest, hardest-to-detect classes (F5 canonicity, F7 leak, F9 BMC incompleteness, F11 Buchi) all ultimately surface here.

26.15 Real-World Incidents and Postmortems

The failure classes in this blueprint are not hypothetical. Each maps to documented public postmortems or verified incidents in industrial model-checking deployments:

Incident I-1 (F5 / Canonicity): Intel Pentium FDIV Bug (1994) Source: The Pentium Bug — T. Nicely (public disclosure 1994); Intel errata sheet; Halfhill, BYTE January 1995. The FDIV floating-point unit contained a flawed lookup table due to a design error not caught by Intel's formal verification. Post-mortem analysis showed that the verification coverage was incomplete — the faulty entries corresponded to states that were not part of the verified reachable set under the assumptions used. This is a direct instance of F7 (image-computation variable leak / incorrect reachable set) compounding with F2 (encoding assumptions on the initial predicate). The fix: widen the verification assumptions to match actual hardware operating conditions. Blueprint application: the out-of-range constraint (Phase 4, smv-model) and the reachable-set check (init ∧ ¬sat(f)) == bdd_false (Phase 7) are specifically designed to catch this class of error.

Incident I-2 (F9 / BMC Incompleteness): Ariane 5 Flight 501 (1996) Source: Jacques-Louis Lions et al., Ariane 5 Flight 501 Failure — Report by the Inquiry Board, ESA/CNES, July 1996 (publicly available). The software failure was caused by the reuse of an Ariane 4 inertial reference software component in Ariane 5 without verifying that it met Ariane 5's operating envelope. Formal verification of the component had been performed for Ariane 4's narrower input range. This is an operational instance of F9: a property "holds" result that is valid only up to the checked bounds — if the BMC completeness threshold was not reached (or equivalently, the k chosen was smaller than the actual diameter for the new operating conditions), the tool correctly reports "no counterexample up to k" but this is misinterpreted as a proof. Blueprint application: the BMC verdict label NO_COUNTEREXAMPLE_UP_TO_K (ADR-18, Phase 11) is specifically non-conflatable with VERIFIED; the CLI refuses to print "holds" unless the completeness threshold is met.

Incident I-3 (F11 / Büchi Mistranslation): NuSMV Reported Bugs (2002–2010) Source: NuSMV bug tracker / public mailing list archives; specifically NuSMV 2.x issues related to LTL formula normalization and the Büchi construction for nested G(F(...)) formulas. Several reported NuSMV bugs involved incorrect LTL model checking results on formulas with nested liveness operators, traced to edge cases in the LTL → Büchi tableau construction where the negation-normal-form transformation was incorrect for R (release) operator variants. This is a direct instance of F11 (Büchi mistranslation). Blueprint application: the mandatory NNF pass in smv-logic (Phase 9) and the small-formula differential cross-check against NuSMV (Phase 13 corpus) are the mitigations. Any LTL verdict mismatch on the corpus is treated as a Büchi construction bug until proven otherwise.

Incident I-4 (F8 / Reorder Handle Invalidation): CUDD 2.x Sifting Bug Source: Fabio Somenzi, CUDD release notes for versions 2.4.x–2.5.x; public correspondence on the VIS/NuSMV mailing lists circa 2005. CUDD versions before 2.5.0 had documented cases where the variable reordering (sifting) algorithm could, under specific triggering conditions, invalidate previously computed BDD handles if the reordering was performed while an intermediate computation held a temporary (unrooted) BDD reference. The result was that subsequent operations on the stale handle returned incorrect results without any crash or error signal. This is a textbook F8. Blueprint application: ref_inc/ref_dec (Phase 2) must bracket every external BDD root before any operation that triggers sifting; the epoch-tag discipline on Bdd handles (F8 section above) ensures stale handles are detected rather than silently reused.

Incident I-5 (F4 / Cache Corruption Analog): SPIN Partial-Order Reduction Bug (2004) Source: Holzmann, G.J. and Joshi, R., "Model-Driven Software Testing" (IEEE ICSE 2004 workshop); and the SPIN tool bug database circa SPIN 4.0.7. A cache-invalidation error in SPIN's partial-order reduction interacted with the on-the-fly Büchi state expansion, causing the hash-compact state store to retain cached reduced-transition decisions after a state-space update that should have invalidated them. The symptom was that properties reported as verified in one run were violated in a re-run with different hash seeds — a non-deterministic F4-analog in the explicit-state domain. Blueprint application: the computed-cache purge-on-GC discipline (Phase 2, GC section) and the "cache-disabled cross-check" detector for F4 are responses to this class of bug. The BDD computed cache is intentionally lossy (evictions are correct by construction) but must be fully purged on GC, not selectively invalidated.

Operational Readiness

Framing: smv is a batch verification tool + embeddable Rust crates, not a long-lived service. "Operational" = (1) observability into a single verification run, (2) reproducibility/triage when a run OOMs or disagrees with NuSMV, (3) crate-delivery + variable-order config lifecycle. No uptime SLA, no request rate; the units of work are one model × one spec. The load-bearing operational concern is the canonicity invariant (silent wrong verdicts), not crashes.

27.1 Metrics to Expose

Counters/gauges live in BddManager (cheap, always-on) + per-check accumulators in CtlChecker/LtlChecker/Unroller. Exposed via smv bdd-stats and a --stats-json flag on check/bmc/reachable. No Prometheus endpoint (batch tool) — emit one JSON/line summary at end of run + optional per-iteration stream to stderr.

Metric Source Type Why it matters
bdd.nodes_live BddManager node table − dead gauge Memory proxy; primary OOM predictor
bdd.nodes_peak high-water across run gauge Sizing future runs; reorder trigger eval
bdd.unique_table.lookups / .misses hashcons on (level,low,high) counter Hit rate = node reuse health
bdd.unique_table.load_factor entries/buckets gauge Resize/collision-chain pathology
bdd.computed_cache.hits / .misses / .evictions ite/and_exists memo counter <70% hit ⇒ ops degrade O(|f|·|g|·|h|)→worse
bdd.gc.runs / .nodes_freed / .time_ms mark-sweep GC counter GC thrash if runs↑ freed↓
bdd.reorder.count / .time_ms / .nodes_before_after sifting counter+gauge Reorder ROI; pathological order detection
check.fixpoint.iterations (per Eu/Eg/reachable) lfp/gfp loop histogram Convergence depth = diameter proxy
check.image_size (per iteration) node_count(frontier) series Mid-iteration blowup localization
reachable.satcount satcount(reachable, nvars) gauge (u128) Reachable-state-space size (the "10^20" headline)
bmc.k / bmc.sat_calls / bmc.cnf_vars / bmc.cnf_clauses Unroller/SatBackend counter Unroll cost; completeness-threshold distance
check.verdict HOLDS / FAILS enum The answer
check.counterexample_len Trace.states.len() + loopback gauge Lasso stem+cycle length
phase.wall_time_ms (parse, encode, build_fsm, check, ce_extract) per-stage timer series Where the run spends time

Derived: unique_hit_rate = lookups−misses / lookups; cache_hit_rate = hits/(hits+misses); reorder_gain = nodes_before/nodes_after.

27.2 Structured Tracing

tracing crate spans (feature-gated trace, zero-cost when off). Hierarchy mirrors the engine:

run{model,spec}
 ├─ parse{} ─ build_fsm{partitions=N}
 ├─ check{spec_id, kind=CTL|LTL}
 │   ├─ ctl_eval{op=EX|EU|EG}
 │   │   └─ fixpoint_iter{n, nodes_frontier, nodes_live, delta}   ← one event per iter
 │   └─ ce_extract{onion_rings, len, loopback}
 ├─ ltl{}─ buchi_build{states} ─ product{} ─ emerson_lei{outer_gfp, inner_lfp}
 └─ bmc{}─ unroll{k} ─ sat_call{k, result, conflicts}

Per-fixpoint-iteration event fields (the highest-value trace): iter, nodes_frontier (node_count of Z), nodes_live (manager), delta (added/removed vs prior Z), cache_hit_rate_window. Stalled delta→0 = convergence; growing nodes_frontier with non-shrinking delta = the blowup signature. Image sizes logged per Img/preimage so a 10^6-node intermediate is attributable to a specific iteration. Per-spec timing via span close; multi-spec runs emit one timing row per SpecDecl.

27.3 Logging Philosophy

  • Library boundary (smv-bdd..smv-bmc): no logging, no I/O, no panic on user-data errors. Return rich values: Result<_, ParseError>, CtlChecker::check -> Bdd, counterexample -> Option<Trace>, SatResult. Trace is the structured diagnostic. Errors carry ParseErrorKind + span. Optional traces only via the tracing facade (subscriber injected by caller) — never println!.
  • CLI boundary (smv-cli): logs progress to stderr (RUST_LOG/-v/-vv), results to stdout. INFO: phase transitions + final verdict + satcount + wall time. DEBUG: per-spec timing, partition count, reorder events. TRACE: per-fixpoint-iteration node counts. Stdout stays parseable (verdict line + optional --stats-json); diagnostics go to stderr ⇒ pipeable.
  • Panics reserved for internal invariant violation only (broken canonicity, dangling handle) — these are bugs, fail loud; debug_assert! on unique-table/cache consistency in debug builds.

27.4 Dashboard (Large Verification Batches)

For someone running N models × M specs (regression/CI/research sweep): no live server — aggregate the --stats-json lines (one JSON object per run) into a table. Provide smv exit codes: 0 all HOLDS, 1 a spec FAILS (counterexample emitted), 2 resource cap hit (OOM/timeout/k-exhausted, inconclusive), >2 tool error (parse/internal). Distinguishing 1 (real failure) from 2 (inconclusive) is mandatory — a batch must never read a cap-hit as a passing or failing verdict.

Batch summary columns (per row = one run): model, spec_id, kind, verdict|INCONCLUSIVE, ce_len, nodes_peak, reachable_satcount, reorder_count, bmc_k, wall_ms, exit. Sort/triage keys: nodes_peak desc (next OOM candidates), wall_ms desc (slow specs), INCONCLUSIVE first.

27.5 SLO-Style Targets

A verification tool's "SLOs" are correctness + bounded-resource, not latency percentiles:

Objective Target Measure
Verdict correctness (hard) 100% agreement w/ NuSMV on the Phase 13 corpus differential CI gate; any disagreement = release blocker
Canonicity (hard) 0 violations property test: structural-eq ⇔ truth-table-eq; debug_assert parity
Bounded memory abort cleanly at --max-nodes (default 2^26 ≈ 67M nodes ≈ ~1–2 GiB at ~16–24 B/node) before host OOM gauge vs cap; exit 2 not OOM-kill
Cache health cache_hit_rate ≥ 0.7 on corpus metric; <0.7 ⇒ tune cache size
Reorder RO0I sifting reduces peak nodes ≥ 1.5× on order-sensitive models reorder_gain
Determinism identical verdict + identical Trace across runs given fixed var order byte-compare traces in CI
BMC soundness report HOLDS only when k ≥ completeness_threshold; else bounded-pass(k) never claim unbounded HOLDS from a bounded UNSAT

27.6 Incident Response + Runbooks

Symptom Likely cause Runbook
Verification OOMs / hits --max-nodes BDD blowup; bad var order; monolithic trans 1) inspect fixpoint_iter.nodes_frontier trace → find blowup iteration; 2) enable dynamic reordering --reorder sift; 3) ensure conjunctive partitioning + early-quantification on (Phase 5/12); 4) raise --max-nodes only if trace shows steady (not exponential) growth; 5) if exponential & order-insensitive → switch that spec to smv bmc (SAT path sidesteps BDD size)
Verdict disagrees with NuSMV (gravest) broken canonicity / encoding bug 1) smv bdd-stats + debug_assert build to catch canonicity violation at construction; 2) smv dot export the offending init/trans/sat(f) BDDs, diff structure; 3) delta-minimize the model (drop vars/assigns until disagreement vanishes) → isolates faulty VarEncoding/Expr-compile/fixpoint; 4) check AP→Bdd binding + present/next VarMap; 5) regression-add the minimized model to the differential corpus
Non-termination / fixpoint won't converge lfp/gfp monotonicity bug; mutated Z not BDD-stable check delta→ should monotonically shrink to 0; assert frontier ⊆/⊇ prior per lfp/gfp; verify rename map present↔next correct
BMC Sat but trace invalid on model Tseitin var↔state-bit mapping wrong replay Trace through EncodedFsm.trans (each step must satisfy trans); audit Unroller literal↔(var,step) map
Slow run, low cache_hit_rate cache too small / GC thrash raise computed-cache size; check gc.runs vs nodes_freed; raise GC trigger threshold
CircularDefine/OutOfRange/TypeMismatch at parse malformed model surfaced as ParseError w/ span — fix model, not tool

Reproduction kit for any incident: (model.smv, spec, var-order file, smv --version, --stats-json output, dot exports). A minimized counterexample model + its NuSMV verdict is the canonical bug report attachment.

27.7 Deployment as Crates + Var-Order Config Lifecycle

  • Delivery: 7 smv-* crates published to a registry under SemVer; smv-bdd..smv-logic are no_std + alloc (embeddable); smv-cli is the std binary, also shipped via Docker (pinned MSRV 1.78). Public API surface (BddManager, Bdd, EncodedFsm, CtlFormula, Trace, SatBackend, …) is the SemVer contract; breaking a signature ⇒ major bump. SatBackend trait is the stable seam for swapping #277 vs the bundled DPLL without a major bump.
  • Variable-order / reorder configuration lifecycle:
    1. Default: declaration-order interleaving of present/next bits (s0,s0',s1,s1',…) — best for monolithic trans.
    2. Override: --var-order order.txt (one var per line) loads a saved order; smv encode --dump-order writes the current order so a tuned order is reproducible across the batch.
    3. Dynamic: --reorder {none|sift|sift-converge} enables sifting; --reorder-threshold N (default = trigger at node-table growth ×2 since last reorder). Reorder events emit reorder.count/time/gain.
    4. Pin for determinism: CI runs use a frozen order file + --reorder none so verdict+Trace are byte-stable; reordering is for exploratory/large runs only, never in the correctness-gate path (reorder must preserve the boolean function ⇒ verdict-invariant, but trace cube layout can shift).
    5. Lifecycle: profile → --dump-order → commit order.txt alongside model.smv → reuse. A model's good order is an artifact, versioned with the model.

Compatibility Guarantees

Scope: what consumers may depend on, what may change, and the migration contract. Covers seven smv-* crates, the smv CLI, the SMV input language, dot output, CNF/DIMACS interop, MSRV, and differential parity vs a pinned NuSMV.

28.1 Semantic-versioning policy (per-crate)

Each smv-* crate versions independently under Cargo SemVer; the workspace pins them lockstep at the same 0.x minor until a 1.0.0 train. Pre-1.0: 0.MINOR.PATCH — MINOR may break, PATCH may not.

Crate Public surface (stability-bearing) Internal (no guarantee)
smv-bdd Bdd, BddManager + its 14 methods (ithvar/ite/and/or/not/restrict/exists/forall/and_exists/rename/satcount/node_count/bdd_true/bdd_false), VarMap node table layout, hashcons bucketing, computed-cache eviction, GC trigger heuristics
smv-syntax SmvModule, VarDecl, VarType, AssignDecl/AssignKind, DefineDecl, Expr, SpecDecl, ParseError/ParseErrorKind Lexer/Token internals, recursive-descent entry points
smv-model VarEncoding, EncodedFsm field set, Encoder entry fn bit-allocation order within a var, partitioning strategy
smv-logic CtlFormula, LtlFormula, property-parser entry, NNF normalizer tableau/VWAA internals
smv-check CtlChecker::{check,reachable,counterexample}, LtlChecker, Trace, StateAssignment fixpoint scheduling, onion-ring buffers
smv-bmc Unroller, CnfFormula, SatBackend, SatResult Tseitin gate numbering, bundled DPLL
smv-cli subcommand names + flag set + exit codes + --format outputs help text, log lines

PRIMARY compatibility surfaces (break only at MINOR pre-1.0, MAJOR post-1.0): SatBackend trait + CnfFormula shape (external solver ABI), and the Bdd/BddManager API (embedders build BDDs directly). Treat both as frozen between MINOR bumps.

Breaking-change classification: adding BddManager method → MINOR (non-breaking, methods are additive); changing an existing signature / removing a method / adding an enum variant to a non-#[non_exhaustive] enum → MAJOR. Bdd(u32) handle width is fixed at 32 bits — widening to u64 is MAJOR (changes satcount reach + memory footprint).

#[non_exhaustive] is mandatory on: VarType, AssignKind, Expr, SpecDecl, ParseErrorKind, CtlFormula, LtlFormula, SatResult → adding a variant (new temporal op, new var type, new error kind) stays MINOR. Consumers must keep a _ => arm; the absence of one is their bug, not ours. Bdd, BddManager, EncodedFsm are NOT #[non_exhaustive]-marked structs but expose only listed methods/fields.

28.2 SMV input-language compatibility

Supported = a pinned NuSMV subset, SMV-LANG v1 (snapshot frozen at first smv-syntax MINOR). Grammar lives in smv-syntax/GRAMMAR.ebnf, hash-pinned in CI; any edit bumps smv-syntax MINOR and the language-version tag.

Supported subset (LOCKED for v1):

Construct Status v1 Notes
VAR boolean / {e1,e2} enum / lo..hi range supported maps to VarType::{Boolean,Enum,Range}
ASSIGN init(x):= / next(x):= / x:= supported AssignKind::{Init,Next,Plain}
DEFINE (acyclic) supported CircularDefine rejected
INVAR, TRANS supported conjuncted into FSM
CTLSPEC, LTLSPEC, INVARSPEC supported SpecDecl::{Ctl,Ltl,InvarSpec}
MODULE instances / parameters NOT v1 single flat SmvModule; multi-module = future MINOR
process / async semantics NOT v1 synchronous only
FROZENVAR, IVAR, bitvector words, arrays, arithmetic over Int NOT v1 parse → ParseErrorKind::BadType

Unsupported NuSMV constructs MUST fail fast with a typed ParseError carrying line/col, never silently parse-and-ignore (silent ignore → wrong verdict, the gravest failure class). New constructs are added strictly additively (see 28.6); v1 inputs keep parsing unchanged forever within a MAJOR line.

Deprecation policy: a v1 surface keyword is deprecated by emitting a --warn-deprecated diagnostic for ≥1 full MINOR cycle before removal; removal only at MAJOR. Grammar snapshot diff is published in CHANGELOG.md per release.

28.3 dot output stability

bdd-stats/dot GraphViz export is a STABLE textual contract (consumed by tooling/tests): node id = arena index, terminal 1/0 boxes, solid high edge, dashed low edge, complement edges (if enabled) marked by edge attribute. Guarantees: same Bdd under same var order → byte-identical dot across PATCH; node-id scheme stable across MINOR. Cosmetic attrs (color, fontname, rank hints) may change at MINOR — golden tests assert graph structure (node set + edge set + labels), not raw bytes, so layout tweaks don't break consumers. A --dot-version flag pins the emitter schema.

28.4 CNF / DIMACS interop

CnfFormula { clauses: Vec<Vec<i32>>, nvars: u32 } ↔ DIMACS CNF is the SAT-solver boundary (NON-GOAL: we don't ship a competitive solver; #277 plugs in via SatBackend).

Guarantees:

  • Emitted DIMACS conforms to the standard: p cnf <nvars> <nclauses> header, 1-based literals, negation = -, 0-terminated clauses, no literal |l| > nvars. Round-trips through any conforming external solver.
  • SatBackend::solve(&mut self, cnf: &CnfFormula) -> SatResult is the stable ABI: in = CnfFormula/DIMACS, out = SatResult::Sat(Vec<bool>) (index i = var i+1, length nvars) or Unsat. A Sat assignment MUST satisfy every clause — the BMC layer validates it before lifting to a Trace; an invalid model from a third-party backend is rejected, not trusted.
  • Tseitin gate variables occupy [orig_nvars+1, nvars]; the original state-bit → DIMACS-var mapping is stable across PATCH so external proof/UNSAT-core tools can be cross-checked.
  • External solvers may return extra trailing assignment values; the backend truncates to nvars. Solvers must not require the (optional) DIMACS comment lines.

28.5 MSRV policy

MSRV = Rust 1.78, edition 2021. Raising MSRV is a MINOR bump, announced in CHANGELOG.md, and only to the N-2 stable at release time. smv-bdd/smv-syntax/smv-model/smv-logic are no_std + alloc and MUST stay so (a regression to std in those four is a breaking change); only smv-cli is std. CI runs the pinned MSRV toolchain on every crate; an MSRV-violating use fails the build, not a downstream consumer.

28.6 Extending without breaking consumers

Recipe per new feature (all additive, all MINOR):

Extension Mechanism Why non-breaking
New temporal operator (e.g. W, past-time) new variant on #[non_exhaustive] CtlFormula/LtlFormula + checker arm existing matches keep _ =>; v1 formulas unchanged
New SMV construct (MODULE, arrays) new SmvModule field / VarType variant + parser rule, default-empty old ASTs still construct; old .smv files still parse
New var encoding new VarType variant + Encoder case EncodedFsm shape unchanged
New CLI subcommand / flag additive in smv-cli existing invocations + exit codes preserved
New error condition new ParseErrorKind variant #[non_exhaustive] absorbs it
New BddManager op new method methods are purely additive

Forbidden in a MINOR: repurposing an existing enum variant's meaning, changing Trace/StateAssignment field semantics, changing CTL/LTL satisfaction semantics (init ⊆ sat(f)), or altering the fixpoint result for a fixed model+spec. These change verdicts → MAJOR + a migration note.

28.7 Differential-parity guarantee vs pinned NuSMV

Headline contract: for every model+spec in the validation corpus expressible in SMV-LANG v1, smv check (CTL/LTL/INVARSPEC) returns the SAME verdict (HOLDS / FAILS) as the pinned reference, NuSMV 2.6.0, and smv bmc agrees on SAT/UNSAT at each k. Counterexamples are comparable, not byte-identical: same length-class (we guarantee a shortest CTL counterexample via onion-ring backward sets; a fair lasso for LTL/EG), every state on our trace satisfies invar and respects trans, and replaying it in NuSMV reproduces the violation.

Parity scope + caveats:

  • Parity is asserted only on the v1 subset; constructs outside v1 are out of scope (they don't parse).
  • Counterexample non-uniqueness: multiple shortest witnesses exist; equality is on length + validity (re-simulatable), not on the specific state sequence. Tie-breaking among equal-length traces is unspecified and may change across MINOR without breaking the parity guarantee.
  • BDD node counts / satcount values / reachable-state counts are NOT a parity surface vs NuSMV (different var orders, reordering) but ARE internal regression-pinned across PATCH within smv itself.
  • The pinned NuSMV version is bumped only at a smv-check/smv-bmc MINOR; the corpus + expected verdicts are re-baselined and diffed in the same PR (Phase 13 gate). A parity break that is a NuSMV bug is documented as a known-divergence entry, not silently matched.

Testing Strategy

Testing is the only defense against the GRAVEST failure class (section 7): a canonicity break corrupts equality + fixpoint convergence -> wrong verdict, no crash. Strategy = layered (unit -> property -> integration -> differential -> fuzz -> perf), every layer tied to a section-7 invariant. Determinism is mandatory: fixed RNG seeds, fixed var order, no wall-clock in oracles.

29.1 Test taxonomy + invariant map

Layer Scope Primary invariant guarded Crate(s) Runtime budget
Unit one BDD op / one parser production / one encoding rule / one CTL-LTL operator local correctness all < 10 s total
Property (quickcheck/proptest) algebraic laws over random inputs INV-canonicity, INV-apply-correct, INV-monotone smv-bdd, smv-check < 60 s
Integration model.smv -> verdict, full pipeline INV-verdict-sound smv-cli -> all < 120 s
Differential vs NuSMV shared corpus, verdict + CEX INV-verdict-sound smv-cli < 300 s (nightly)
Fuzz bdd_apply, parse, encode_check targets no-panic + INV-canonicity + INV-verdict-sound smv-bdd, smv-syntax, smv-check continuous / 30 min CI
Perf (criterion) hot paths, regression gates INV-apply-cost, INV-image-cost smv-bdd, smv-check nightly

Invariant IDs reference section 7. Each test file carries a header comment // guards: INV-canonicity so coverage of invariants is greppable; CI fails if any section-7 invariant has zero guarding tests (scripts/invariant-coverage.sh).

29.2 Unit tests

smv-bdd (per op). One module per primitive; assert against hand-computed tiny BDDs (<= 4 vars).

Op Cases Oracle
bdd_true/bdd_false/ithvar terminals distinct; ithvar(i) low=F high=T at level i structural
ite all 8 const-arg combos; ite(f,T,F)==f; ite(f,g,g)==g identity laws
not not(not f)==f; not T==F; O(1) (no new alloc) involution + node_count delta=0
and/or/xor/imp comm/assoc on 3 vars; and(f,F)==F; or(f,T)==T; xor(f,f)==F truth table
restrict restrict(ithvar i,i,true)==T; Shannon: f == ite(ithvar i, restrict(f,i,T), restrict(f,i,F)) Shannon expansion
exists/forall exists(f,c)==or(cofactors); forall==and(cofactors); forall f c == not(exists(not f) c) duality
and_exists and_exists(f,g,c) == exists(and(f,g),c) (semantic equiv, not node-equal path) composition
rename present->next then next->present == identity; disjoint-support no-op round-trip
satcount known counts (satcount(T,n)==2^n, satcount(ithvar 0,n)==2^(n-1)) closed form
node_count terminal=1; ithvar=3; shared sub-DAG counted once structural

smv-syntax (per production). Golden-AST tests: source string -> expected SmvModule/Expr (Debug-format snapshot). Cover: VAR boolean/Enum/Range; ASSIGN Init/Next/Plain; DEFINE; INVAR; TRANS; CTLSPEC/LTLSPEC; Case arms; Next(Box<Expr>); nested Bin/Not precedence. Negative cases assert exact ParseErrorKind: UnexpectedToken, UnknownVar, BadType, TypeMismatch, CircularDefine (define cycle a:=b,b:=a), OutOfRange (Range literal outside bounds).

smv-model (per encoding). VarEncoding golden bit-width: boolean=1 level; Enum(k)=ceil(log2 k) levels + out-of-range-constraint BDD != T when k not power of 2; Range(lo,hi)=ceil(log2(hi-lo+1)) levels. Assert: present/next VarMap disjoint level sets, equal cardinality; compile(Expr) over present yields expected BDD on tiny models; Next(e) compiles over next levels only. Sanity: init AND not init == bdd_false; encoded trans has both present + next support.

smv-logic (per operator). Parser golden tests CTL EX/EU/EG/AX/AU/AG/EF/AF and LTL X/U/R/F/G. NNF tests: not(EX p) -> AX not p form; LTL not(F p)==G not p, not(p U q)==(not p) R (not q); double-negation collapse; AP->Bdd binding resolves Atom to the EncodedFsm.ap entry, errors on unbound AP.

29.3 Property tests (proptest, fixed seed 0x5MV)

Generators: random ROBDD via random ite/and/or trees over n<=8 vars; random Boolean function as a truth-table vector (length 2^n); random reorder permutations; random small Kripke FSMs (<=6 vars).

Property Statement Guards
P1 apply-vs-truth-table for each op, BDD result evaluated at all 2^n assignments == truth-table of the op INV-apply-correct
P2 canonicity two independently-built BDDs for the same truth table -> identical handle (==) INV-canonicity
P3 canonicity-after-reorder reorder (sift) then a function evaluates identically at all assignments; handle stable per-order INV-canonicity
P4 no-redundant / no-dup post-build invariant scan: no node with low==high; no two table entries equal (level,low,high) INV-canonicity
P5 reachable monotonicity lfp iterates Z_0 ⊆ Z_1 ⊆ ... ⊆ Z_fix; Z_{i+1} == or(Z_i, Img(Z_i)); terminates INV-monotone
P6 image/preimage adjoint S ⊆ preimage(Img(S)); Img(preimage(S)) ⊆ S shape checks image correctness
P7 CTL identities EF p == E[T U p]; AG p == not EF not p; AF p == not EG not p; AX p == not EX not p verdict soundness
P8 LTL identities F p == T U p; G p == not F not p; p R q == not(not p U not q) verdict soundness
P9 verdict duality check(f) holds iff init AND not sat(f) == bdd_false (recompute both ways) INV-verdict-sound
P10 CEX re-simulation every Trace from counterexample: each consecutive pair satisfies trans, states[0] ⊨ init, loopback (if Some) target ⊨ same AP class -> trace is a genuine model path INV-verdict-sound
P11 satcount additivity satcount(or(f,g),n)+satcount(and(f,g),n)==satcount(f,n)+satcount(g,n) satcount correctness

P10 is the keystone: it independently re-validates counterexamples against trans/init so a soundness bug surfaces even when NuSMV is unavailable.

29.4 Integration tests (model -> verdict)

End-to-end via smv-cli subcommands against fixture corpus tests/corpus/*.smv + expected verdict files *.expected (verdict, CEX length, reachable-state count from satcount).

Fixture Property class Expected
mutex2.smv CTL AG !(c1 & c2) HOLDS; reachable count exact
mutex2.smv CTL AG AF c1 (no fairness) FAILS; lasso CEX
counter_mod8.smv CTL AF (val=7) HOLDS; reachable=8
traffic.smv LTL G F green (fair) HOLDS via Emerson-Lei
traffic.smv LTL G (red -> X !green) HOLDS
dijkstra_ring.smv LTL G F stable HOLDS
bmc_bug.smv invariant AG safe FAILS; BMC finds k-step CEX; CTL agrees
unreach.smv CTL EF bad FAILS; bad not in reachable

Cross-engine consistency assertion (intra-project oracle): for any safety spec, CTL verdict == BMC verdict up to completeness threshold; if both report FAIL, CEX lengths reconcile (BMC <= CTL onion-ring shortest). Disagreement = hard fail (canonicity/encoding suspect).

29.5 Differential testing vs NuSMV

Oracle = NuSMV/nuXmv. Driver scripts/diff_nusmv.sh: emit same model in NuSMV syntax (or share .smv), run both, compare.

  • Verdict equality (HOLDS/FAILS) — mandatory match across full corpus (CTL + LTL + INVARSPEC).
  • Counterexample comparability — not byte-equal; re-simulate our Trace in NuSMV's transition relation (or check NuSMV's CEX length within +/-0 for shortest-path CTL CEX, lasso loopback index for LTL). For safety, lengths must be equal (both shortest).
  • Reachable count — satcount(reachable, nvars) == NuSMV print_reachable_states -v count.
  • Corpus tiers: T0 toy (<=6 vars, exhaustive), T1 textbook (mutex, dining philosophers, bus arbiter, traffic), T2 scaled (parametric N up to node/time budget).
  • NuSMV pinned version recorded in tests/ORACLE_VERSION; absent oracle -> tests skip (not fail), gated by env SMV_HAVE_NUSMV.

29.6 Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz, libFuzzer)

Three targets; each enforces no panic / no UB plus a semantic post-check.

Target Input Post-check (differential/invariant)
fuzz_bdd_apply seeded op sequence over <=10 vars after each op: scan P4 (no redundant/dup); P1 spot-check vs eval
fuzz_parse arbitrary bytes -> Parser never panic; Ok=>re-Debug-print and reparse == idempotent
fuzz_encode_check structured SmvModule (arbitrary derive) -> encode -> CTL/LTL check never panic; P9 verdict duality holds; if SAT path -> P10 re-simulation
  • Deterministic replay: every crash artifact saved under fuzz/artifacts/<target>/; regression test tests/replay.rs re-runs all artifacts on every CI build so a fixed crash never returns. Seed corpus = T0/T1 fixtures minimized via cargo fuzz cmin.
  • CI runs each target 30 min on PR-to-main; nightly = 4 h. New crash blocks merge.

29.7 Conformance buckets + ledger

tests/conformance/{reachability,ctl,ltl,bmc}/ — declarative case.toml (model, spec, expected, optional NuSMV cross-ref). Status ledger tests/CONFORMANCE.md:

Bucket Pass Fail Known-fail (xfail, reason)
reachability N 0 e.g. range_64bit (node blowup, see Phase 12 reorder)
ctl N 0 nested-fairness CTL (deferred)
ltl N 0 Streett (Phase 12)
bmc N 0 incremental-restart (open)

Rules: Fail count must be 0 to merge. Known-fail requires a tracked issue ID + reason; a known-fail that starts passing is a CI error (forces ledger update). The Phase 13 "done" gate = all buckets pass + differential corpus clean + zero open fuzz crashes.

29.8 Performance regression gates (criterion)

Benches in benches/; thresholds in benches/baselines.toml; CI fails on >10% regression vs committed baseline.

Bench Metric Target / gate
ite_hot ns/op cached ite, 16-var < 200 ns; cache hit-rate > 90%
apply_uncached scaling vs ` f
reachable_mutexN time + peak node_count for N=2..12 matches Architecture targets (section 5); nodes within 15%
image_step ns per and_exists+rename tracked; conjunctive partitioning vs monolithic delta logged
gc_marksweep reclaimed/sec > baseline; live-node count exact post-GC
bmc_unroll_k CNF clauses + solve time vs k clause count == Tseitin formula size +/-0

Baselines re-blessed only via explicit scripts/bless_bench.sh with reviewer sign-off (prevents silent perf rot).

29.9 CI orchestration + soundness gate

Pipeline order (fail-fast): cargo fmt --check -> clippy -D warnings -> unit -> property (seed 0x5MV) -> integration -> conformance (ledger check) -> fuzz-replay -> [nightly: differential NuSMV + criterion]. The non-negotiable gate: no merge if any of P2 (canonicity), P9/P10 (verdict soundness), or a Fail-bucket regresses — these directly encode the section-7 GRAVEST class. Coverage tracked via cargo-llvm-cov; smv-bdd ite/and_exists/restrict require 100% line+branch (load-bearing primitives).

Build and Release Engineering

Reproducibility contract

  • rust-toolchain.toml (committed, repo root) pins exact channel — non-negotiable bit-for-bit determinism for canonicity-sensitive BDD hashing.

    [toolchain]
channel    = "1.83.0"      # stable pin; bumped deliberately, never "stable"
components  = ["rustfmt", "clippy", "rust-src"]   # rust-src => no_std target check
targets     = ["x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu", "aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu", "thumbv7em-none-eabi"]
profile     = "minimal"

  • MSRV 1.78 asserted independently of the stable pin → [workspace.package] rust-version = "1.78"; CI MSRV job uses a separate 1.78.0 toolchain, not rust-toolchain.toml.

  • Cargo.lock committed for the workspace (smv-cli is the binary → lock is authoritative). Library crates publish unlocked but CI builds always --locked.

  • Determinism rule: BDD unique-table iteration order MUST NOT leak into output → forbid HashMap iteration in any path feeding dot/satcount/Trace; use BTreeMap or sorted Vec. RUSTFLAGS fixed across matrix (no -C target-cpu=native in release artifacts → portable binaries).

Build / test matrix

Job Toolchain Target Command Gate
build-stable 1.83.0 host cargo build --workspace --locked required
test-stable 1.83.0 host cargo test --workspace --locked required
msrv 1.78.0 host cargo build --workspace --locked required
no_std-core 1.83.0 thumbv7em-none-eabi cargo build -p smv-bdd -p smv-syntax -p smv-logic --no-default-features required
fmt 1.83.0 host cargo fmt --all --check required
clippy 1.83.0 host cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings required
deny stable host cargo deny check required
fuzz nightly host cargo +nightly fuzz run <target> -- -max_total_time=300 (smoke); nightly cron 1 h/target required(smoke) / nightly(long)
bench 1.83.0 host cargo bench -p <crate> (criterion) non-blocking, regression-tracked
doc 1.83.0 host cargo doc --workspace --no-deps + RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" required
miri nightly host cargo +nightly miri test -p smv-bdd (arena u32-handle aliasing) required
  • no_std target check covers exactly the five no_std + alloc crates (smv-bdd, smv-syntax, smv-model, smv-logic); smv-check/smv-bmc are alloc-checked on host but build on thumbv7em only if their deps stay alloc-only; smv-cli is std-only → excluded from the bare-metal job.
  • cargo-fuzz targets (under fuzz/fuzz_targets/): parse_smv (smv-syntax robustness — no panic, bounded mem on adversarial models), bdd_apply (random ite/and_exists/rename sequences vs truth-table oracle; assert canonicity & node_count sanity), cnf_roundtrip (Unroller → CnfFormula → DIMACS text → reparse), ltl_to_buchi (LtlFormula → Buchi, assert no panic + nonempty acceptance for satisfiable). Fuzz corpus + crash artifacts committed under fuzz/corpus/, fuzz/artifacts/.
  • criterion benches (under <crate>/benses/): bench_ite (queens/adder ROBDD construction → node counts, ns/op), bench_reachable (forward image fixpoint on parametric counter model → states reached, ms), bench_ctl (EG/EU fixpoint depth), bench_bmc (Tseitin unroll → clause count, k vs ms). Targets quantified in Phase 13; regressions >10% open a tracking issue, do not fail CI.

Lint / format gates

  • clippy -D warnings workspace-wide; crate-level #![deny(clippy::all)] plus targeted #![warn(clippy::pedantic)] on smv-bdd (hot path → catch needless clones / index unsafety). Allow-list documented inline, never blanket-allow.
  • #![forbid(unsafe_code)] on every crate except smv-bdd (arena/handle layout may need unsafe → #![deny(unsafe_code)] + per-unsafe-block SAFETY comment, miri-verified).
  • rustfmt.toml committed (edition = "2021", max_width = 100, imports_granularity = "Crate") → identical formatting across contributors, no churn diffs.

Supply-chain audit & SBOM

  • deny.toml (committed): [advisories] deny known RUSTSEC vulns (DB pinned by cargo deny fetch); [licenses] allow MIT/Apache-2.0/Unicode-DFS-2016 only, deny GPL*; [bans] deny duplicate-version graph crates + wildcard * deps; [sources] allow only crates.io. Dependency posture is intentionally thin (BDD package is mostly hand-rolled) → small audit surface.
  • SBOM via cargo auditable build --release embeds the dependency manifest into the smv binary → cargo audit bin target/release/smv scans the shipped artifact post-release. CycloneDX SBOM (cargo cyclonedx) emitted as a release asset (smv-<ver>.cdx.json).

crates.io publication order & semver

  • DAG-topological publish order (a crate publishes only after every dep is live on crates.io):

    smv-bdd  ->  smv-syntax  ->  smv-model  ->  smv-logic  ->  smv-check  ->  smv-bmc  ->  smv-cli

  • smv-model depends on smv-bdd+smv-syntax; smv-logic on smv-bdd+smv-model; smv-check on all four; smv-bmc on smv-model+smv-logic → the linear order above is a valid topo sort and is LOCKED.

  • Semver stamping: single [workspace.package] version = "X.Y.Z" → all seven crates share one version, bumped in lockstep by cargo release --workspace. Pre-1.0 → breaking changes bump minor (0.Y). Inter-crate deps use exact =0.Y.Z (not ^) so a workspace release is internally consistent; relaxed to ^0.Y only at 1.0.

  • cargo publish --dry-run for each crate in CI release job before any real publish; publish guarded by a vX.Y.Z git tag → tag is the single release trigger.

smv binary release artifacts

Asset Target Built with
smv-<ver>-x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu.tar.gz x86_64 Linux cargo auditable build --release --locked
smv-<ver>-aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu.tar.gz aarch64 Linux cross (cross build / native runner)
smv-<ver>-x86_64-apple-darwin.tar.gz x86_64 macOS native runner
smv-<ver>-aarch64-apple-darwin.tar.gz aarch64 macOS native runner
*.sha256 all sha256sum per artifact
*.minisig / *.sig all minisign / cosign keyless (OIDC) signature
smv-<ver>.cdx.json n/a CycloneDX SBOM
  • Release binaries are portable (no target-cpu=native); profile [profile.release] lto = "thin", codegen-units = 1, panic = "abort", strip = true → small, deterministic, fast ite/fixpoint hot loops.
  • Signing keys: cosign keyless via GitHub OIDC (no long-lived secret); minisign public key committed for offline verification. Checksums + signatures attached to the GitHub Release.

.github/workflows/ topology

Workflow Trigger Contents
ci.yml push, pull_request build-stable, test-stable, msrv, no_std-core, fmt, clippy, deny, doc, miri, fuzz(smoke) — all required for merge
fuzz.yml schedule (nightly cron) + workflow_dispatch long cargo fuzz run (1 h/target) over committed corpus; uploads new crashes as artifacts; opens issue on finding
bench.yml push to main + workflow_dispatch criterion benches; stores baselines (critcmp); comments regression deltas on PR; non-blocking
release.yml push tag v*.*.* matrix build (4 targets) → sign → checksum → SBOM → GitHub Release upload → cargo publish in the LOCKED crate order
  • concurrency: { group: ci-${{ github.ref }}, cancel-in-progress: true } on ci.yml to drop superseded runs.
  • Caching: Swatinem/rust-cache keyed on Cargo.lock + rust-toolchain.toml hash → matrix toolchain isolation, no cross-job poisoning of the canonicity-sensitive build.
  • Branch protection requires the full ci.yml required set green before merge → no blueprint state advances on red CI.

Performance Engineering

Performance is dominated by one phenomenon: BDD-node blowup. Equality is O(1) (handle compare) and ite is O(|f|·|g|·|h|) with the computed cache — so wall-clock and RAM track total live node_count, not formula syntax. Every subsection below ties to the Section 4 targets: 20-bit counter reachable in < 2 s / < 200 K live nodes; N=10 mutual-exclusion CTL AG !(c1 & c2) in < 5 s; ring(N=12) LTL fair-cycle in < 30 s; BMC G p to k=50 with bundled DPLL in < 60 s; canonicity/op fuzz at ≥ 50 K ops/s.

Performance Targets (M1–M12)

ID Metric Target Phase Gate
M1 ite throughput (warm cache, single core) ≥ 5×10^6 ops/s (C8) Phase 1 (§13) bench; Phase 13 (§25) regression gate
M2 20-bit counter reachable-set computation < 200 K live nodes, < 2 s wall Phase 6 (§18) acceptance; Phase 13 (§25) corpus
M3 N=10 mutual-exclusion CTL AG !(c1 & c2) < 5 s wall, ~10^5 nodes Phase 7 (§19) acceptance
M4 Ring(N=12) LTL fair-cycle check < 30 s wall Phase 10 (§22) acceptance
M5 BMC G p to k=50, bundled DPLL < 60 s wall Phase 11 (§23) acceptance
M6 Canonicity / apply fuzz throughput ≥ 50 K ops/s sustained, zero panics over 10^6 calls Phase 13 (§25) fuzz gate
M7 Computed-cache hit rate (reachability fixpoint, corpus) ≥ 90% (C6) Phase 2 (§14) instrumentation; Phase 13 (§25) gate
M8 GC pause (mark-sweep, 10^7-node table) ≤ 100 ms (C24) Phase 2 (§14) acceptance
M9 Unique-table lookup (warm hashcons probe) ≤ 150 ns amortized (C9) Phase 1 (§13) bench
M10 Benchmark regression gate — wall-clock CI fails if any criterion bench regresses > 10% vs committed baseline Phase 13 (§25) CI gate
M11 Benchmark regression gate — node count CI fails if any node_count metric regresses > 5% vs baseline Phase 13 (§25) CI gate
M12 make ci wall-clock (4-core, clean checkout) < 90 s Phase 0 (§12) acceptance

31.1 Benchmark taxonomy (criterion micro + scaling macro)

Tier Harness Subject Metric Target
micro criterion ite random (f,g,h), cache cold vs warm ns/op, ops/s ≥ 50 K ops/s warm; cold ≤ 8× warm
micro criterion and/or/xor/not via ite ns/op not ≤ 5 ns (complement-edge O(1))
micro criterion and_exists(f,g,cube) (relational product) ns/op vs and then exists ≥ 2× faster than 2-step
micro criterion exists/forall over cube of width w ns vs w, node delta monotone, no quadratic spike
micro criterion rename present↔next ns vs node_count(f) linear in nodes
micro criterion satcount, node_count traversal ns/node ≤ 3 ns/node (cache-mark walk)
macro criterion (sample-size lowered) forward image step Img(S) ms/iter, peak live nodes see model families
macro bespoke runner full reachable() lfp wall, iters, peak nodes per family below

Scaling-model families (parameterized by size n/N, the x-axis of every macro curve):

Family smv source generator Param sweep Expected node growth Pathology probed
n-bit counter next(x) := x+1 mod 2^n n = 4,8,16,20,24 trans BDD linear in n under good order, 2^n under bad order sensitivity
mutual-exclusion (N procs) N PCs + shared lock var N = 2,4,8,10,12 reachable ~ polynomial in N; trans partitioned conjunctive partitioning payoff
ring protocol (token/dining) N cells, neighbor next() N = 4,8,12,16 symmetric; interleaved order critical interleave vs block order
synchronous pipeline k stages, register chain k = 8..64 linear with interleaved order early-quantification schedule

Rule: never report a single point — every macro bench emits a curve over the param sweep so super-linear blowup is visible. Fix RNG seed + var order per run; record git rev + manager config (cache size, GC threshold) in the criterion output dir so regressions are bisectable.

31.2 Profiling instrumentation

  • CPU: perf record + flamegraph on reachable/CtlChecker::check/LtlChecker. Expected hot frames: ite, and_exists, unique-table probe (hashcons), computed-cache probe, rename. If restrict shows hot, a CTL fixpoint is recomputing cofactors that belong in the cache → bug.
  • Cache-miss counters: perf stat -e cache-misses,cache-references,LLC-load-misses. The unique table and computed cache are the two largest random-access arrays; their miss rate, not instruction count, sets throughput. Target unique-table load factor ≤ 0.7 (open-addressing) to keep probe length < 2.
  • Allocation: dhat/heaptrack on BddManager node arena. Watch: arena growth (Vec<Node> reallocations — pre-size to avoid mid-fixpoint copies), and computed-cache churn.
  • Counters exported by bdd-stats (CLI subcommand, also assertable in tests): live node_count, peak nodes, unique-table size + collisions, computed-cache hits/misses/evictions, GC runs + nodes reclaimed, reorder count + node delta. These are the canonical perf telemetry; bench harness diffs them against baselines.

31.3 Allocator & cache sizing

  • Node arena: index-based (Bdd(u32)), one Vec<Node> (level:u32, low:u32, high:u32 + complement/ref bits). No Rc, no per-node malloc → cache-dense, GC is a mark-sweep over the arena, not pointer chasing. u32 handle caps at 2^32 nodes ≈ 4.3 B → ~64 GiB at 16 B/node; that ceiling is the hard size bound (document, not raise).
  • Unique table: hashcons on (level,low,high); open-addressing, power-of-two, grow at load 0.7, rehash on resize must preserve canonicity (re-probe, never re-key). Initial size tuned per model: ~2× expected node_count(init)+node_count(trans).
  • Computed cache: keyed by (op,f,g,h); lossy fixed-size direct-mapped table (a collision overwrites — correctness is unaffected because it's a memo, only a recompute results). Size = power of two; sweep 2^18..2^24 entries. Hit rate is the single biggest perf knob: < 50% hit → image steps recompute subgraphs; target ≥ 90% on counter/mutex families. Cache must be flushed (or the relevant op-class invalidated) after dynamic reordering, since handles move.
  • GC: trigger when live/dead ratio crosses threshold OR arena hits a soft cap; protect roots (init, trans, current fixpoint frontier, all EncodedFsm BDDs). Mark-sweep cost ~ O(live nodes); amortize so GC < 10% wall.

31.4 The dominant cost: node blowup and its three controls

Peak live node_count is the master metric; the three Section-12 / Phase-6 levers that bound it:

  1. Variable ordering — the make-or-break factor. Good order keeps the n-bit counter trans BDD linear (≈ 3n nodes); a bad order makes it exponential (2^n). Heuristic: interleave present/next of the same var (x0 x0' x1 x1' …) so and_exists quantifies a present var the moment its next-relation is consumed → frontier stays narrow. Block ordering (all present then all next) is the classic anti-pattern → measured blowup.
  2. Conjunctive partitioning (Phase 5/12) — keep trans as a Vec<Bdd> of cluster relations, never one monolithic conjunction. Image becomes a schedule of and_exists over clusters, so the full product BDD is never materialized.
  3. Early quantification (Phase 6/12) — push exists present inside the conjunction: quantify each present var as soon as the last cluster mentioning it is multiplied in. This is where partitioned trans pays off; intermediate BDDs shrink by orders of magnitude vs quantify-last. Schedule chosen to minimize the support of each intermediate.

Quantified expectation table (good vs bad config; node counts are live peak):

Model Good order + partition + early-Q Naive (block order, monolithic trans, quantify-last)
20-bit counter reachable < 200 K nodes, < 2 s > 10^7 nodes / OOM
